# Changelog

## v0.4.2.1 — 2026-10-14

- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: the built-in calibration now applies on any Halo kernel and NPU firmware.** `npu_trunk=auto` armed only when the compiled-in Halo calibration matched the host exactly, including the kernel release, NPU firmware and NPU PCI slot of the machine it was measured on, so every stock-kernel Halo logged `npu-trunk: off (calibration key mismatch: kernel.uname_release)` and served GPU-only. For the built-in calibration those three fields are now advisory: when any differs, it is used with its margin raised to 0.05 (the pipe must be predicted at least 5% faster than GPU-only, since the timings were measured on a different kernel/firmware), and the load logs `npu-trunk: built-in calibration measured on <kernel>/<fw>, this host <kernel>/<fw>; using it with margin 0.05`. Arch, build, model-index hash, epilogue and trunk route stay strict, and a user calibration file stays fully strict. Output is exact on either route; the calibration only chooses between them. First-load self-calibration is planned.
- **The NPU trunk calibration identity is pinned to `hipfire-0.4.2:npu-trunk-bf16-rowmajor-v1`.** It no longer derives from the crate version: it names the NPU serving numerics/transport, which a release bump does not change. Deriving it from the crate version would have orphaned the shipped Halo calibrations and left `npu_trunk=auto` GPU-only on 0.4.2.1.
- **Flash-Next: tool-call turns in the history replay the tokens the model emitted.** A follow-up turn whose history held an assistant tool call rendered that turn through the chat template. That stays a strict extension of the committed conversation only while the model's output is exactly the template's own canonical tokenization; a different BPE split of the emitted text, or spacing the template rewrites around `<tool_call>`, made the follow-up miss its live continuation and re-prefill the whole conversation. Qwen4 now replays a cached tool-call turn as the whole body the model emitted (reasoning, prose and every `<tool_call>` block), as it already did for plain turns, so the follow-up no longer depends on that. On Strix Halo with canonical model output both builds already continued in place: a 7-request tool chain and a 32K-context tool append reused every committed token before and after, with identical output. The cache key covers each call's name and arguments, and prose the client echoes with the calls must equal the emitted prose (empty content is accepted); an edited turn misses and falls back to the template render. Session-exact like every live continuation. Other model families keep the template render (`ToolTurnSplice::Envelopes`). `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES=0` turns reuse off.
- **Flash-Next session reuse is traceable per request.** With `HIPFIRE_QWEN_CACHE_TRACE=1`, every Qwen4 request (native MTP and AR) prints `[qwen4-session] route=… prompt=… prior=… lcp=… reused=… new=… source=live|snapshot|cold`: the previous turn's committed history, its common prefix with the new canonical prompt, and what the session cache reused. When the prompt diverges from that history, `[qwen4-session ids]` shows the token ids around the first difference. Host-side only; off by default.
- Changelog correction: the Flash-Next session cache's resident snapshot for deep appends past the pool budget (#847, HIPF-46, @fivetide; +512 tokens on a 256K prefix 4.7 → 197.5 tok/s on Strix Halo) shipped in v0.4.2 but was missing from its notes.

## Unreleased (0.4.3)

- **Flash-Next (qwen4) singleton native MTP on the R9700 expert hot set: the AR floor no longer retires requests where MTP still wins (humaneval_0 49 → 53 tok/s, records_parser_1k 38.7 → 49 tok/s in the processes that retired, bare_factual 37 → 42, fiction_lighthouse 40 → 45; aggregate 47.1–49.2 → 50.5–50.8).** Route-only: greedy ids are identical at every route and a sampled request stays distribution-exact. With the hot set on, the single calibration token that priced AR was 7–26% off the request's mean AR token (humaneval_0 19.6 ms against 23.7 ms mean and 24–28 ms for the AR tokens that followed; bare_factual 23.2 against 29.5; fiction_lighthouse 22.5 against 25.3; records_parser_1k 31.4 ms in one hold and 21.1–21.5 ms in the next, so it retired in 2 of 3 fresh processes), because one token's cost is a property of its routed experts (VRAM hot set against host-mapped). A losing verdict past the retirement grace is now confirmed before it is sticky: the floor first spends 6 real AR tokens (the calibration step again, head kept synced; retirement would run them anyway), re-prices AR by their mean (26.0–26.2 ms humaneval_0 and 27.8 ms records_parser_1k in every fresh process, 27.6–29.2 ms bare_factual), at most once per 16 windows, and also needs the ~25-window realized cost of all its windows (the 8-window memories dip on any streak of rejected drafts) to lose to that price. Continuous-batching lanes are unchanged. `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_AR_PROBE=0` is the kill switch (single-token floor); `HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_TRACE=1` prints one `QWEN4_FLOOR_TRACE` JSON line per window / AR token (developer vars in `docs/env-vars.md`).
  - Measured on one R9700 (gfx1201), Flash-Next mq4-xts, hot set on (6479 experts in VRAM, 18097 host-mapped, `ple rows: q4`), l2 prompt set, MTP greedy, 3 interleaved fresh processes per arm after one pre-warmed kcache, decode tok/s: records_parser_1k beta 49.9 / 38.9 / 38.7 (retired in 2 of 3, tau 0.12) against fix 49.3 / 48.4 / 49.6 (0 of 3 retired); humaneval_0 48.9–49.2 (retired after 9 native windows, tau 0.59, all 3) against 52.9–53.1 (tau 2.05); fiction_lighthouse 39.5–40.0 against 45.2–45.4; bare_factual 37.4–37.7 against 41.6–42.2; every other prompt within spread (glimmer_coding 64.5–66.6 / 64.8–64.9, lru_cache 63.6–64.6 / 64.0–64.4, code_edit 47.4–47.5 / 47.4–47.5, agentic_user_read 44.0–44.2 / 42.4–45.4, glimmer_prefill_1024 56.7–57.4 / 56.8–57.5, records_parser_4k 49.4–50.7 / 50.0–50.2). Aggregate beta 49.16 / 47.14 / 47.22, fix 50.72 / 50.47 / 50.77. Greedy id md5 identical across all 6 processes, the no-hot-set cells and the kill-switch cell. No hot set (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=0`, one process per arm): aggregate 39.57 → 41.29, records_parser_1k 32.9 (retired) → 41.5, bare_factual 36.4 → 41.1, every other prompt within 3%, except agentic_user_read 35.6 → 33.3 (a 45-token request where MTP and AR both cost ~29–30 ms per token: the fix retires after its probes, beta did not). Kill-switch cell (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_AR_PROBE=0`) retires exactly like beta (humaneval_0 83 AR windows, fiction_lighthouse 219, bare_factual 92). Receipts: `qcal/release-0.4.3/fn-floor-hotset-r9700/runs/dec-b-*` and `GATE.txt`, traces `dec-a-*`.
- **`hipfire pull <model>` asks instead of requiring flags.** On a terminal it walks through only what the registry leaves open: the tier (other tags `<model>-<suffix>` with the same `arch_id`, e.g. `qwen3.8:flash-next` / `-mq3-xts` / `-mq2-xts`, each with size, a one-line note from `desc`, and whether it fits the detected VRAM or VRAM+RAM), the PLE rows of a split entry (q4 preselected on gfx1151 or when RAM cannot hold base + q8, else what `--ple auto` fetches), the registry's draft/MTP sidecars, then a summary with the total download and a free-disk check before it downloads anything. The choices only feed the existing pull plan (`plan_pull`, the registry entry). Flags skip their question (`--ple`, the tier's own tag, new `--draft` / `--no-draft`); a single-variant model with nothing to ask never prompts. `-y` / `--yes`, a non-terminal stdin, and the automatic pulls of `run` / `bench` / `serve` take the previous defaults with no prompts, so scripts and CI are unchanged.
- **Dense Qwen TP2 on two R9700s (gfx1201): each rank now decodes a whole token as one HIP graph; 19.99 → 18.55 ms/token at ctx 128 and 20.59 → 18.97 ms/token at 8K (exact).** The gfx1100 whole-token route (`HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH`, #830) is admitted on an exact gfx1201 pair and is the default there: each of the 128 per-token reductions is one graph-resident kernel (bounded device signal barrier plus the rank-ordered `x + (p0 + p1)` add), replacing 257 graph-segment launches, 258 kernel launches and 512 event record/wait pairs per token. Arithmetic and kernels are unchanged; a mixed gfx1100/gfx1201 pair is not admitted. `HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH=0` restores the segmented route; gfx1100 stays opt-in.
  - Measured on 2x R9700, Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts, greedy decode of 64 tokens, 3 fresh processes per arm, median after the first token: one card 29.86 / 26.82 ms, TP2 segmented 19.99 / 20.59 ms, TP2 whole-token 18.55 / 18.97 ms (ctx 128 / 8K). Rank-0 logits were byte-identical between the two TP2 routes on every token of every process; the TP2-vs-one-card route oracle passes with the same per-position logit differences; the first captured token takes about 25 ms instead of 39–46 ms for the segmented capture.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Flash-Next long-context decode: exact QSA filter select and pooled BF16 mirror, on by default; 256K decode token latency -5%.** Selected indices, attention, state bytes, logits and greedy ids are unchanged. Both are on by default on exact gfx1151, where they were measured; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT=0` / `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_POOLED_BF16=0` are the kill switches. Elsewhere: the filter route serves only F32 QSA states on gfx11+ (`=1` forces it; gfx1201's default fp8 state selects through the fused BF16 selector, which this route does not replace), and the mirror is gfx1151-only (gfx1201 stores BF16 index keys natively).
  - `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_FILTER_SELECT=1` replaces the single-workgroup select-from-scores pass of large eager score/select calls with a deterministic 2048-sample threshold, tiled candidate compaction and an exact finish (`kernels/src/qsa_filter_select.hip`); underfilled or overflowing rows repair exactly in-kernel. It is admitted only past the measured crossover of the select it replaces: 4096 pooled blocks against the hipcc select; against the gfx1151 PM select 24576 blocks for calls of at most 16 rows and 49152 otherwise (Halo per call: 1 row PM 18.6 / 50.7 / 160 us vs filter 37 / 39 / 39 us at 8192 / 32768 / 65536 blocks; 256 rows PM 0.26 / 1.15 ms vs 0.35 / 0.80 ms at 32768 / 65536).
  - `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_POOLED_BF16=1` keeps a derived BF16 mirror of each QSA layer's pooled index keys (lossless: `qsa_pool_rope` stores BF16-rounded values), synced past a block watermark after pooling, never snapshotted, clamped/invalidated at every F16-arena lifecycle site and charged in admission (16 MiB per layer at 256K); mirror-covered select calls run the PM BF16 rows16 score instead of the F32 one.
  - Exactness: `tests/qsa_filter_select.rs` on gfx1151 and gfx1201 (6,048 from-scores and 864 full-route cases equal to the incumbents, forced underfill/overflow repair); `qsa_select_pm_check --filter` 17,280 cases with 0 mismatches on both architectures (depth/tie/tail/budget families, an 8192-row chunk at position 245,760); 63 real natural-text captures (layers 0/6/11 at 8K-256K plus 8K prefill chunks) replay byte-exactly through the incumbent hipcc and PM pairs and the filter route; `qwen4_qsa_arena_check` four arms identical through prefill, rollback, branch and restore with 990,372 mirror blocks checked. The oracle gains `--filter` and the `QSA_DUMP_LAYERS` / `QSA_DUMP_LAST_FULL_DECODE` / `QSA_DUMP_SELECTOR_ONLY` capture filters.
  - Measured (Halo, Flash-Next GPTQ3, natural-text prefixes; one fresh process per arm running nat-8k, nat-32k, nat-128k, nat-256k with 128 AR tokens each; 2 interleaved processes per arm; trimmed-mean inter-token latency): base 29.92 / 30.73 / 31.21 / 33.11 ms vs both switches 29.90 / 29.88 / 30.48 / 31.43 ms; 32K -> 256K growth +7.7% -> +5.2%.

    | depth | base rep 1 | base rep 2 | both on rep 1 | both on rep 2 | change |
    |---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
    | 8K | 29.84 | 29.99 | 29.89 | 29.91 | -0.1% |
    | 32K | 31.32 | 30.14 | 29.87 | 29.88 | -2.8% |
    | 128K | 31.31 | 31.11 | 30.59 | 30.37 | -2.3% |
    | 256K | 33.15 | 33.07 | 31.16 | 31.69 | -5.1% |

    (ms per token; at 32K, 128K and 256K both candidate reps beat both base reps; 8K is a tie.) Greedy ids are identical on all four depths and on the 8-prompt lane AR cell. Prefill is unchanged within the process-to-process variance of these cells. A one-off 0.4-0.9 s stall when the QSA context maps its next 8192-token granule at 256K is excluded from the latency figures and is not QSA work.
- **CPU-only mesh splitting contract.** Dispatch now describes programs with stable `StepGraph` operation/resource IDs and explicit split rules; runtime's pure `split_program` binds TP reductions and PP transfers exactly once to manifest schedules. Weight placements retain their declared shard policy and logical shape. Single-device plans preserve operation order without collectives, and admission refusals allocate nothing. This is a planning API only: existing forward, loader and executor paths do not consume it.
- **SKU application-capacity emulation (`HIPFIRE_EMU`, `hipfire bench --emu`, `hipfire emu list|validate|estimate`), opt-in and capacity-only; no CU-count emulation on RDNA.** `HIPFIRE_EMU=<id>` (ten registry profiles: `rx9070xt`, `rx9070`, `rx9060xt`, `rx9060xt-8g`, `rx7900xt`, `rx7800xt`, `rx7700xt`, `rx7600`, `strix-point`, `krackan`; process start only) caps this process's managed application allocation (device, pinned-host, VMM and ROCr kernarg backing, charged by a ledger before the raw allocation) to the target's memory capacity, and routes the sizing and admission queries through the same budget. The host still executes at full speed on all its CUs with its own gfx, clocks and cache: it is "R9700 execution at 16-GiB application capacity", never a measured RX 9070 XT. CU masks are not used: they hung an R9700's MES on 2026-10-14 and are refused at runtime init, no safe method is approved, and clocks and power cannot be emulated without root. A target-performance figure is only ever an *estimate*: `hipfire emu estimate <id> --input <derating-input.json>` is CPU-only (starts no daemon, never touches HIP) and applies the disjoint-bucket derating model to measured kernel observations and host/target rates, reporting `unavailable` instead of a scalar when time closure, rates or run identity are missing. `hipfire emu validate <id>` starts a fresh model-free daemon with the profile installed and prints a schema-v1 receipt that keeps physical (raw hardware), emulated (budgeted ledger) and estimated fields apart; it exits nonzero on a capacity, host-topology or graph failure. `hipfire bench <model> --emu <id|off>` passes the profile to every spawned bench daemon arm, overrides `HIPFIRE_EMU` (including `off`), validates the id before anything spawns and logs the effective value. UMA profiles (`strix-point`, `krackan`) use a scenario (32 GiB host, 1 GiB carve-out, 4 GiB reserve, 16 GiB GTT; override with `HIPFIRE_EMU_HOST_BYTES`, `HIPFIRE_EMU_CARVEOUT_BYTES`, `HIPFIRE_EMU_HOST_RESERVE_BYTES`, `HIPFIRE_EMU_GTT_BYTES`), not an AMD limit. With a profile active the daemon's `diag` line gains an `emu` object (budgeted free/total and ledger) next to the unchanged physical `vram_free_mb`/`vram_total_mb`; with no profile every output is unchanged. A bandwidth-contention limiter is a proposal only and is not shipped. Measured sizing finding, documented in [docs/EMU.md](docs/EMU.md) and [docs/MODELS.md](docs/MODELS.md): an RX 9070 XT (16 GB) host with 64 GB RAM (TTM cap 31 GiB) cannot load Qwen3.8 Flash-Next, whose 38–61 GB of host-mapped routed experts exceed it; Flash-Next on a 16 GB card needs at least 128 GB of host RAM. Env rows: [docs/env-vars.md](docs/env-vars.md).
- **Flash-Next native MTP: a turn the AR floor retired now continues live in the next turn.** After the AR floor retires a request, its AR tokens advance the target alone. The MTP head used to stay where retirement found it, so the turn ended with the head behind the target. The session commit then dropped the live state, because live continuation needs head and target at the same position. The conversation's next turn restored the newest chunk snapshot (a multiple of 8192 tokens) and re-prefilled everything above it: on Strix Halo in a 32K agent conversation, a turn that stopped on `max_tokens` after retiring cost the next request 9,613 re-prefilled rows (4.8 s) instead of 55. Length stops themselves never needed a repair (an MTP window is capped at the remaining `max_tokens`); retirement was the cause. Now each retired AR token's target hidden row is copied into the spec hidden capture, and the head appends the owed rows teacher-forced, as an n-gram takeover appends its kept rows (one batched decode fill where it runs, else one Append per row). It does so every 1024 tokens and before every teacher-forced advance, the terminal pending-seed flush included, so the turn ends with head and target together and commits live. Retired windows stay head-free apart from that one row copy per token. Greedy output is unchanged (native MTP emits AR's greedy tokens).
- **Flash-Next native MTP: a think-budget force-close no longer re-prefills the conversation, and a stop sequence or other mid-window stop repairs the window instead of dropping live state.** A native MTP window commits up to k+1 tokens before the emitter observes any of them, so a think-budget force-close that fired on an interior token used to take the full-history Realign (reset, then re-prefill every prompt and decoded token), and a stop sequence or think-cap stop strictly inside a window used to invalidate the conversation cache so the next turn restored the newest chunk snapshot. The serve decode loop now clamps each native Qwen4 window to the emitter's `window_cap()`, so a think-budget force-close or think-cap stop only ever lands on a window's last token and the intervention needs no repair. For a stop inside a window (which cannot be predicted), the loop first calls the speculator's `repair_terminal_prefix`, before any reset, forced advance or prefill: native Qwen4 restores its retained pre-window target and head and replays only the consumed prefix, so the turn ends live (`HIPFIRE_QWEN_CACHE_TRACE=1` logs `[qwen-cache window-repair] … repaired=true`), and only an unrepairable window falls back to the existing reset. Requests that carry stop sequences now say so to the drafter through `SpecRequestConfig::stop_sequences`. Other speculators are unchanged (the cap applies to native Qwen4 MTP only); greedy output is byte-identical where no intervention lands inside a window, and `serve_harness --stop TEXT` sends stop sequences for chain checks.
- **CU masking is refused on RDNA (gfx10/gfx11/gfx12), at runtime init and at every mask entry point; `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_REDLINE_CU_COUNT` is refused and deprecated (CDNA unchanged).** On 2026-10-14 a CU-masked queue probe hung the R9700 (gfx1201) MES scheduler, forced mode1 resets and took the host down. `hipfire-config` now refuses a non-blank `HSA_CU_MASK` / `ROC_GLOBAL_CU_MASK` in the real process environment before ROCr (`Runtime::initialize`) or HIP (`HipRuntime::load`) loads whenever any KFD GPU node is RDNA, or the topology cannot prove otherwise; all-gfx9 hosts pass. In `redline-rocr`, `AqlQueue::create_with_cu_mask`, `QueueSet::create_with_cu_masks` and the queue/PM4 set-mask paths return `CuMaskRefusedOnRdna` on RDNA devices before any HSA call (the API itself stays). `diagnostic.replay.gfx1151_cu_count` / `HIPFIRE_GFX1151_REDLINE_CU_COUNT` can no longer build a mask: any value other than `all` fails PM4 replay preparation on any arch, and the key is deprecated (0.4.0, removal 0.5.0). The earlier "200× under `ROC_GLOBAL_CU_MASK=0x1`" stress recipes are withdrawn and are now refused at runtime init on RDNA.
- **Device spin waits are bounded at 500 ms; a stalled peer GPU or rendezvous now fails the request instead of leaving waves spinning.** Several kernels spin on a flag that another GPU or workgroup writes, and none of those waits had a device-side bound: the gfx1201 TP3/TP4 graph barrier `tp_graph_signal_wait2/3_gfx1201` (default-on for DeepSeek-V4 MQ2R on 3–4 R9700s), the gfx1100 TP2 barrier/reduce (`HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH`), the gfx1100 RMSNorm wavegrid kernels (`HIPFIRE_RDNA3_RMSNORM_WAVEGRID`, `HIPFIRE_AWQ_NORM_WAVEGRID`) and the gfx1151 `hc_compute_control` `rsqrt_once` handoff, which was a pure spin with no `s_sleep`. If the producer stalled, the waiter spun until teardown, and a reset on any card suspends every GPU process on the host. Each wait now reads the realtime counter (`wall_clock64`). After 500 ms, well inside the ~2.1 s MES hang window, it records its site in a per-device host-mapped error block (`kernels/src/device_wait_deadline.h`, `rdna_compute::device_wait`) and stops. TP barriers exit the wave. Rendezvous kernels finish their epilogue, so their self-restoring scratch stays consistent. Later waits on that device stop within about 100 µs. The host reads the block after the step: `Gpu::download_*` and `Gpu::take_device_wait_error`, `Gpus::take_device_wait_errors` after the DS4 TP graph sync and the dense TP2 logits, and every bounded-wait launch for a timeout left unread. A timeout fails the request with `device wait timeout on GPU <n>: <kernel> waited 500 ms for … (<epoch|workgroup> N)`, and after a TP timeout the TP graph signals are re-zeroed. The polls keep their `s_sleep(1)`, `rsqrt_once` gains one, and a healthy wait reads neither the counter nor host memory. On one R9700 (gfx1201) the normal-path outputs are byte-identical to the pre-change kernels: wavegrid, AWQ wavegrid at K 2048/4096/5120, `hc_compute_control_vec4_finalize` t256/t1024 with and without `rsqrt_once`, and the TP4 waits, 3 seeds × 20 launches each. Forced timeouts (an unpublished peer signal, poisoned rendezvous scratch) stop at 500.1 ms with the right site and detail and restore the scratch, and the next launch matches the old kernel bit for bit. In interleaved A/B launches, the bounded kernels measured 0.998–1.003× the old ones over 3 runs. A second harness measured up to 1.02×, but an old-kernel-plus-unused-kernarg control moved −1.1% to +0.5% (A/A floor 0.1%), so the remaining spread is code placement rather than wait cost. Gate: `cargo run --release -p rdna-compute --features lab --example device_wait_deadline` (one gfx1201 card).
- **`hipfire serve` optional bearer authentication; a key is recommended on a non-loopback bind.** New `serve.api_key_file` (env `HIPFIRE_SERVE_API_KEY_FILE`, flag `--api-key-file <absolute-path>`) names a UTF-8 key file of at least 32 bytes; on unix a group- or other-readable file is refused with a `chmod 600` hint, and only the path is ever stored or printed. With a key, every route (including UI assets and CORS preflight) needs `Authorization: Bearer <key>` and otherwise gets an empty `401` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`; unauthenticated `GET /health` returns only `status` and `version`. The key is optional on every bind: without one, behavior is unchanged, and a non-loopback bind logs one recommendation line suggesting a key. Windows never auto-generates a key. Local CLI/TUI clients read the configured key automatically through the new `hipfire_client::ServiceEndpoint` (http/https only, verified TLS, no insecure mode). docs/SERVE.md has a tailnet-only Tailscale Serve recipe.
- **Native Windows `hipfire stop`, `ps` and home discovery.** On Windows every serve binds a second loopback listener on an OS-assigned port with a per-process 32-byte random credential; `POST /api/v1/shutdown` with that bearer runs the existing bounded drain, and the route is not on the inference listener. A Windows `serve.pid` also records `manage_port`/`manage_token` (written by exclusive create and rename) and `hipfire ps --json` shows `manage_token_present` instead of the token. Windows `hipfire stop` uses the management route after proving the PID's image, creation time and listen port (kernel32/iphlpapi); Windows `hipfire update` refuses with the `install.ps1` re-run command. The Windows hipfire home is `HIPFIRE_HOME`, then `%USERPROFILE%\.hipfire`, then `%HOME%\.hipfire`, and never the current directory. Unix serves bind no management listener; their `serve.pid`, `ps` output, `stop` (SIGTERM) and home are unchanged.
- **`hipfire diag` reports host memory and, on Windows, per-adapter DXGI budgets.** `diag --json` gains an additive `host_memory` object (`/proc/meminfo` on Linux, `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` on Windows); Linux text output is unchanged. Windows adds a "GPU memory budget (DXGI, this process)" section: `QueryVideoMemoryInfo` local/non-local budget and usage for node 0 of each adapter. These are calling-process views, not engine admission budgets. On Windows the GPU-less OOM guard's swap test uses the pagefile approximation (commit limit minus physical memory).
- **Native Windows builds: the CLI and daemon cross-compile and link for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; Unix-only engine paths now fail with explicit errors there.** POSIX-FD VMM export/import is compiled out on Windows; PLE conversion, the PLE hot-row store/pack writer and prewarm return "unsupported on Windows" errors (run on Linux). Linux behavior is unchanged. A new advisory `Windows MSVC (advisory, no GPU)` CI job builds both binaries, tests hipfire-config/registry/client, smoke-checks `hipfire --version` and `hipfire diag --json` without HIP, and uploads an unsigned developer ZIP (`scripts/package-windows.ps1`, with `manifest.json` and SHA-256). This is compile/package coverage, not Windows GPU certification.
- **Windows: ROCm roots derived from a compiler path are plain `C:\…` paths.** The root that `hipfire-config` derives from the selected `hipcc`/`amdclang++` (exported as `ROCM_PATH`) came from `std::fs::canonicalize`, which on Windows returns verbatim `\\?\C:\…` paths. Verbatim drive paths are now rewritten to the plain form when it names the same file (under MAX_PATH, no reserved device names or trailing dots/spaces). Linux is unchanged. The Windows CI job's hipfire-config tests no longer assume Linux library names (`install_guidance`) or the Debian multiarch layout.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) fn-lanes: a request promoted from the native-MTP singleton into an AR lane now continues the AR singleton's text byte for byte.** With `serve.batch_spec = false` beside a loaded MTP head, the first request runs alone on the native-MTP singleton; when a peer queued, its promotion into an AR lane was refused after the lane had been admitted, so the stream went on from a fresh trunk state and its text differed from running alone (exactly one stream per process at C2/C4/C8). An AR lane now takes the request's trunk state and drops the resident head. `fn_lane_oracle --spec ar --promote P` checks the promoted stream against the AR singleton ids (at P=0 also every logits row and trunk state). Halo, 3 processes each at C2/C4/C8: every stream is identical to its alone run on AR lanes and on MTP lanes.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Flash-Next: agent-turn appends under 512 rows run on the >= 512-row kernels by default (401 rows 900 → 434 ms at 32K; KLD within +0.005).** Below 512 rows a prefill tile used to run every family on F32 SIMT routes, at about 2 ms per row. A 401-row tool result appended at 32K depth took 900 ms (446 rows/s), while a whole 529-row append took 461 ms. Now, on exact gfx1151, a prefill tile of 48 to 511 rows engages the canonical routing switch for that tile only. That switch, `Gpu::prefill_invariant_scope`, is the one the `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PM_INVARIANT` profile uses. Every row-count routing predicate sees the canonical chunk while launches keep the real row count. So the tile runs:
  - F16 WMMA for the dense and MQ6 projections;
  - WMMA QSA;
  - BF16 HC streams;
  - the WY GDN recurrence;
  - the F16 WMMA grouped MoE, with the symmetric IU4 arm suppressed for the tile (`Gpu::qwen4_append_f16_moe_scope`).

  No new kernels. Decode, MTP verify, tiles of 512+ rows and the `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PM_INVARIANT` profile keep their route. Other arches are unchanged. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA=0` restores the F32 routes, and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_APPEND_WMMA_MIN_ROWS` (default 48) sets the floor.
  - **Timing.** Strix Halo, Flash-Next GPTQ3, a committed 32K document followed by tool-result appends. Server prefill ms, median of 3 fresh processes per arm, interleaved:

    | Append rows | Before (ms) | After (ms) | After (rows/s) |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 145 | 427.8 | 282.1 | 514 |
    | 273 | 664.9 | 359.3 | 760 |
    | 401 | 899.9 | 434.1 | 924 |
    | 81 | 336.7 | 252.0 | 321 |
    | 529 (control) | 461.2 | 460.3 | — |

    The route loses at 33 rows (211 → 217 ms), which sets the floor at 48. Tool calls and text at 145/273/401/529 rows were byte-identical between the arms in every process.
  - **KLD.** Measured against the BF16-source teachers, with `qwen4_kld eval --append-rows R`, a new mode in which every scored row comes from consecutive appends of at most R rows. ΔKLD on minus off was:
    - WT2: −0.0011 / −0.0000 / −0.0006 at R = 145 / 273 / 401;
    - code24: +0.0032 / +0.0013 / +0.0008.

    Top-1 agreement rose at every point. The same route with the IU4 MoE arm is not shipped: it measured +0.035 (WT2) and +0.026 to +0.032 (code24).
  - **Coherence.** `serve_harness` tool-chain (`tool_chain_agent.json`), chain and battery, greedy with MTP. The on-arm floor was 16, so the 25–56-row chain and battery turns also took the route. Every turn finished, and every tool call had a valid name and JSON arguments. Lever-on text differs from lever-off only in wording, and the tool chain reaches the same diagnosis.
- **Sub-4-bit G2 run carrier: `scripts/subq4_g2_carrier.py` (lab tooling, no engine change).** One scheduler submission (class correct, card C, 30 GB device, 44 GB pinned host, hostmem lock, P1, 7200 s) through `/home/kaden/qcal/locks/fabric/fabric-run.sh` runs `fn_lane_oracle` (AR and native-MTP lanes with controls, ks 1,2,4,8), `qwen4_parity --mode state` and `qwen4_g2_replay_gate` serially on the ctl3-hot artifact and writes `g2.json` with per-check rc, command, hashes, card identity, mismatch counts and the first mismatch's repro. `--expert-vram-layers N` (integer, default 16; 15 only if 16 still runs out of device memory) sets `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS` for the lane and parity checks and the replay gate's CLI flag, is required in the replay report's params, and is recorded in `g2.json`. The scheduler is mandatory: `fabric.py` silently falls back to in-process admission when fabricd is down, so the carrier refuses unless a live fabricd answers `--status --json`, refuses when `FABRIC_NO_DAEMON`/`FABRIC_SANDBOX` is set, and inside the job requires fabricd to list the wrapper as a running `jN` job with the declared card, memory, lock, priority and owner, before any GPU child starts. It also refuses to start without an explicit real-artifact PASS smoke receipt, a `--code-sha` that matches the G2 worktree's HEAD, and a card whose UUID/BDF match the known map, the KFD topology and the scheduler. An MTP run that never drafts, accepts and rejects a window is a FAIL.
- **Real-model expert-slab retained-replay gate: `qwen4_g2_replay_gate` (lab example, no engine change).** On a ctl3-hot slab artifact it captures the decode body through the production lifecycle, checks that the production route, the prepared plan and the recorded-HIP oracle reproduce ordinary HIP byte for byte (logits, GDN, QSA, PLE state, lengths, position) at consecutive positions while proving each arm actually replayed, then frees and rebuilds the bundle between capture and replay and requires all three executors to refuse the changed binding with the route-identity error before any retained submit. Output is JSON with an exact mismatch count and error per check; the exit status is non-zero on any failed or unevaluable check.
- **Flash-Next on one R9700: per-expert VRAM hot set (default when a bound sidecar is present, exact).** On gfx1201, an aggregate-MQ4 artifact with a bound decode-routing sidecar now replaces whole-layer expert placement by default. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=0` is the kill switch and restores whole-layer placement; an absolute path in the variable selects a sidecar explicitly. An absent or unusable sidecar, or an artifact the hot set cannot back, falls back to the unchanged whole-layer placement instead of refusing the load. The VRAM budget is the same as `auto` (free − non-expert − reserve − tier tables − long-prefill stage buffers) minus a 512 MiB safety margin. It is filled with the experts ranked highest by a decode-routing sidecar, in one device owner plus one host-mapped owner, and reached through the existing per-layer absolute expert pointer tables. Kernels, slot order and combine order are unchanged. Long-prefill staging copies only a layer's host-tier experts into two VRAM stage buffers. EP, stock FP8, slab arenas, unified memory and an explicit `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_VRAM_LAYERS` are not eligible and keep whole-layer placement.
  - Measured on R9700 card B with the 512 MiB safety margin in force (default-on hot set vs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_HOTSET=0`): mq4-xts, PLE q4, max_seq 16384, 11 committed prompts. The sidecar `hotset-decode-v1` (sha256 `7f6760e1…`) was ranked on 25 prompts disjoint from the bench set and is the same one that ships. Both arms use the same binary. MTP is the median of 3 interleaved fresh processes per arm and AR is 1 process per arm. Greedy ids are identical on all 11 prompts, AR and MTP. It is not a blanket gain on every prompt: `records_parser_1k` regressed (43.1 → 39.4 tok/s, −8.6%) and `code_edit` is flat (47.7 → 47.6). Receipts: `/home/kaden/qcal/release-0.4.3/lever1-r9700/runs/{dec,cmd}-l2-*` (scheduler job j723).

    | decode tok/s | whole-layer | hot set |
    |---|---:|---:|
    | MTP warmup | 37.7 | 46.0 |
    | MTP glimmer_coding | 45.7 | 64.8 |
    | MTP fiction | 33.9 | 40.2 |
    | MTP bare_factual | 36.4 | 38.0 |
    | MTP humaneval_0 | 43.7 | 49.1 |
    | MTP lru_cache | 48.2 | 64.9 |
    | MTP code_edit | 47.7 | 47.6 |
    | MTP agentic | 36.7 | 43.2 |
    | MTP glimmer_prefill_1024 | 42.6 | 56.9 |
    | MTP records_parser_1k | 43.1 | 39.4 |
    | MTP records_parser_4k | 41.3 | 50.4 |
    | **MTP aggregate** | **41.41** | **47.44** |
    | **AR aggregate** | **34.95** | **42.23** |
    | p16k prefill | 3,787.0 | 3,786.3 |
    | VRAM experts (MTP / AR) | 7,168 / 7,680 | 6,479 / 6,850 |
    | peak VRAM MiB, decode runs (max of 4 per arm) | 31,662 | 31,517 |
    | peak VRAM MiB, p16k prefill run | 31,841 | 31,841 |

    The MTP aggregate rises +14.6% and the AR aggregate +20.8%, but per prompt the change ranges from −8.6% (`records_parser_1k`) to +41.8% (`glimmer_coding`). p16k prefill is flat. With the margin the hot set holds 6,479 experts under MTP and 6,850 under AR (daemon log); the whole-layer arm holds 14 (MTP) and 15 (AR) layers of 512 experts, 7,168 and 7,680. Peak VRAM is the maximum `peak_vram_mib` in each run's `vram-peak.txt`: the decode row is the max over the 4 decode runs per arm (3 MTP and 1 AR; the whole-layer maximum is the AR run at 31,662, the hot-set maximum the AR run at 31,517), the prefill row the single p16k run per arm. These are VRAM-poller samples, not an allocator high-water mark, and decode and prefill are separate measurements. In decode the hot set peaks 145 MiB lower than whole-layer placement; at p16k prefill both arms peak at the same 31,841 MiB.
- **New tag `qwen3.8:flash-next-mq3-xts`: the Flash-Next MQ3 XTS split base (59.3 GB), 3-bit GPTQ on 16,839 of the 24,576 routed experts and the MQ4 XTS bytes everywhere else.** It carries no PLE rows and loads them from the shared `qwen3.8-flash-next.ple-q4` (29.12 GB) or `.ple-q8` (54.4 GB) in `hipfire-models/qwen3.8-flash-next`, so the download is about 88.37 GB with the q4 rows or 113.65 GB with the q8 rows. Loader gates on the split base: greedy AR token ids equal the `qwen4_kld` reference on 8 prompts, MTP output equals AR, and both sidecars load.
- **Flash-Next expert-slab artifacts load on gfx1201.** A version-1 expert-slab artifact (MQ3-I4 experts plus an MQ4 hot set) loads as one slab arena per trunk layer, resident or host-mapped through the existing expert-VRAM placement, with a zeroed tail pad and a per-expert codec table. Decode (up to 8 rows) runs the K1 lane-union GEMV over codec buckets, planned on the device with no readback; longer prefill runs the grouped IU4 kernels of both codecs over the same buffers on codec-masked tiles. Other architectures keep refusing slab artifacts.
- **gfx1201 pointer-addressed slab MoE decode added, without production-route changes.** `mq_i4_moe_gemv` specializes MQ3-I4 R256 gate/up and R128 down plus symmetric MQ4, using the frozen 88-byte F4 ABI. Codec/profile buckets keep ascending expert IDs and original output slots; C1–C8 share each expert's weight reads across its selected lanes. Down outputs remain unweighted. The loader-independent `slab_moe_decode` launch module and lab byte-oracle runner are provided for sealed-route integration; legacy MQ4 kernels and existing bundles are unchanged.
- **MQ-I4 CPU reference twins added (no default-path changes).** Rotated dequantization, signed A4/IU4 folding, grouped BF16 epilogues, a frozen F4 decode reduction tree and original-slot routing provide byte/arithmetic oracles for PeaceMaker kernels; qt131–134 remain refused by production GPU dispatch.
- **gfx1201 PeaceMaker MQ3-I4 / MQ2-I4 one-row decode GEMV kernels added (`mq_i4_rows_pm_gfx1201`, not dispatched yet).** Four builder-emitted symbols `mq_i4_rows_{w3,w2}_{r256,r128}` over the planar qt131–134 tensors (32-byte kernarg `codes/x/y/M/K`, one row per wave32 workgroup, scale plane derived from `codes + M*K*b/8`), reproducing the frozen `mq_i4_reference::decode_gemv_f32` tree (R256 four group chains folded `(a0+a1)+(a2+a3)`, R128 one chain, shuffle-down 16..1, lane 0 stores). The lab-gated `mq_i4_rows_byte_oracle` example compares raw code-object outputs against the frozen twin. Existing PM bundles and the legacy MQ4 kernels are unchanged; no runtime path uses the new image. MoE original-slot indexing (F4) and gfx1151 remain out of scope.
- **gfx1201 PeaceMaker row-local R128 / K640 A4 producer for the sub-4-bit down projection added (`mq_i4_a4_pm_gfx1201`, not dispatched yet).** One builder-emitted symbol `mq_i4_a4_r128_k640` (48-byte kernarg `h/sorted/xq/signs1/signs2/P/L`, grid `5*P` wave32 workgroups, one per row and K128 group, no LDS, no barrier, no loop) turns grouped BF16 SwiGLU rows into the down `block_i4_128` sidecar record `g*L + sorted[p]` (repr(C) 72 B `{f32 d, i32 s, 64 qs}`, low nibble first, no MQ4 XOR): the `mq_rotate_x_128_v2` R128 rotation, then the `{5,7}` one-block quantizer with every divide a full IEEE `v_div_*` expansion. It reads only its own row group and a pad row (`-1`) writes nothing, so its bytes do not depend on the batch row count. The acceptance harness `crates/peacemaker-isa/tools/mq_i4_a4_oracle.py` (with the lab-gated `mq_i4_a4_byte_oracle` example) compares raw code-object output byte for byte with `pilot_solve.a4_emulate` over 10,000 random rows plus extremes, zeros, row counts 1/2/7/8/15/16/17, sparse slots and chunk boundaries. Existing PM bundles and the legacy MQ4 producers are unchanged; no runtime path uses the new image.
- **gfx1201 PeaceMaker MQ3-I4 grouped MoE GEMM module embedded in `rdna-compute` (`qwen4_moe_iu4_sym_w3_pm_gfx1201`, no default-path change).** `kernels::QWEN4_MOE_IU4_SYM_W3_PM_GFX1201` embeds the separate builder module of the NT1 gate/up (R256, K2560) and down (R128, K640) entries over planar qt131/qt132 experts, each with an F32 qualification `_acc` twin (four symbols, same 60-byte ABI, A4 activations, WMMA chain and BF16 epilogue as the MQ4 NT1 entries). It is gfx1201-only, has no hipcc counterpart, and is mirrored with the other PeaceMaker bundles. The MQ4 `qwen4_moe_iu4_sym_pm_gfx{1151,1201}` bundles are byte-identical.
- **Qwen4 per-expert slabs recognized CPU-side and refused before model GPU allocation.** Validate the complete v1 inventory and actual payload spans in linear time, report typed unavailable-kernel errors with W3/W2/MQ4 counts, and expose optional shared-codec scale validation; legacy aggregate admission and default paths are unchanged.
- **Qwen4 sub-4-bit expert-slab packer (CPU-side, offline): `qwen4_i4_slabs`.** `hipfire_quantize::i4_slabs::write_i4_expert_slabs` streams the pinned MQ4-XTS Flash-Next HFQ plus per-expert MQ3-I4/MQ2-I4 final-code NPZ records (R1 schema; exact f16-valued F32 scale conversion, `grid=uniform`, unit `sd`, allow-listed `scale_rule`) into the version-1 expert-slab container: 49,152 per-expert entries first (qt 131/132, 133/134 or the published 44/53 slices, from an R1 `alloc.ep2.json` point or `--uniform`), then every other source tensor byte-identical, with `qwen4.expert_slabs` and `qwen4.expert_slabs_provenance` metadata. Always-on verification (encode/decode round trip per expert, reopen and re-hash every payload and the whole file) plus an optional byte-for-byte cross-check against the Python R1 packs; a JSON receipt records hashes, counts and timings. New dependency-free `npz` reader. No GPU kernels, no loader admission, no default-path change.
- **Qwen4 sub-4-bit expert wire types registered: qt=131–134 (`MQ3I4G128R256`/`R128`, `MQ2I4G128R256`/`R128`), CPU-side only.** New planar formats (codes plane, then f16 scale plane) for the Flash-Next routed experts, a shared CPU codec (`hipfire_runtime::mq_i4`: pack/unpack, exact byte sizes, import validation) and the version-1 `qwen4.expert_slabs` metadata/entry-name schema (`hipfire_arch_qwen4::expert_slabs`). `rdna_compute::DType` gains the four variants as typed-qwen4-only formats that generic GEMV/GEMM/MoE/dequant consumers refuse explicitly. There are no GPU kernels and the loader does not admit them yet; no existing format or default path changes.
- **MiniMax-M2 decode runs as a typed dispatch step program (#666 G7).** Single-lane decode (eager and the opt-in hipGraph route) is one program per layer: fused Q8 QKV, the flat QK-norm, RoPE on the first `rotary_dim` channels of each head, Q8 KV attention, `o_proj`, then one sealed MoE call. The hand loop and the #397 super-op path are removed, so `HIPFIRE_FORWARD_LOWERED` no longer affects MiniMax; batched prefill and EP decode keep their routes (EP attention now runs the same attention steps).
  - The partial RoPE is an explicit `RopeKind::PrefixHalfSplit { rot_dim }` (rotate-half over a prefix of the head), shared with the Gemma RoPE kinds. It refuses `HIPFIRE_ROPE_INTERLEAVED_LEGACY`, which would silently switch it to the interleaved pairing.
  - The sealed MoE takes MiniMax's router as the sigmoid recipe with a selection bias (`e_score_correction_bias`, route scale 1.0) and admits the biased router at top-8 for every indexed expert format (MQ4/MQ6, MQ2-Lloyd, MQ3-Lloyd). A no-shared recipe now rotates the routed input with the routed gate/up's per-layer AWQ scale (and the opt-in down scale), as MiniMax's packed experts carry them. Experts are sealed once at load as byte views into the packed per-layer blobs.
  - Parity on Strix Halo (gfx1151) against beta `ed9e02bf0` on `MiniMax-M2.7.mq2` (sha256 `deb83795…`), 174 positions (140 forced, 34 greedy): token ids, every logit's bits, every layer's K/V cache bytes and `n_tokens` identical, eager and hipGraph; two beta runs agree bitwise as well. Greedy text identical; COR-007 probe 0 B drift over eight cycles on both. Decode 24.31 vs 24.32 tok/s (beta), median of 3 fresh processes.
  - New ignored GPU rows in `dense_rollback_gpu.rs`: the G4 rollback/retry/unload contract on `MiniMax-M2.7.mq2`, and the COR-007 eight-cycle load/generate/reset/unload VRAM drift probe (≤64 MiB, no fall on every cycle).
- **hipfire-quantize: MiniMax-M2 quantizes again.** Its Q8 router tensors were missing from the parameter accounting, so every MiniMax run refused with `param accounting does not close`.
- **`.rip` is agent-friendly: located, coded diagnostics with fix-its, a `check | fmt | explain` loop, instruction validation, and a tested reference and cookbook.** `peacemaker-rip check FILE --variant V [--json]` compiles gfx1151 and gfx1201 and reports every problem as `error[Rxxxx]` with the source line, a caret, a specific fix-it and `peacemaker-rip explain Rxxxx`. Driver and builder refusals now point at the call that caused them. Instruction text is validated per arch at the line that wrote it (unknown or wrong-arch mnemonics with the other arch's spelling, malformed operands). Declared defs/uses must cover the registers an instruction touches (R0603), and a register that nothing writes and the launch does not initialize is refused (R0604, e.g. `s2` on gfx1201). New builtin `workgroup_id(b, axis, dst)`. Docs: `crates/peacemaker-rip/docs/` (REFERENCE, ERRORS, nine cookbook kernels run on the CPU emulator by tests). Production `.rip` kernels emit byte-identical code objects. On a 12-task RDNA authoring eval with fresh agents, first-try emit went from 5/12 to 12/12 and first-try oracle match from 4/12 to 10/12; median attempts dropped from 2 to 1.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Flash-Next decode: the per-token PLE row fetch no longer stalls the GPU, and 120 fewer launches per token (exact; AR +2.6%).** A decode token used to spawn 16 reader threads to fetch its 16 PLE rows; the GPU finished layer 0 and then waited for them (0.94 ms per token at 1.4K context, 1.80 ms at 32K). Small fetches (at most 64 reads) are now read inline from the page cache with `preadv2(RWF_NOWAIT)`. Only reads that would block go to readers, and never more readers than reads. The greedy token id is copied to pinned memory right behind the argmax, so the host waits once instead of twice. On exact gfx1151 single-token decode, two launch merges reuse the incumbent arithmetic verbatim: router plus shared gate/up in one launch, and GDN z/b/a in one launch. Few-row QSA score/select now takes the certified PeaceMaker gfx1151 pair that prefill already uses. Every lever has its own kill switch: `HIPFIRE_PLE_INLINE_FETCH`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TOKEN_READBACK_EARLY`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_HEAD_FUSE`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_DECODE_ZBA_X3`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_PM_DECODE`.
  - New kernels live in new files; existing kernel sources are byte-identical. GPU unit tests compare every fused launch bitwise against the incumbent launches on gfx1151, and the x3 test also on gfx1201. The QSA test compares PM against hipcc selections for 1–8 rows.
  - Halo, Flash-Next, lane AR cell (8 committed prompts, 3 fresh processes per arm, interleaved): 34.15 → 35.05 tok/s; greedy MTP cell 60.19 → 60.41. Greedy token ids are identical on every prompt and run, and the 1200-ID p1 identity test matches v0.4.2's AR ids on all five arms (AR and four MTP routes). The PLE stall per token went from 0.94 ms to 0 at 1.4K context (rocprofv3) and from 1.80 ms to 0 at 32K.
- **Flash-Next: tool-call turns in the history replay the tokens the model emitted.** A follow-up turn whose history held an assistant tool call rendered that turn through the chat template. That stays a strict extension of the committed conversation only while the model's output is exactly the template's own canonical tokenization; a different BPE split of the emitted text, or spacing the template rewrites around `<tool_call>`, made the follow-up miss its live continuation and re-prefill the whole conversation. Qwen4 now replays a cached tool-call turn as the whole body the model emitted (reasoning, prose and every `<tool_call>` block), as it already did for plain turns, so the follow-up no longer depends on that. On Strix Halo with canonical model output both builds already continued in place: a 7-request tool chain and a 32K-context tool append reused every committed token before and after, with identical output. The cache key covers each call's name and arguments, and prose the client echoes with the calls must equal the emitted prose (empty content is accepted); an edited turn misses and falls back to the template render. Session-exact like every live continuation. Other model families keep the template render (`ToolTurnSplice::Envelopes`). `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_BYTES=0` turns reuse off.
- **Flash-Next session reuse is traceable per request.** With `HIPFIRE_QWEN_CACHE_TRACE=1`, every Qwen4 request (native MTP and AR) prints `[qwen4-session] route=… prompt=… prior=… lcp=… reused=… new=… source=live|snapshot|cold`: the previous turn's committed history, its common prefix with the new canonical prompt, and what the session cache reused. When the prompt diverges from that history, `[qwen4-session ids]` shows the token ids around the first difference. Host-side only; off by default.
- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: the built-in calibration now applies on any Halo kernel and NPU firmware.** `npu_trunk=auto` armed only when the compiled-in Halo calibration matched the host exactly, including the kernel release, NPU firmware and NPU PCI slot of the machine it was measured on, so every stock-kernel Halo logged `npu-trunk: off (calibration key mismatch: kernel.uname_release)` and served GPU-only. For the built-in calibration those three fields are now advisory: when any differs, it is used with its margin raised to 0.05 (the pipe must be predicted at least 5% faster than GPU-only, since the timings were measured on a different kernel/firmware), and the load logs `npu-trunk: built-in calibration measured on <kernel>/<fw>, this host <kernel>/<fw>; using it with margin 0.05`. Arch, build, model-index hash, epilogue and trunk route stay strict, and a user calibration file stays fully strict. Output is exact on either route; the calibration only chooses between them. First-load self-calibration is planned.
- **Crate maps: one sorted entry per line.** `scripts/check-crate-maps.py` now generates only stable structure — each `src/**/*.rs` file with a sub-list of its exported symbols, plus dependency and reverse-dependency edges — and drops the per-file line, public-item and test counts and the totals line. Every generated list and sub-list is sorted alphabetically with one entry per line (dependencies under one `####` heading per kind), so branches that add different entries merge without conflict. `--check` still fails when a file is added or removed, an export changes, or a dependency edge changes; a body-only edit no longer needs a regen. All 50 `crates/*/map.md` regenerated; `docs/crate-maps.md` updated.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Qwen4 grouped MoE down: batches of >= 9 tokens below the WMMA arm now run a bitwise O8×R16 twin that shares X through LDS (exact).** Below the F16 WMMA window the grouped down GEMM (M = 2560, K = 640) ran `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_gfx1151`, where every 8-row wave loads each group's X chunk itself. `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_xs4_gfx1151` (module `gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_top10_o8_r16_xs`) runs the same per-output decode, accumulation order and wave32 reduction, but stages each group's X chunk in LDS once per four-wave block (32 rows per block), so its output bytes are identical and only the X traffic changes. Measured: Measured on Halo (bitwise oracle, every case identical): 1.31x at 145 tokens, 1.24x at 273, 1.14x at 401, 1.06x at 529, 1.27-1.32x at 9-129 tokens. 32K-depth tool-call appends (3 fresh processes per arm, same hold, other twins off), server prefill median: 450.7 -> 442.7 ms (145 rows), 699.3 -> 690.9 ms (273), 944.0 -> 938.0 ms (401), 529 rows unchanged (WMMA arm). Batches below 9 tokens, the >= 512-token WMMA arm and every other architecture keep O8×R16; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_DOWN_TWIN=0` restores O8×R16 for every row count.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Qwen4 HC read tail: `hyper_read_up_fused` on batches of >= 16 rows now runs a bitwise twin (exact).** `hyper_read_up_fused_f32_sx` (module `hyper_read_up_fused_twin`) takes the same arguments, grid, block and dynamic LDS as `hyper_read_up_fused_f32` and runs the same per-output arithmetic; its output stores are deferred until the last pass, so the wave-uniform X loads become scalar loads instead of vector loads. Measured: Measured on Halo (bitwise oracle, 10 cases identical): 1.20-1.30x, 1.26x geomean over 16-529 rows. 32K-depth tool-call appends (3 fresh processes per arm, same hold, with the MoE twins on): 436.7 -> 432.0 ms (145 rows), 681.2 -> 672.4 ms (273), 925.2 -> 911.5 ms (401). Batches below 16 rows and every other architecture keep `hyper_read_up_fused_f32`; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_HC_UP_TWIN=0` restores it for every row count.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) Qwen4 grouped MoE gate/up: batches of > 8 tokens below the WMMA arm now run a bitwise O4×R8 twin (exact).** The grouped gate/up GEMM (M = 1280, K = 2560) ran `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r8_x4`; `gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_top10_o4_r8_pipe_gfx1151` runs the same per-output arithmetic with a fully unrolled ten-group walk and explicit-fma pinning of the incumbent's contraction, so its output bytes are identical and only the scheduling changes. Measured: Measured on Halo (bitwise oracle, 25 cases identical): 1.04x at 145 tokens, 1.08x at 273, 1.10x at 401. 32K-depth tool-call appends (3 fresh processes per arm, same hold, down twin on): 442.7 -> 436.7 ms (145 rows), 690.9 -> 681.2 ms (273), 938.0 -> 925.2 ms (401). Batches of eight tokens or fewer, the >= 512-token WMMA arm and every other architecture keep the incumbent; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_GATEUP_TWIN=0` restores it for every row count.
- **MI300X (gfx942): hipfire-native FP8 Flash-Next, stock HF FP8 checkpoint carried byte-for-byte.** Qwen4 / Flash-Next (arch 16) gains a stock-fp8 serving path that only gfx942 admits; every other arch fails closed. The on-card gates (one MI300X, stock-fp8 artifact sha256 `650e7f43…`) are listed under *Gates and tests*; no throughput claim is made for P1.
  - **Formats.** `qt=55 FP8E4M3B128` is OCP e4m3fn codes plus BF16 128x128 `weight_scale_inv` blocks per expert, padded to 256 B; `qt=56 FP8E4M3PT` is e4m3fn codes plus one BF16 per-tensor scale behind a 16-byte header. Codes and scale bits are stored verbatim, so a payload is checkable against the source safetensors manifest. `rdna_compute::fp8_e4m3` is the one CPU decode (`bf16_rne(f32(q) * f32(s))`, the teacher's formula, plus a model of the gfx942 FNUZ x2 scale fold); NaN codes and non-exact or non-finite scales are refused, `0x80` (-0) is kept. A golden fixture generated from the teacher (254 finite codes × 49 scales) is bit-identical. Layout and the FNUZ fold are in [docs/quant-formats/fp8-e4m3.md](docs/quant-formats/fp8-e4m3.md).
  - **`hipfire-quantize --recipe stock-fp8`.** Copies the routed-expert codes and scales verbatim into per-expert qt=55 blocks (gate rows then up rows, scale grids stacked), the PLE shards into qt=56, and everything else as BF16 qt=16; `mtp.*` is omitted. It refuses NaN codes and an unpinned source manifest, counts `0x80` bytes, and writes a per-tensor source byte-range sha256 receipt. On the pinned checkpoint it scanned 75,392 FP8 tensors with 0 NaN codes and emitted 73,728 routed + 128 PLE tensors (1,536 MTP FP8 tensors omitted); a sampled check against the BF16 teacher was bit-identical.
  - **Qwen4 stock-fp8 recipe.** `qwen4_recipe.id = stock-fp8` uses `Qwen4Profile::FlashNextArOnly` (no MTP head; EP and host-mapped loads refused). The `fp8_experts` capability is true on gfx942 only. GDN recurrent state is pinned to F32 (`q8` GDN state is refused), `memory.kv_cache` `auto`/`bf16` stay the exact F32 QSA state and `q8` is refused on gfx942 (an explicit `fp8` selects the FP8 K/V rows below), and PLE rows are read from the qt=56 store through the shared CPU decode.
  - **gfx942 BF16 trunk and head.** Every Qwen4 BF16 projection (GDN/QSA in/out, HC, PLE k/v, lm_head) stages x to BF16 and runs `GemmBf16Mfma` for every row count, M=1 included, so one reduction order serves prefill and decode. `HIPFIRE_G942_TRUNK_GEMV=1` restores the previous 1-row GEMV. The QSA output projection gains a BF16 activation rounding at the teacher boundary. gfx942 also freezes Full scratch and a 1536-row chunk and always uses canonical invariant routing. Other archs keep their existing projection routes.
  - **gfx942 FP8 grouped MoE kernel and sealed route.** `gemm_fp8b128_moe_grouped_bf16mfma` decodes qt=55 codes in-register to the teacher's exact BF16 (hardware FNUZ convert with the x2 scale fold, `0x80` decoded explicitly) and accumulates with `v_mfma_f32_16x16x16bf16_1k` in F32. It uses a fixed contiguous ascending K order (128-blocks, 16-wide steps), no split-K and no atomics (code-object census clean), over 16-row tiles with 1..16 rows, so decode runs the same kernel. The route is stable atomics-free scatter, FP8 grouped gate_up, unscatter + SiLU, FP8 grouped down, then a fixed ascending-expert-ID BF16 combine. Every M, M=1 included, takes the grouped kernels (one K order). SiLU(gate) is rounded to BF16 before the BF16 multiply, matching torch `F.silu` on BF16. Router and shared expert go through `GemmBf16Mfma`. qt=55 is refused on every other arch and by the generic MoE prefill resolution, path 1/2 and the MQ4 indexed GEMVs. The stable scatter's arch guard now admits gfx942; its source-sha pins in the gfx1151/gfx1201 jit-g0 manifests were refreshed with the compiled code unchanged.
  - **Gates and tests.** `qwen4_kld` gains an `s11ref` mode that scores an HFQM against a subq4 `.s11ref` (corpus-sha-bound, cold per-sequence windows, sparse assistant masks) and writes per-row float64 KL(ref‖hipfire) in score_rows order with sidecar shas; `qwen4_greedy_ids` writes deterministic C1 argmax generations as `{id, token_ids}` JSONL for the identity gates. `test_kernelsQA` gains 83 registered Qwen4 Tier C cases (`qwen4_tierc`: io, hc, gdn, qsa, moe, trunk) comparing GPU against CPU at Qwen4 shapes for every kernel the gfx942 C1 route launches, the FP8 route prerequisites, and, last, the `qwen4_qsa_parallel_*` direct-kernel cases; each case runs as its own supervised subprocess with frozen tolerances. Measured on MI300X (gfx942) at P1 (`cdc37659a`): FP8 dequant identity against the teacher, 22,860 / 22,860 BF16 bit patterns over 256 codes × 90 scales, for both the hardware FNUZ fold and the software decode; `qwen4_parity` PASS on an exact `cdc37659a` build; KL mean (95% CI) wt2 0.0199 (0.0177–0.0221), code24 0.0213 (0.0181–0.0247), assistant 0.0068 (0.0046–0.0087), each within its floor 0.0265 / 0.0267 / 0.0143; greedy C1 identity across 3 fresh processes on the 18 short battery prompts (64 tokens each); 0 atomics in the FP8 route's code objects. P1's serial QSA kernels could not finish the two ~16.5K-token long-context prompts (prefill plus 64 tokens) within 2,400 s, so long-context identity was decided on the P2 build below, where it PASSED (3 fresh processes per prompt). Tier C (`--filter qwen4_`): 81 PASS / 2 FAIL on the P2 build; the two failures (`qwen4_gated_delta_step_norm128_q8_gated`, `qwen4_gemv_bf16_xf32_x4`) are not on the gfx942 route (Q8 GDN state is refused there; the trunk takes `GemmBf16Mfma` for every row count) and fail identically at P1.
  - **gfx942 `tensor_ops` corrections (P2; the route enablement is the parallel QSA and GDN entry below; gfx1151/gfx1201 code unchanged).** The QSA attention kernels `qsa_attention_batched_hg4` and `indexed_attention_attention_f32_batched_hg12` build a raw buffer descriptor over the value rows with the RDNA3/RDNA4 dword3 `0x31004000`, which is not a valid CDNA layout; under `__gfx942__` they now use the gfx9 descriptor `0x00020000` (DATA_FORMAT 32, the CK_TILE gfx9 value). The F32 (non-Q8) `gated_delta_step_persistent256` recurrence is pinned to the sequential kernel's arithmetic under `__gfx942__`: the decay product, the delta subtract and multiply, and the query scaling use explicit `__fmul_rn`/`__fsub_rn`, and the `kv_mem`, state-update and output accumulations are serial `__fmaf_rn` chains in k order, so the compiler cannot contract register-resident state. The Q8 path and every other arch keep the original expressions.
  - Observed on MI300X (gfx942): `test_kernelsQA` QSA filter 3 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP (hg4 and hg12 at rows 1/7/33, ends 2802/402); `halves_state128` 1 PASS, output and state bit-exact at rows 1/7/33/257. The compiled `tensor_ops` `.text` is identical between baseline and current on gfx1151 and gfx1201. The `jit-g0` factory-source pin was refreshed (it includes `mq_fwht256.h` and `rope_yarn.h`) and `jit_g0_route_data` passes 7/7. Persistent-versus-sequential bitwise agreement on gfx1151/gfx1201 was not observed and is not claimed.
  - **gfx942 explicit FP8 QSA K/V: admission and state sizing (P2; opt-in `memory.kv_cache = "fp8"`; `auto` stays the F32 state; gfx1201/RDNA formats unchanged).** `resolve_qwen4` accepts `fp8` on exact gfx942 as well as exact gfx1201 (every other arch is refused as before; `auto`/unset on gfx942 still resolves to the exact `bf16`/F32 state, never fp8), and `resolve_qsa_format` maps it to the new `QsaKvFormat::Fp8Fnuz` on gfx942 while gfx1201 keeps `QsaKvFormat::Fp8`; an odd KV-head count or head_dim != 256 is refused, never rewritten. The format keeps the F32 raw and pooled index keys, so on Flash-Next (H24, Hkv 2, D256; one 128-wide index head, compress 4) a K or V row is 2 × (256 + 2) = 516 B (K+V 1,032 B) and a trunk QSA layer's context state is 1,032 + 512 + 128 = 1,672 B per token, 20,064 B over the 12 QSA layers (5,016 MiB at 262,144 tokens against 4,736 B, 56,832 B and 14,208 MiB for the F32 state). Admission, `Qwen4State` allocation and the load census charge these rows from the format (gfx942 is not a VMM arch, so context storage is legacy and charged whole). The native FNUZ encoder stores the OCP-equivalent code bytes with the f16 per-head scale (the decoder doubles the scale), differing from the gfx1201 row in one code only: FNUZ has no negative zero (`0x80` is its NaN), so a stored K/V row never contains `0x80` and the quantized negative-zero code `0x80` is stored as `0x00`, which covers an input of exactly −0 and a negative nonzero input that rounds to zero; every other finite code is stored unchanged. Unit tests pin the resolution table, the 1,672 B charge and that code mapping (every finite OCP code except `0x80` maps to itself, `0x80` to `0x00`, and `0x80` is never produced); Measured on MI300X with `HIPFIRE_KV_MODE=fp8`: the eight `qwen4_qsa_fp8_fnuz_*` Tier C cases pass; `qwen4_kld s11ref` mean KL (95% CI) wt2 0.0212 (0.0185–0.0243), code24 0.0218 (0.0190–0.0249), assistant 0.0088 (0.0049–0.0121), against 0.0199 / 0.0213 / 0.0068 for the F32 state, each within its floor 0.0265 / 0.0267 / 0.0143; greedy ids on the 18 short battery prompts are identical across 3 fresh processes. No speed claim. See [docs/CONFIG.md](docs/CONFIG.md#mode-k-and-v).
  - **gfx942 parallel QSA and autoregressive-prefill GDN routes (P2; gfx1151/gfx1201 routes unchanged, every HIP change is `#if defined(__gfx942__)`).** A new capability `has_qwen4_parallel_qsa_gdn()` (`has_gfx11_plus_simt() || is_gfx942()`) gates only the batched QSA score/select/attention launchers and the persistent GDN route. On gfx942 the public select now runs the rows8 (decode, at most 8 route rows) or rows16 (prefill: `prefill_invariant_scope` routes every count as 8192) block-score kernel plus `select_from_scores` for F32 pooled keys and budgets up to 512, or the fused `select_{f32,bf16}_batched` (budget above 512, BF16 keys, or an arena past the LDS row with scores in global rows), and attention runs the per-head parallel kernel, `hg4` (from 2 route rows) or `hg12` (from 16); the single-row select/attention launchers stay serial. The exact `mov_dpp8` selector (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_SELECT_EXACT`) stays gfx11+ only, so the env var falls back to the fused selector on gfx942. The kernels run wave64 with software 32-lane shuffle groups (width-32 shuffles, `lane = tid & 31`, `wave = tid >> 5`). GDN autoregressive prefill (the invariant prefill scope) now takes `gated_delta_conv_params_batched`, the F32 persistent256 recurrence and `gated_delta_gate_batched`, whose gfx942 F32 body is pinned to the sequential kernel's expression order (explicit `__fmul_rn`/`__fmaf_rn` chains); decode stays per-row, the WMMA chunk route stays gfx11-only, and native gfx942 MTP verify / row capture is not claimed (that gate is unchanged). The claim under test is bitwise equality with the `*_serial` HIP kernels (scores, selection with lowest-index ties, attention F32 bits for generic/hg4/hg12, FP8 FNUZ hg4 included) on gfx942, and batched-equals-sequential F32 GDN. Tier C (`qwen4_tierc`) renames the six off-route probes to `qwen4_qsa_parallel_*` (rows8/rows16, fused select f32 and bf16, attention generic/hg4/hg12), runs the public select/attention route cases over both scope settings, every arm and a geometry sweep, and adds non-dyadic cancelling "realistic" operands with forced lowest-index ties so equality is not proved by dyadic data alone, plus the FP8 serial-equality route case. `qwen4_greedy_ids` now reports each prompt's prefill wall time (`prefill_final` through the device-synchronizing logits download).
  - Measured on MI300X (gfx942), stock-fp8 Flash-Next: every `qwen4_qsa_parallel_*`, persistent256 and FP8 FNUZ Tier C case passes. Greedy ids are byte-identical to P1 (serial kernels) on all 18 short battery prompts, 3 fresh processes each, and the `qwen4_kld s11ref` KL rows are byte-identical to P1's on wt2 (8,160 rows), code24 (6,120) and assistant (11,079). The two ~16.5K-token long-context prompts (16,635 and 16,282 tokens) give identical ids across 3 fresh processes each. Prefill wall time, one process: 145 / 572 / 1,932 / 3,995 / 7,938 / 16,635 tokens take 0.38 / 0.93 / 2.6 / 5.1 / 10.1 / 20.6 s; P1 takes about 2 s and 12 s at 145 and 572 tokens (1-second timer resolution) and could not finish a long-context prompt plus 64 tokens within 2,400 s.
  - **gfx942 FP8 notes (P2 and byte-exact scheduling).** The gfx942 FP8 K/V encoder stores `0x00` wherever OCP e4m3fn stores `0x80`: an input -0 and every negative value that underflows to zero. Stored bytes equal gfx1201's except those zero codes, and decoded values are equal only up to the sign of zero; full byte identity is not claimed. The FP8 MoE kernel gains a contiguous raw-code wave64 transpose and raw-operand lookahead; accepted B3 keeps F32 X raw until LDS publication and scales unpacked until consumption. Arithmetic, activation RNE, the ascending BF16 MFMA chain and exported ABI are unchanged. `--byte-identity` compares against an independent SHA-pinned P1 source, including the full-D2 K=256 boundary; the immutable oracle is unchanged. Individually accepted B3 passes the independent-reference byte cases. Grouped top-10-of-512 gate_up, medians of three fresh-process medians (five warmups, 60 event-timed launches, warm buffers): F32 x C1 23.453 µs and 2,048 tokens 1,666.03 µs / 80.56 TFLOPS; BF16 x C1 22.251 µs and 2,048 tokens 1,514.57 µs / 88.62 TFLOPS. Both dtype routes meet ≤25 µs decode and ≥80 TFLOPS prefill jointly in individually accepted B3 (receipts: `/home/kaden/qcal/release-0.4.2/subq4/review/rcpt/g942p2/s2-B3-time-{1,2,3}.json`); these are kernel measurements, not independent serving-performance claims or exact-integrated-commit gate results. Down B1 → B3: C1 F32 x 12.75 → 12.35 µs / BF16 x 12.55 → 11.95 µs; 2,048 tokens F32 x 844.9 → 848.9 µs (+0.5%) / BF16 x 779.6 → 767.6 µs. Fresh B3 production ISA has no `vmcnt` wait between next raw X/W/scales issue and the current group's 16 MFMA updates; resources are 108 VGPRs for F32 x / 92 for BF16 x, with AGPRs and scratch both zero (`s2-B3-production.isa` / `.notes` in the same receipt directory). The FP8 grouped GEMM source and the Tier C FP8 decode probe refuse a non-gfx942 device compile (`#error`), so no RDNA object of either can be built or packed; every RDNA kernel-registry module compiles to the same bytes as on beta for gfx1100, gfx1151 and gfx1201.
  - **gfx942 HC read activation: one launch per BF16 boundary instead of one per row (exact; 16.6K-token prefill 20.54 → 12.37 s, 810 → 1,345 tok/s).** The non-gfx11 arm of the HC read ran `bf16_roundtrip_f32` once per row at each of its three BF16 boundaries: 288 launches per token, 4.79M of the 4.82M kernels of a 16,635-token prefill. Each boundary is now one launch over all rows of `low` (the round trip is elementwise, so the bits are the per-row launches'), staged in the read's `up` buffer, which the up projection overwrites before any read; `HyperReadOp` drops its unused `bf16_scratch`. MI300X, stock-fp8 Flash-Next, against the landed P2 receipts: greedy ids identical on the 18 short battery prompts and both ~16.5K long-context prompts (3 fresh processes each), `qwen4_kld s11ref` KL rows byte-equal on wt2/code24/assistant, Tier C hc 7/0 and hyper 5/0. 16,635-token prefill, 3 fresh processes per arm interleaved against beta 434b99f65: 20.494 / 20.536 / 20.545 s → 12.369 / 12.371 / 12.374 s (median 1.66×, ranges disjoint). Decode is unchanged (one row was already three launches).
- **PeaceMaker CDNA arm (gfx942 / MI300X): new `peacemaker-cdna` crate and `peacemaker_isa::cdna`, opt-in and not selected by any serving path.** `peacemaker-cdna` is the wave64 CDNA3 instruction model: an `Arch` variant with a capability table, typed encoders/decoders with `llvm-mc`-compatible printers (SOP, DS, MUBUF/SMEM/FLAT, VOP1/2/C/3, VOP3P and MFMA), FNUZ/OCP FP8/BF8, F16 and BF16 conversions, the gfx90a+ kernel descriptor, and a CPU wave64 emulator. `peacemaker-isa` gains `Arch::Gfx942` (its RDNA text paths refuse it), the CDNA3 wait-ledger rules, a checked wave64 `cdna::builder`, a wait-state hazard pass and native code-object emission. Three first kernels (`fp8_act_producer`, `fp8_moe_gate_up`, `qsa_fp8_kv`) ship with HIP twins under `kernels/src/pm_cdna/` and run Tier C in emulation, plus a `cdna_card_tierc` MI300X card harness (runbook in `crates/peacemaker-isa/tools/`, receipts under `docs/pm-cdna/card/`). Default-off at run time: `peacemaker-isa` now depends on `peacemaker-cdna` unconditionally and `rdna-compute` depends on `peacemaker-isa`, so the new crate is a transitive compile-time dependency of runtime builds, but no dispatch table, callsite selection or kernel set references the arm or the kernels, so run-time behavior on every arch is unchanged; no throughput claim. `docs/pm-cdna/route/` and `ROUTE-MIRROR.md` are design inventory for the gfx942 route mirror, not shipped routes. Crate maps for `peacemaker-cdna`, `peacemaker-isa` and `hip-bridge` regenerated.
  - **gfx942 GDN: decode rows take the batched persistent route, and the recurrence runs the register-resident `gated_delta_step_pipe128_f32` (exact; decode +5.5 % at 145 tokens of context and +5.3 % at 16.6K, 16.6K-token prefill −0.8 %).** A one-row GDN step (decode) ran the sequential `gated_delta_step_f32`, 204 µs per layer (7.4 of the 128 ms per token); `gdn_persistent_batch` now admits one row on gfx942, whose persistent recurrence is pinned to the sequential kernel's arithmetic. gfx1201's `gated_delta_step_pipe128_f32` (one thread per value column, register-resident state, one barrier per row) now also builds for gfx942 with persistent256's `__fmul_rn` / `__fmaf_rn` / `__fsub_rn` pins and is the gfx942 F32 route (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_PIPE=0` keeps persistent256); Q8 pipe128 stays gfx1201-only, and the gfx1100/gfx1151/gfx1201 `tensor_ops` objects are byte-identical to the base. MI300X, stock-fp8 Flash-Next: `gdn_pipe128_is_bytewise_the_persistent256_kernel` passes on gfx942 (F32, rows 1–1536); Tier C gated 6 pass / 1 known fail (`qwen4_gated_delta_step_norm128_q8_gated`, unchanged since P1, Q8 state not on the gfx942 route); greedy ids identical to the P2 receipts on 18 short and both ~16.5K long-context prompts (3 fresh processes each); `qwen4_kld s11ref` KL rows byte-equal on wt2/code24/assistant. 3 fresh processes per arm against the L1 build: decode 7.98 → 8.42 tok/s (145-token prompt) and 7.75 → 8.16 tok/s (16,635-token prompt); 16,635-token prefill 12.37 → 12.27 s; every range disjoint.
- **Qwen4 UMA context admission counts arena-inclusive QSA growth, and the context takes precedence over the reclaimable PLE page cache.** Admission counts the opt-in F16 arena at full capacity and partial contexts with pooled-index and four-token F16-arena row rounding (2 KiB/token/layer at 2 KV heads), plus the gather workspace, native MTP request buffers and the existing headroom. The PLE prewarm/load-fill budget is not a hard reservation: it is admitted up to what free memory leaves after the context, shrunk to 0 if need be (one `qwen4 ple budget: …` log line reports the shrunk budget), and the carrier caps the prewarm budgets it sets at that value. `card_cap` is the largest context that fits with PLE at 0 and moves with free host memory only. Oversized explicit `max_seq` values refuse under the default auto OOM guard with byte charges and the largest fitting `card_cap`; discrete GPUs are unchanged. Strix Halo receipt: Flash-Next at `max_seq` 262144 with native MTP on needed 33,660 MiB against 33,068 MiB free and was refused; it now admits with PLE 14,336 → 13,743 MiB, and `auto` resolves to 262,144 again. CPU Halo regression tests cover the 256K serve receipt, the exact-cap refusal, a full-PLE fit, arena/mirror growth and discrete behavior.
- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: the HIP interop now loads the HIP runtime the way the GPU runtime does, so it works on hosts without a system ROCm.** `railgun::npu` opened a bare `libamdhip64.so` through ldconfig, which fails on a host whose ROCm lives under a configured or bundled root (no `/usr/lib` copy), so the NPU trunk could not start there even though the GPU runtime loaded fine. It now reuses a HIP runtime the process already mapped (an `RTLD_NOLOAD` probe of each HIP soname), then tries `hipfire_config::rocm::library_candidates` in order, the same list and order as `hip-bridge`; it never falls back to a bare soname. A unit test pins that order without a GPU.
- Flash-Next load refusals now name the offending option.
- **LFM2 decode runs as typed dispatch steps (#666 G10).** Single-lane LFM2.5 decode (dense and the 8B-A1B MoE) is one `hipfire_dispatch` step program per layer: the LIV short convolution is the model-neutral `Step::GatedShortConv`, and MoE layers run the shared sealed MoE executor through its new biased sigmoid router (`MoeParams::selection_bias`: select on `sigmoid + expert_bias`, weight unbiased, normalize, scale; runtime top-k on MQ4/MQ6 experts). The hand loop and the #397 super-op path are gone, so `HIPFIRE_FORWARD_LOWERED` no longer affects LFM. Tokens, logits, convolution state and KV bytes are bitwise identical to beta at every position on `lfm2.5-350m.{q8,mq4}`, `lfm2.5-1.2b.mq4`, `lfm2.5-8b-a1b.mq4` and LFM2.5-VL-3B MQ4V2 (gfx1151); VL image turns are token-identical. LFM2 MoE files with mixed expert formats or expert AWQ sidecars are now refused at load. The continuous-batching decode is unchanged.
- **Muse Glimmer decode runs on the shared sandwich-norm operations, and Glimmer refuses every parallel topology (#666 G11).** Single-lane decode (`decode_step` and the DFlash hidden-capture decode) declares each layer as `Step::SandwichAttention` + `Step::SandwichMlp` (`crates/hipfire-arch-muse-glimmer/src/program.rs`), the operations Gemma 4 runs; the hand layer loop is removed. `pipeline::sandwich` gains the fields Glimmer needs instead of new steps: an attention output gate (`AttnGate`), a separate post-norm eps, NoPE layers (`RopeKind::NoPe`), `Activation::Silu` with the FWHT-rotated SwiGLU product (`RotationFamily` `WithSwiGLU`), the absolute-position windowed Q8 attention (`KvRoute::WindowedQ8`, tiled from 1,280 positions), an output stream that replaces the residual copies, and `SandwichRoute::Steps`, which runs decode projections as generic `Gemv` steps so the fusion table groups them. Every sandwich stream carries one explicit `pipeline::RowsRoute` (`Decode`, `Prefill`, `Verify`; lifted out of `sandwich`): the route, never the row count, picks decode or batched kernels, so `SandwichRoute` is only the ladder and fusion choice (Gemma 4 binds `Decode` exactly at one row). `SandwichAttentionOp` and `SandwichMlpOp` are validated before launch: a decode route with more than one row, batched rows without positions, windowed Q8 KV / draft KV / an output gate off the steps route, rows past the position-indexed cache (`position + rows <= physical_cap`), and the tiled / flash / rotation / BF16 scratch. `Step::RmsnormAutomatic` gains `rows`, so batched row norms are the row-complete norm op rather than `QkNorm`; Gemma 4's batched final norm runs on it too. Prerotated MQ4 gate/up (and the `.mq4r` q/k/v/gate) take the fused multi-output launch on every arch, not only on gfx1100. Design and semantic checkpoint: `docs/design/glimmer-declarative-program.md`.
  - gfx1151, on `muse-glimmer-30b.mq4` and `.mq4r` (route oracle against beta `09540d8c4`; serve and decode against `f826e2197`): tokens, full logits, the residual stream and all KV bytes are bitwise identical at every one of 2,148 committed positions (past the 2,048 sliding window and the tiled-attention threshold), as is the DFlash hidden capture; serve battery and chain transcripts are byte-identical. Decode (`hipfire bench --matrix --tg 64`, A/B/A/B): `.mq4` 11.82 → 11.98 tok/s at ctx 128 and 11.31 → 11.52 at 2048; `.mq4r` 13.48 → 13.86 and 12.84 → 13.25.
  - Loading a Glimmer source now fails closed when its config declares semantics the decoder does not implement: a non-SiLU `hidden_activation`, `attention_bias`, or a layer whose `layer_types` entry and RoPE theta disagree (full layers must be NoPE, sliding layers must rotate).
  - Admission refuses arch 14 with `pp > 1`, `tp > 1` or `topology.ep > 1` before teardown or allocation, with one message. Before, `tp > 1` hit the generic EP refusal, `pp > 1` the carrier refusal, and `topology.ep` was ignored for Glimmer.
  - Removed developer switches: `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_NO_{EMBED_NORM,QK_NORM,QK_SCALE,ATTN_GATE,OUTMUL,SOFTCAP}`, `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_ROPE_{ALL,INTERLEAVED}` (bring-up ablations), `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_FUSED_POSTNORM`, `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_FUSED_QK_ROPE` and `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_FLASH_DECODE`.
- **Muse Glimmer prefill, DFlash verify and the drafter run on the shared sandwich-norm operations (#666 G11 slice 2).** The slice-1 layer program also serves batched rows (`LayerIo::route`: a prefill or verify `RowsRoute`, whose batched kernels apply at any row count): batched prefill (`prefill_chunk_batched`), batched DFlash verify (`verify_block_capture_impl`) and the shared draft/verify LM head (`glimmer_lm_head_picks`) run it. The DFlash drafter declares each layer as the same two operations without post-norms, its attention on `KvRoute::Draft` (context rows projected, cached, copied with a recorded `copy_f32_buffer` launch and rotated; scalar `AttnFullF32` tile pinned through `AttentionFamily::run_full_attention_on`). The arch crate keeps staging, embedding and hidden capture; the hand layer loops and their projection helpers are gone (about 3,000 lines). `sandwich::gemm_rows` is the one rows projection of sandwich models: its `Ladder` is `Wide` (Gemma 4) or a `RowsRoute` (Q8 chunked GEMM; gfx12 residual, Muse residual and fused QKVZA prefill GEMMs). Once the next DFlash verify window would cross `max_seq`, the request finishes on AR decode, so DFlash requests that fit the context complete as AR ones do; behind that, rows past the KV cache are refused by the sandwich cache bound before any launch (`tests/kv_cap_gpu.rs`, `dense_rollback_gpu::glimmer_dflash_request_that_fits_max_seq_completes`), never written.
