  - gfx1151, `muse-glimmer-30b.mq4` and `.mq4r` against beta `28d70996c`: the route oracle (2,251-token prompt in 192-row chunks through the 1,280 and 2,048 boundaries, greedy decode, 1-row and 300-row turns, verify blocks of 16/16/1/7 with partial accepts, verify blocks at 300/1,270/2,040, a device-capture prefill/verify/commit, and five drafter windows with delta rows, a rollback and an empty context) is bitwise identical on all 130 digests per fixture: every logit, captured hidden row, KV byte, pick and drafter output.
  - A verify block now maps the VMM KV pages it writes. BF16 verify projections (calibration teachers, gfx942) take the staged MFMA GEMM prefill takes, and the BF16 LM-head picks no longer apply the output multiplier and soft cap twice. On gfx12 the MQ4/HFQ4 members of a mixed-format projection group take the residual GEMM like every other prefill projection.
  - Drafter: the MQ4 `fc` input rotates into a scratch sized to its K. The old view was `hidden` wide, so each rotation wrote five times its view and, near the context limit, past the end of its buffer. The unused `kv_input` scratch is gone.
  - Removed developer switches: `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_NO_FLASH`, `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_FLASH_FULL`, `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_O_RESIDUAL`, `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_MUSE_GEMM`, and the verify timing line of `HIPFIRE_GLIMMER_TIMING`.

## v0.4.2 — 2026-10-14

- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: the fused HC kernels now also run under the NPU pair route (16K Flash-Next TTFT −298 ms, exact).** The pair executor runs each bank layer as prepare, an NPU-fed Core and the output projection, and the interpreter dropped its HC pairing state between those windows. So under the NPU route the attention HC read never handed its gates to the HC write, the `hcw` output-projection epilogue never fired, and the MoE HC row fold was skipped. The GPU-only route took all three. `HcFusionCursor` now carries each bank's pairings across its windows, and `hc_gates` is bank-private (+16 B/row) because those gates now cross the bank switch. The fused kernels are the GPU-only route's byte-exact twins.
  - Halo, Flash-Next p16k, bounded memlock, 3 fresh processes × 3 measured per arm, interleaved against 1be80ed54. The all-9 median dropped from 7,194 to 6,896 ms (2,274 → 2,372 tok/s), with per-process medians of 6,882 / 6,892 / 6,907 ms against 7,709 / 7,086 / 7,194 ms. Every candidate TTFT was lower than every base TTFT.
  - Greedy p16k, p16k plus a cached-prefix suffix, and short prompts: NPU `on`, `emulate` and `off` bytes match beta.
- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: the memlock limit it needs is now exact, bounded and reported loudly.** Under an 8 MiB `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` (Tailscale SSH, many default logins) the Flash-Next NPU trunk turned itself off with a one-line `npu-trunk: off (mmap heap: errno=11 (RLIMIT_MEMLOCK?))`.
  - railgun and hipfire-xdna no longer map amdxdna BOs with `MAP_LOCKED` or `mlock` them. The driver already pins those pages (VM_PFNMAP), so the flags locked nothing (`VmLck` 0 kB). They only tested 3.1 GiB of mappings against the limit.
  - The memory the trunk really pins is its userptr A/C arenas: 1,053,843,456 B for the shipped two-bank 8192-row layout. The driver charges it to `VmPin`. At open the trunk computes that figure. When the hard limit allows, it raises the soft limit to exactly `VmPin` + need. Otherwise it fails closed with a message naming the bytes and a bounded fix: `LimitMEMLOCK=1318060032` / `ulimit -l 1287168`, which is the need × 1.25 rounded up to the MiB. No `infinity`. See [host memlock limit](docs/npu/npu-trunk.md#host-memlock-limit).
  - With `npu_trunk=auto`, a qualifying load whose NPU fails to arm now logs `WARN npu-trunk: off (...)` and serves GPU-only. With `npu_trunk=on` it is a load error. An ineligible model keeps the plain `npu-trunk: off (...)` line.
  - Output is unchanged once armed: greedy 16K bytes match beta.
- **Strix Halo NPU trunk: bank B's PLE rows are fetched ahead, and the trunk arenas ask for transparent huge pages (exact; no kill switch).** In a paired prefill, bank A now adopts the PLE row fetch started at admission instead of dropping it, and bank B's rows are enqueued as soon as bank A's fetch owns the epoch, so bank B's initialization adopts a fetch already in flight. The userptr A/C arenas are mapped on a 2 MiB boundary and marked `MADV_HUGEPAGE` before first touch, so under `transparent_hugepage=madvise` they can fault as 2 MiB pages and the NPU DMA and GPU arena reads need fewer translations. Arena lengths are unchanged, so the memlock need is the same 1,053,843,456 B; without THP support the arenas keep 4 KiB pages.
  - Exactness on gfx1151 + XDNA2: greedy output is byte-identical across NPU trunk `on`, `emulate` and `off`.
  - Measured (Strix Halo, Flash-Next 16K, NPU trunk on, interleaved against the base, 3 fresh processes × 3 requests per arm, median of per-process medians): TTFT 7,054 ms against 7,111 ms on the base. That is measured not slower than the base; the gain is not resolved beyond run-to-run noise.
  - On a loaded host THP backed only about 44 % of the arenas; the rest stayed on 4 KiB pages.
- **Opt-in `POST /v1/systemone` decide endpoint (#790 by @nwoolmer).** Answers single- or multi-question classification requests (choice, score, yes/no-or-unclear) from one prefill of label logits instead of generating text, with optional per-model temperature calibration (`decide.calibration.choice|score|noul`, default `1.0` = raw readout). Session mode reuses the chat prompt cache. Chat completions and the default serve path are unchanged; the route only runs when a client calls it. Evaluation harness in `scripts/jev_eval/`; published results in `bench/jev/` carry hipfire's own numbers on licence-clear datasets only (BoolQ-derived data was removed as CC BY-SA 3.0; see `bench/jev/DATA-LICENSES.md`).
- **Redline tapes now record the Lloyd2/Lloyd4 V-cache decode write.** `kv_cache_write_v256_{2,4}bit_vec` launched outside the recorder, so a retained Redline PM4 tape with `--kv-v lloyd2`/`lloyd4` replayed without the V write (Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B MQ4R: 623 launches instead of 633). On gfx1100 the VMM arm page-faulted and dropped the card off the bus. Both writers, and the same-shape Givens 2/3/4-bit and FWHT3 head-dim-512 K writers, now launch through the recorder. `redline_daemon_harness.py` gains `--kv-v` and `--kv-backend`.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151): PeaceMaker barriers now retire in-flight LDS loads of the slots they free.** The A4 gate/up epilogue (27B dense prefill, default on) and the Qwen4 QSA select kernel could read an LDS row another wave had already overwritten when the GPU was shared, so greedy output changed under concurrent GPU load (any second process; on an APU, any GPU client). Same bytes when the GPU is not shared; deterministic under co-tenancy.
- **gfx1100 DFlash draft: reuse shared MQ4V2 input rotations (#838 by @HUSRCF).** Noise Q/K/V, FFN gate/up and incremental context K/V rotate their common input once per group instead of once per projection. Each group keeps its existing GEMMs, weights and scratch. Default on exact gfx1100 only; `kernel.draft_shared_qkv_rotation`, `kernel.draft_shared_ffn_rotation` and `kernel.draft_shared_ctx_kv_rotation` set to `false` (or the corresponding `HIPFIRE_DRAFT_SHARED_QKV_ROTATION`, `HIPFIRE_DRAFT_SHARED_FFN_ROTATION`, `HIPFIRE_DRAFT_SHARED_CTX_KV_ROTATION` set to `0`) independently restore per-projection rotations.
  - Admission stays eager-only, 2..=16 token rows, compatible MQ4G256V2 weights with identical K divisible by 256, sufficient existing scratch, no AWQ or GEMM dump. Recorded/captured calls and every other architecture keep their original per-projection dispatch. The already-default stream-ordered draft copies remain unchanged.
  - Maintainer evidence on gfx1100 (7900 XTX), pinned Qwen3.8-27B XTS plus MQ4V2 draft: default-on, all-switches-off and beta greedy batteries match text, token counts, acceptance τ and cycle counts (5/5); on/off chains match (5/5), VMM throughout. gfx1201 beta versus explicitly-all-switches-on battery also matches (5/5), and the no-GPU flag-default test excludes gfx1201/gfx1151. Sharing removes redundant rotations, not a new numerical GEMM route.
  - Stock `hipfire bench`, three fresh processes per arm (five measured requests each), greedy DFlash: beta → shared-on decode remains 290.7 → 290.7 tok/s on `humaneval_0_has_close_elements` and 60.8 → 60.8 tok/s on `fiction_lighthouse`; acceptance τ is unchanged. These short-prompt measurements do not establish a significant serving throughput gain. The gfx1100 dense-trunk `redline_daemon_harness.py --pm4` also passes on beta and the candidate (659 dispatches, eight shadow positions, HIP/blob/PM4 logits, KV and recurrent state bit-exact); this is not certification of gfx1100 DFlash-cycle recording invariance.
- **DFlash on gfx1201: the exact-F16 projection producers, the GDN pre/tape fusion and the batched FA prep + paired Q8 K/V write (S3/S5/S6 of #759, by HUSRCF) now run on the R9700 too; byte-identical, decode unchanged within noise.** All three were exact-gfx1100 only. `ArchCaps::supports_dflash_f16_projection_fusions`, `supports_dflash_gdn_pre_fusions` and `supports_dflash_fa_batch_fusions` admit exact gfx1100 and exact gfx1201; gfx1151 and every other arch keep the pre-fusion launches.
  - On gfx1201 the F16 QKVZA/QKV/gate-up consumers launch under the module names the F32 entries use (`gemm_*_hfq4g256_wmma_gfx12_mq4v2`, the ldsstage arm under `HIPFIRE_HFQ4G256_LDSSTAGE=1`), and the F16 projection route stays off inside the gfx1201 IU4 verify scope, where every projection already takes the A4 producer + IU4 GEMM.
  - The AWQ F16 producer now reduces with the live F32 AWQ symbol's two-level wave-shuffle tree instead of the retired LDS tree, which could move `rms` by one ULP and cross an F16 rounding boundary on gfx1201.
  - Fix: the S5 GDN pre conv now matches the reference's operation order on every arch, spelling the first step as `fmaf(w2, s0, w3*x)`. The old `fmaf(w3, x, w2*s0)` rounded `w2*s0` instead of `w3*x`, a 1-ULP change on `q_raw`, so `test_dflash_gdn_pre_gfx1100` failed on a 7900 XTX (gfx1100, beta) and on an R9700. It now passes on both, and greedy output is unchanged.
  - The S3, S5 and S6 parity oracles (`test_mq_f16_projection_producers_gfx1100`, also with the ldsstage arm, `test_dflash_gdn_pre_gfx1100`, `test_qwen35_fa_batch_fusion_gfx1100`) pass bit-exact on gfx1201.
  - Qwen3.8-27B MQ4-XTS + mq4v2 DFlash draft on an R9700, `hipfire bench`, fresh process per cell, 3 per arm, rotated order: every committed text is byte-identical across beta, fused and all-off (code 27/27, 8K prose 9/9, 21.6K code 9/9). Medians beta → fused: 21.6K TTFT 4,670 → 4,665 ms, decode 50.6 → 50.9 tok/s; 8K TTFT 1,647 → 1,646 ms, decode 83.8 → 84.3 tok/s; code decode 311.9 → 312.2 tok/s. Seeded serve battery transcripts are byte-identical to beta.
  - On a 7900 XTX (gfx1100), same fixture and protocol: all three oracles pass and every committed text is byte-identical across beta, fused and all-off (27/27, 9/9, 9/9). Medians beta → fused: 21.6K TTFT 8,410 → 8,418 ms (beta spread 8,409–8,418), decode 56.7 → 56.7 tok/s; 8K TTFT 3,070 → 3,068 ms, decode 87.6 → 88.4 tok/s; code decode 307.9 → 308.0 tok/s.
  - Kill switches as before: `HIPFIRE_MQ_F16_PROJECTION_OFF=1`, `HIPFIRE_GDN_PRE_FUSE_OFF=1`, `HIPFIRE_FA_BATCH_FUSE_OFF=1`.
- **Flash-Next session-cache SSD tier (opt-in, default off).** `memory.session_cache_disk_bytes` / `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_DISK_BYTES` enables a directory-wide disk budget for device-evicted or model-unloaded prefill snapshots. `memory.session_cache_dir` / `HIPFIRE_SESSION_CACHE_DIR` selects the locked directory (default `$HIPFIRE_HOME/session-cache`); snapshots restore across daemon restarts of the same executable build. Snapshots still on the device at a kill/crash are not persisted. Thanks @fivetide (#840).
- **gfx1201: opt-in exact MQ4V2 K5120 gate/up decode specialization** (`kernel.gfx12_mq4v2_gateup_k5120` / `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_GATEUP_K5120`, default off), by @HUSRCF. Exact `(gate_m, up_m, K) = (17408, 17408, 5120)` only; preserves DEV/RT cache policy, arithmetic order and weight layout. The existing gfx1100 default is unchanged. The original two-pair serving comparisons were within run-to-run noise; this is an experimental opt-in, not an established serving-speed improvement.
- **gfx1100 decode campaign (#845, @ghazni101):** on gfx1100, Qwen3.5-4B decode now fuses the conv scalar prep, the gated-norm MQ rotate, the FA prep and the FA epilogue. Output is bit-identical, and decode on an RX 7900 XTX is +5.5% (MQ4) and +3.0% (MQ3). MQ3-Lloyd prefill on gfx1100 uses the 4× batch tile from batch 64 on projections with ≥4096 rows, and at batch 128–191 on the residual. Output is bit-identical; 4B pp64 is +22%, 9B pp64 +34% and 4B pp127–191 +7–17%. Each fusion has a `0` kill switch (`HIPFIRE_CONV_SCALAR_PREP`, `HIPFIRE_GATED_NORM_MQ_ROTATE`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_FA_PREP_FUSE`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_FA_EPILOGUE_FUSE`). The fusion defaults cover only the Qwen3.5-4B geometry and the batch-tile policy only MQ3-Lloyd; other arches keep their routes. The folded Q8 KV writer (`HIPFIRE_FA_KVWRITE_FOLD=1`) and the AWQ wavegrid norm (`HIPFIRE_AWQ_NORM_WAVEGRID=1`, not bit-exact) stay opt-in.
- Dense Qwen TP2 gains opt-in alternating-partial synchronization (`HIPFIRE_TP_PEER_ALTERNATING=1`) and exact-gfx1100 whole-token HIP graphs (`HIPFIRE_TP_FULL_GRAPH=1`). Runtime/mesh owns the reusable graph/barrier orchestration and double-buffered coherent exchange; the existing reduction protocol remains the default.
- gfx1100: IU4 V2C prefill transposes complete 4×4 tile groups for N ≥ 4096, Q8 GQA attention stages V in 64-key slabs, and HIN C2 quantization uses one pass. Other architectures retain their existing kernels.
- **Qwen3.5-family VMM KV admission (#833 by @HUSRCF):** exact gfx1100 TP1 admits FWHT2/3/4 K with Q8 or Lloyd2/3/4 V. On gfx1151, an FWHT K override of unset/`auto`/`q8` mode now refuses unless legacy storage is explicitly requested; ordinary mode-only fallback remains available. Admission-only change; gfx1201 and defaults are unchanged.
- **Experimental gfx1100 long-context DFlash FA2 split-KV S8 verifier** (@HUSRCF, #817): opt-in via `kernel.gfx1100_fa2_split_verify` (default **off**), restricted to exact gfx1100 dense Qwen H24/KV4/D256, plain-Q8 K and V on VMM KV and sequential B4..32 above the live-context threshold (4,096 by default). In-envelope legacy-KV loads are refused, never silently substituted. HipGraph/retained recording remain conditional on precompiled kernels, fixed-address Q16 scratch and sufficient S8 partials; Redline/PM4 retains B16 and existing single-GPU/state guards. Not a supported default: ON is a different-numerics route (split-KV partial/merge reduction order), not greedy-byte-identical to OFF; on gfx1100 with a 20.7K-token prompt, greedy DFlash text stays coherent but diverges from OFF after about 700 characters. Promoting it to a default would need KLD, a task tie and maintainer approval.
- Container validation uses supported Python harness commands and honors `HIPFIRE_HOME` for runtime storage.
- Redline reproduction packages now capture and install indexed-object metadata alongside code objects and hashes for fail-closed kernel verification.
- Detached `hipfire serve` now preserves `--vision` and forwards `--max-seq` exactly once, matching foreground serving.
- **Serve queue config fallbacks now come from the registered defaults.** If the process config lacked `serve.queue_timeout_ms` the daemon fell back to a hard-coded 30000 ms instead of the registered 600000 ms (`serve.max_queue` and `serve.max_queue_bytes` had the same hard-coded fallback); all three now read the hipfire-config field-table default.
- **Strix Halo (gfx1151) 27B DFlash: exact 16-row verify GEMMs, +38 % decode (`HIPFIRE_GFX1151_XLDS`, default on for exact gfx1151; `0` is the kill switch).** The DFlash target verify (16 rows) ran its MQ4G256V2 projections through the one-wave base gfx11 WMMA kernels at about 132 GB/s, against 241 GB/s measured read bandwidth. Each 16-row block re-read the whole 16 x K fp16 activation panel from L2 (3.8x the weight bytes) and issued its weight loads one K-pair at a time. New module `gemm_mq4g256v2_wmma_gfx1151_xlds` (gate_up, qkvza, qkv, residual including the verify lm_head; the base ABIs) runs 4 waves x 16 weight rows per block, stages each 256-K slab of the activation panel in LDS once per block (double-buffered), loads each lane's whole 136 B group up front and dequantizes with packed exact half2 math. Per output the WMMA chain, K order, dequant values and epilogue are the base kernels', so outputs are byte-identical. It is taken for 1..=16 rows with K % 256 == 0, eager and under the DFlash verify HipGraph capture; Redline replay recording and every other arch keep the base kernels. hipcc for 0.4.2; no PeaceMaker twin yet.
  - Kernel-level on Strix Halo (16 rows, rotating weight copies past the 32 MB MALL): gate_up 136 → 196 GB/s, qkvza 133 → 193, qkv 158 → 186, down 134 → 208, out-proj 130 → 188, lm_head 167 → 199. In the traced verify: 98.0 → 66.3 ms of GEMMs per cycle (132 → 195 GB/s) and lm_head 4.1 → 3.5 ms; the draft's 16-row GEMMs take the same kernels.
  - Also on exact gfx1151, two existing pure-copy DFlash state paths: the DeltaNet snapshot bulk copy (one launch per save/restore instead of about 190 blocking copies; `HIPFIRE_DN_SNAPSHOT_BULK_OFF=1` opts out) and the hidden-ring commit/scatter fusion (`HIPFIRE_HIDDEN_SCATTER_FUSE_OFF=1`), which on gfx1151 refuses a windowed scatter whose rows would alias one destination row, so the sequential last-row-wins bytes hold. gfx1100/gfx1201 unchanged.
  - Measured (Strix Halo, PerfOpt kernel, 27B XTS sha256 `3e38ccba…`, paired DFlash draft `d0a74a23…`, `hipfire bench --backend noslots --workload stateless --max-tokens 256`, 3 fresh processes per arm, 5 runs each, median of per-process medians, interleaved base/new): DFlash code (`merge_sort_thinking_off.txt`) 97.9 → 135.3 tok/s (+38.2 %, cycle 144.9 → 104.8 ms, τ 13.18 both); DFlash prose (`prose_river_short.txt`) 19.0 → 26.3 tok/s (+38.4 %, cycle 133.4 → 96.4 ms, τ 1.52 both); AR 15.0 → 15.0 on both prompts. Phase split (prose, mean ms per cycle): verify 111.3 → 78.8, draft 15.6 → 12.3, snapshot 1.40 → 1.11, restore 1.42 → 1.08, scatter 0.04 → 0.01, replay 2.94 → 2.93.
  - Exactness: decoded text byte-identical to beta `21cc6f023` in every Halo arm (DFlash and AR, code and prose, all runs) and with all three switches off; kernel oracle `test_mq4v2_xlds_gfx1151` (`--features lab`) bit-exact against the base kernels on all 27B verify shapes plus odd shapes at N = 1, 5, 16. `serve_harness` battery + chain with DFlash on: same decoded text, finish, cached tokens, τ and cycles as beta on every turn. `redline_daemon_harness` plain-AR passes; its `--dflash-verify-shadow` arm fails on gfx1151 identically on beta (recorded-HIP/PM4 hidden staging and recorded-HIP logits, not a gfx1151 production route), with byte-identical per-position logits and final-hidden hashes across beta and every switch setting. R9700 (gfx1201) greedy DFlash code/prose and AR code byte-identical to beta with unchanged tok/s.
- **Flash-Next on Strix Halo: pipelined trunk input projections on the XDNA2 NPU ship default on via `auto` (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK` / `fusions.npu_trunk` = `off|auto|on|emulate`).** Auto arms only with an open NPU and matching keyed calibration (device, firmware, release contract, uname, model index sha, epilogue, trunk route); any load refusal logs one reason and runs GPU-only. The configured calibration file wins; if absent, built-in measured Halo overlay/stock calibration covers the reference key, never arbitrary devices. Every other arch stays silently off without NPU I/O or allocation. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_NPU_TRUNK=off` / `fusions.npu_trunk = off` is the kill switch. Two consecutive prefill banks (8192-row chunks, or a rebalanced last pair) run layer-major as banks A/B: G80 BF16 integer epilogue, W8 from stock MQ6, per-row int8 activations and one RNE to BF16, row-major host-registered arenas consumed directly by the GPU, with device-side waits and a watchdog. `emulate` is the GPU-only bit-exact twin. Deterministic calibrated plans admit natural and rebalanced pairs and preserve session-cache boundaries; unpaired chunks and tails remain GPU-native. Request-time NPU failures remain sticky and fail closed.
  - Exactness on gfx1151 + XDNA2 (PerfOpt kernel, zab overlay, default serve config): greedy output `on` == `emulate` byte-identical at p16k, including a session-cache hit on the snapshot staged inside a pair; NPU transport, row-major drain and BF16-arena consumer oracles exact against CPU/incumbent references. Flag-off: `serve_harness` battery + chain identical to beta `d6c723bc5`.
  - Measured (default serve config, session cache on, zab overlay in both arms, 3 fresh processes per arm, 2 cold nonce-prefixed requests each, median of per-process medians): p16k (16,355 tokens) TTFT off 7,878 ms → `on` 7,173 ms (−8.9 %); p56k (57,556 tokens) off 30,052 ms → `on` 27,867 ms (−7.3 %). Peak GTT +0.8 GiB at 56K.
  - Quality accepted by Kaden: S11 j103 BF16-direct family B, ΔKLD WT2 −0.0005 / code24 +0.0052, both within the +0.015 cap.
  - Overhead batch 1 (exact; no numerics change): the C/A arenas are flushed once at open instead of after every NPU chain (the CPU never reads them in production), and the K2560 activation pack is coalesced (0.456 → 0.321 ms per pack, byte-identical). Measured with fine tracing enabled (pessimistic), same instrument otherwise: p16k off 7,881 → `on` 7,120 ms (−9.7 %), p56k off 30,024 → `on` 27,665 ms (−7.9 %); `on` == `emulate` greedy on both requests.
  - Rebalanced pairs, row-adaptive NPU chains and in-bank session sub-launch (no numerics-contract change: still BF16-direct, `emulate` is still the bit-exact twin). The planner's rebalanced last pair (two banks of about equal rows, e.g. 9,330 → 4,672 + 4,658, or the 57K tail 8,404 → 4,224 + 4,180) is now executable. A bank below 8,192 rows runs the G80 program of its own 256-row wave count: the relay builds it on first use, at most 4 wave counts per load, and a pair that would need a fifth runs GPU-native. Before this, a 4.6K bank paid the full 8,192-row chain and the 9.3K pair measured 13 % slower than `off`. A default-cache snapshot row inside a pair no longer ends the group: that bank's Core and PLE depthwise step run as two launches split at the row, with the state staged between them. `on`/`emulate` follow the calibrated plan whenever the calibration matches. Bank B's arenas initialize after bank A's first kick.
    - Exactness (gfx1151 + XDNA2, default serve config): greedy `on` == `emulate` at 9.3K (rebalanced pair, cache hit at 8,192 inside bank B) and at 57K (rebalanced tail, cache hit at 57,344). With the route forced `off`, the `serve_harness` battery + chain match the earlier beta flag-off hashes (`20866dcdd924` / `7152cad43aa3`).
    - Measured (default `auto` vs `off`, same instrument as above, no trace env, median of per-process medians): 9,330 tokens 4,625 → 4,319 ms (−6.6 %; previously GPU-only); p16k 7,878 → 7,085 ms (−10.1 %); 24,438 tokens 11,944 → 11,159 ms (−6.6 %); p56k 30,047 → 27,260 ms (−9.3 %).
  - Calibration schema 4 is BF16-direct only: it drops the int32 contract (`glue.fold_ms_per_col_8192`, and `epilogue` must be `bf16`). The planner refit (per-family DRAM contention, pipelined-visit overhead, QSA order stall) loads v4 records unchanged. The built-in calibration stays the measured v4.
  - Out-projection program, not wired into the serving route: a K = 6144 BF16-direct G80 program (`gemm_g80_n512`, N = 512 panels, B panel resident in the memtiles) with its packing, a second-PDI relay hardware context and a transport oracle.
    - On silicon it is exact against the twin at 8192 × 512 × 6144 and runs 24.6 TOPS alone. A 5-panel chain takes 13.0 ms alone and 19.3 ms against GPU copy load; a context switch costs 1.4 ms.
    - Wiring it into the QSA out projection was measured and not shipped: it was +77 ms at p16k and was not byte-exact against its twin.
- **Flash-Next (Qwen4) EP2/EP4 owner-routed prefill chunk MoE (`HIPFIRE_EP_OWNER_ROUTED_PREFILL`, default on under EP2, `=0` disables).** Chunks of at least 512 rows run the grouped symmetric IU4 MoE on each rank's own experts only (tile ids masked to the -1 sentinel in a copy, certified PeaceMaker kernels) and pull peer-owned tiles' BF16 grouped rows over P2P before the unchanged combine; other chunks keep the full-table route. Needs EP2 on gfx1201, sym-IU4 and all layers symmetric with identical verdicts on every rank, else refused collectively with one log line per rank. The transport and the 4-source pull are rank-generic and CPU-tested, but EP4 is refused at admission (`EP4 owner-routed prefill not yet gated`) because its hardware cells have not run.
  - Exactness on 2x R9700 (pinned XTS sha256 `8b15b6fe…`): EP2 flag-off == base EP2 == single, S1-on == single (p8k 64 tokens AR, 65/65 logits identical; 4396-token prompt with a 300-row tail chunk), bitwise per-tile `y_down_grouped`, FFN output and HC-stream digests at layers 0/2/3/31/47 on p8k and p16k (70 boundaries, both ranks), M0 tile-mask oracle on the PM mask kernel. Receipts: `fn-ep/coal/s1/RESULT.md`.
  - Final gate on the combined build (`HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS=1 HIPFIRE_EP_OWNER_ROUTED_PREFILL=1 HIPFIRE_EP_EXPERT_STAGE=1`, W1 default-on base `a55d72ba3`): 25/25 exactness compares pass vs single (AR and MTP greedy 256 tokens at p8k and p16k, tail-300 prompt, obatch ar512/mtp512f/mtp8192f/s3, flag-off EP2 == base EP2, bitwise chunk dumps with S2 off); EP-off redline harness and serve battery + chain pass. Prefill (3 fresh processes per arm, warm, committed prompts): single 3,033 / 3,754 tok/s at p8k / p16k, EP2 combined 3,943 / 4,308 (+30.0 % / +14.7 %, every run above the single median; measured on the MR binary, corroborated by an earlier run at 3,943 / 4,311). Decode vs W1 default (MTP code / MTP prose / AR code medians): 84.0 -> 84.1, 62.2 -> 65.9, 50.4 -> 50.3 tok/s (no trade); resident layers unchanged (29 MTP / 30 AR). S2-on exactness is shown by logits/state/MTP equality; the chunk-dump path does not cover the staged branch. Receipts: `fn-ep/coal/s1/RESULT.md`.
  - Default-on revision (both flags on under EP2, exact `0` disables, EP4 still refused), binary built on beta `21cc6f023` (YaRN + NPU round), defaults only: EP2 == single for greedy AR and MTP (p8k, 64 tokens) and the 4396-token tail-300 prompt; sampled MTP (T 0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, seed 7, two fresh processes) EP2 tokens, windows and state are identical to single and per-seed stable; serve think-sampled (lru_cache, thinking med, seed 42) and a thinking-on battery and chain complete with closed think blocks and non-empty answers (no runaway or attractor output). p8k prefill (3 fresh per arm, defaults): single 3,036 vs EP2 3,942 tok/s (1.298x, every EP2 run above the single median). Receipts: `fn-ep/coal/s1/RESULT.md` (default-on revision).
- **Flash-Next EP long-prefill expert staging (`HIPFIRE_EP_EXPERT_STAGE`, default on under EP2 when the owner-routed prefill is admitted, `=0` disables).** Stage each rank's own compact host experts through its last two resident layers without changing residency; full-table EP forwards never stage, and ranks quiesce restored donors before returning to peer weight reads. Charge both ranks' pinned donor mirrors against shared host RAM/GTT and keep decode row exchanges on host event waits independently of the staging stream. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EXPERT_STAGE` remains the pipeline kill switch; single-card and flag-off EP paths are unchanged.
- **Flash-Next S1/S2 code is rank-generic (EP2 and EP4); EP4 is refused at admission until its hardware cells run.** Rank-generic registration, owner tables and all-peer ready/done waits preserve the two-parity grouped-row lifetime; staging charges every rank's donor mirrors to one host. The model-independent PeaceMaker pull retains the 48-byte EP2 specialization and adds four owner-indexed sources for EP4.
- **Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism: balanced per-call expert ownership on host-mapped MoE layers (default on under EP; `HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS=0` is the kill switch).** On the trunk layers whose routed experts are read zero-copy from pinned host memory, the static `e % ranks` split gives each rank a different number of routed experts per call, so the faster rank waits for the other at every such layer. With it on (the default under EP; unset or any value other than `0`), each call deals its distinct routed experts to the ranks alternately in ascending expert id (identically on every rank, per-rank distinct counts differ by at most one): a new `OwnerAssign` MoE stage right after routing runs one 512-thread PeaceMaker kernel (`qwen4_ep_owner_assign`) that rewrites the rank's live gate/up and down pointer tables from the routed ids (an assigned expert keeps its real local or peer pinned address, every other entry names the rank's zero dummy expert), and the row exchange copies each expanded row from the rank that computed it (`EpSlotOwners::Explicit`, kernel `ep_gather_rows_by_slot_owner`). The routed kernels run unchanged on the unchanged ids, every expert is computed from its own bytes on exactly one rank and copied bit-for-bit, and the canonical combine is untouched, so the output is byte-identical to the static split and to one card by construction.
  - Scope: host-mapped trunk layers on one-row decode and few-row forwards (verify, replay, takeover, up to 8 rows). VRAM-resident layers, chunk forwards and the MTP head keep the static split. The assignment kernel is a certified PeaceMaker (builder-emitted) gfx1201 module, `qwen4_ep_owner_assign_pm_gfx1201` (embedded `kernels/qwen4_ep_owner_assign_pm_gfx1201.hxaco`, M7 obligation-free, one 512-thread workgroup, two barriers, output bytes equal to the host reference `qwen4_ep_owner_map`); there is no hipcc build of it and any other architecture refuses at launch (EP is gfx1201-only).
  - With `HIPFIRE_EP_BALANCED_OWNERS=0`, nothing is built, allocated or loaded (EP and single-card bytes unchanged): no extra stage is lowered, no live table or slot-owner buffer is allocated, and no kernel module is loaded; the `ep_gather_rows_by_owner` kernel and its callers are unchanged. Each rank logs `qwen4 EP rank r/n: balanced ownership on N host-mapped MoE layers` at attach when it is on.
  - Measured on EP2 (cards 0000:03:00.0 + 0000:c3:00.0, R9700), pinned Flash-Next XTS (sha256 `8b15b6fe…`). Greedy, `hipfire bench --runs 3 --warmups 2 --max-tokens 256`, 3 fresh processes per arm, one binary for both arms (only the variable differs). 19 host-mapped layers per rank under MTP.
    - MTP code (`humaneval_0`, md5 `5333a1f7…`): 80.6 → 84.1 tok/s (+4.3%).
    - MTP prose (`fiction_lighthouse`, md5 `d3768bd6…`): 63.7 → 66.1 (+3.8%).
    - AR code: 48.5 → 50.5 (+4.1%).
    - τ is unchanged. These are short outputs and decode tok/s, not end-to-end.
  - Exactness: `fn_ep_oracle`, EP2 with the variable on vs single card, same binary.
    - Byte-identical: AR logits and every rank's state at ctx 512 and 8,192.
    - Byte-identical: MoE stage dumps of all 48 layers, including the host-mapped ones.
    - Byte-identical: greedy and sampled MTP tokens, per-window results and state at ctx 512 and 8,192, with sampled per-seed stability across fresh processes and greedy MTP equal to AR.
    - Flag-off EP2 and EP-off single are byte-identical to the previous beta.
    - `scripts/redline_daemon_harness.py --qwen4 --pm4` passes on one card.
    - The EP2 native-MTP serve battery, chain and sampled-thinking turns finish cleanly.
- **MoE offload recording inventory synchronized (CI/tooling only).** Updates the Qwen35 placement-loader and shared MTP capture site names, inventories narrow MTP verify eligibility, the serve loader, and CPU expert/transfer capture guards, and classifies CPU-offload load refusals under `eager-only-refusals`. Restores the `railgun-cert` source-inventory gate without changing runtime behavior or weakening its checks.
- **Multi-slot embeddings: the arch-17 encoder (`pplx-embed-v2-late-0.6b`) serves concurrent `embed` requests as one batched forward (`serve.multi_slot`, no kill switch — the single-request path is the batch-of-one case).** The wire is unchanged: `hipfire embed` and `POST /v1/embeddings` produce the same `embedded` envelope whether the backend is the ordinary daemon or a multi-slot engine, so the CLI, the HTTP layer and the container image need no change to reach it. A batch is **row concatenation, not padding to a common length**: each sequence keeps its real length and is padded only to its own multiple of 64 (`padded_rows`), row blocks are laid back to back into one `[T, hidden]` tensor, and everything row-independent (all projections, both norms, the gates, mRoPE, the MLP, the Dense head) runs once over `T` rows. Only the two stateful mixers are per sequence, over contiguous row slices: full attention (bidirectional *within* a sequence — a segment's scores, softmax and `n_valid` key bound never include a neighbour's rows) and GatedDeltaNet (conv ring and delta-rule S matrix cleared at each segment's start, in *both* the conv pass and the later scan pass — they read the state in two separate passes, and clearing only in the first let segment *k* start from segment *k-1*'s state). No attention mask and no `[B·L, B·L]` score matrix exist on this route.
  - **Exactness: batched == serial, bit for bit** (`scripts/check_embed_multislot.py`, 7 inputs 7…2209 tokens incl. two committed fixtures, 4 and 8 slots: every input `max 0.000e+00`), because the forward is deterministic — the engine holds one weight copy and resets the GDN state per segment. Independently, the slot route against the NumPy oracle (`--oracle`) is max 4.6e-5…1.4e-4 abs, rel-L2 1.26e-4…3.61e-4, the same f16-weight-vs-f32-reference band the sequential route sits in. HTTP replies were checked the same way (`/v1/embeddings` vs the oracle: max 5.7e-5, rel 1.8e-4).
  - Batching trade: concurrent requests share one forward, so per-request latency inside a batch is higher than alone, and an isolated request pays the collector window (`HIPFIRE_EMBED_BATCH_WINDOW_US`, default 2 ms). A batch of mixed-length inputs is bounded by its longest segment. No throughput figure is claimed for this release.
  - The engine (`hipfire-arch-qwen35-embed/src/slot_engine.rs`) is deliberately not the qwen3.5 generation engine: no KV, no sessions, no swap, no scheduler. `SlotEngineHandle::encode` is a defaulted trait method (a generation engine returns `Err` and the daemon keeps the ordinary path), `n_slots` is its requests-per-forward cap and `max_batch_tokens` its row budget; the daemon spawns one worker per `embed` request so concurrent requests reach the engine together. Slot admission for this arch is carrier-delegated (`Carrier::preflight_slot_model`), because its geometry comes from HF/dir metadata and its checkpoint may be a safetensors directory, which the qwen35 HFQ preflight cannot open.
  - Two latent service-path bugs surfaced by making embeds concurrent, both fixed here: `/v1/embeddings` took the **exclusive** admission class unconditionally (correct for a single-tenant daemon, but on a multi-slot serve it serialized the very requests the engine batches — now the sharing class when the serve advertises `multi_slot`), and the client's reader routed `embedded` to its **single control slot** because the reply type was not classified (two concurrent embeds then both delivered into whichever request registered last, and every other one hung; the reply now carries `attempt_id: 0` and is routed by `(id, 0)`, which the `embed` caller registers). Regression tests: `route_lifecycle_embed_reply_is_keyed_at_attempt_zero`, and the `batch_parity` example (`--features lab`) for the forward alone.
  - **Adversarial hardening pass (arch 17 review follow-ups, all verified in-container on gfx1101).**
    - Image requests were encoding wrong positions: the mRoPE position builder got the POST-merge patch grid where it expects the PRE-merge grid (it divides by `spatial_merge_size` itself), and the merge factor was hardcoded `2` instead of the checkpoint's `vision.spatial_merge_size` — every image token got collapsed phases and every text token after the image was shifted. Spans now carry the pre-merge grid and the real merge size; a text+image request over the wire returns correct vectors.
    - The `HIPFIRE_EMBED_ATTN=naive`/`no-gemm` dev knobs left the padding keys unmasked for inputs ≥ 256 tokens (the eager-path pad puts zeroed rows past `n_valid`; the unmasked kernels gave them a softmax share and diluted every row). `naive` now passes an f32 key mask and the Q-tiled kernel takes `n_valid` as its key bound — daemon-wire A/B vs the eager path agrees at ~1e-4 on padded and unpadded inputs. The eager GEMM path is additionally gated to wave32-WMMA arches and `head_dim % 64 == 0`, falling back to the Q-tiled kernel instead of a JIT/assert failure.
    - Embeds concurrency wiring: `generate` against a resident arch-17 checkpoint now refuses with a keyed `emit_batch_admission_error` after the attempt id and admission token are bound (it was an attempt-0 error no generate caller routes — the request hung); the embed arm bounds thread creation before spawning like the generate arm; both overload refusals emit on the uncorrelated channel the embed caller actually waits on (`emit_active_attempt_error` was a dead write — TLS attempt 0); `Engine::embed` no longer claims the shared `control` slot (concurrent embeds raced it and could swallow a control verb's reply); `/v1/embeddings` errors now map overload → 429 and engine failures → 500 instead of a blanket 400.
    - `/v1/embeddings` reload hardening: a request-named model goes through `ModelOrigin::Request` (request policy applies — `allow_request_paths`/`allow_pull` gates are honored) and a request naming a different model than the resident one is refused instead of being silently answered by the resident checkpoint.
    - Robustness: malformed configs now fail with an error instead of panicking — zero `num_attention_heads`/`num_key_value_heads` (division by zero at parse and in the daemon's slot preflight), `linear_num_value_heads != linear_num_key_heads` (the delta-rule scan has no GQA interleave — such a checkpoint would have served corrupted output), `attn_output_gate: false` read as a JSON bool, and an explicit `is_causal: true` (this arch is bidirectional only). The 1_Dense sidecar safetensors reader bounds-checks the header length and `data_offsets` before indexing. `decode_f32`/vision `to_f32` byte-length-guard every dtype arm.
    - `names::release` (MADV_DONTNEED) now runs after decode, not inside `read` — releasing before the bytes were touched freed nothing and refaulted on the decode. l2_normalize sends non-finite rows to zero instead of letting NaN/inf reach the wire. `generate_embed` uses the shared `bundle.finalize_embeddings` the slot engine uses, so the two routes cannot drift on mask/normalize.
    - Slot engine: an `encode_embeddings_batch` failure in a multi-group batch errors that group's requests and continues instead of dropping already-computed groups and killing the engine; the all-failed path counts rejections per request, not per command.
    - Kernel hardening: `attention_encoder_qtiled` bounds its score loop by `n_valid` (padded keys excluded), the illegal-`k_tile` early return now writes zeros rather than leaving garbage, and the dead `attention_encoder_qtiled_k_tile` host helper with a wrong LDS formula is removed. `attention_rows_softmax_f16` sizes its reduction block to a power of two (its bit-halving tree is wrong otherwise).
    - Validation assets: `check_embed_multislot.py` asserts the daemon actually entered `experimental_multi_slot` (a silent sequential fallback would trivially pass bit-identity), drains daemon stderr concurrently (a verbose daemon could fill the pipe and deadlock the check), keys its report rows by input index (oracle metrics attach to the wrong row after any error), and the `--oracle` leg now gates at rel-L2 ≤ 1e-2 instead of printing advisory diffs. `check_embed_quant.py` selects the error band from the artifact's real format (HFQ tensor-index quant_type via astrea's parser, dir → raw band) rather than filename substrings, fails closed on unbanded formats, and adds a >256-token fixture that crosses into the eager attention path. `oracle_pplx_embed_hf.py` gates the Dense-head and hipfire legs (they printed but never failed the run). `examples/attn_eager_parity` runs the production op sequence (tile-dispatched GEMMs via shared `attention_lds_tile`/`attention_{qk,pv}_tiles`, masked `attention_rows_softmax_f16`, and an `n_valid < n` padded tail) instead of different kernels.
    - Verified in-container (Ubuntu 24.04 + ROCm 10.1, gfx1101, real checkpoint): daemon-wire `check_embed_multislot.py` 7/7 bit-identical (serial 311 ms → slot wall 240 ms, 1.30x); HTTP probe suite 20/20 (shapes, L2-normalized rows, kinds, skiplist, bit-identical batching, edge/error paths, 24+40-way concurrency); image path (text+image, image-only, bad base64 → 400, data-URL prefix) end-to-end; `check_embed_quant.py` on the raw dir within band (2.0e-4 vs 2.0e-3) including the new eager-path fixture; `HIPFIRE_EMBED_ATTN=naive` vs default attention agree at ~1e-4 on padded lengths.
  - **Maintainer hardening:** Qwen DFlash/MTP context fallback retains the Qwen AR body; the quantizer architecture override applies only to arch 17, preserving MoE arch 6 and fail-closed handling. The embedding gfx1201 route uses per-architecture optimized split1 pitched WMMA without head gathers; the existing gfx11 route is unchanged.
  - **Known caveats (0.4.2):** release validation for arch 17 is an embedding smoke against the committed text oracle on gfx1201, gfx1100 and gfx1151 (7 text cases, rel-L2 ≤ 3.8e-4); the image path has no external oracle; Q8 `.hfq` was checked on gfx1201 only (rel-L2 ≤ 9.3e-3). Generation models are byte-identical to beta on gfx1201 (serve battery + chain). The embedding path carries 9 recorded dispatch-bypass debt sites (`bypass_total` 357 → 366); `daemon_lines` 5393 → 5496 (+103 for the `embed` arm and the `generate` refusal of an embedding checkpoint, on top of the separate 5392 → 5393 raise).

- **Flash-Next (Qwen4) native MTP under expert parallelism.** `--spec mtp` / `speculation.mtp = on` now admits native MTP on an EP mesh (EP2, EP4); every rank holds its own drafter replica and speculative advances fan out across the mesh. `speculation.mtp = auto` under EP stays AR, so EP default and flag-off bytes are unchanged. Lanes and naive sampled mode (`HIPFIRE_MTP_SAMPLED_MODE=naive`) stay refused under EP.
  - Exactness on gfx1201 (R9700), pinned Flash-Next XTS (sha256 `8b15b6fe…`): the `fn_ep_oracle --spec mtp` state oracle passes single vs beta (17/17 compared), EP2 vs single (34/34) and EP4 vs single (52/52): greedy tokens, per-window results and trunk, head and drafter state at window boundaries on every rank. The EP4 serve battery with thinking passes.
  - Measured (3 fresh processes per arm, R9700s, greedy, route `mtp+ngram`, committed prompts): code (`humaneval_0`, 134 tokens) 44.4 tok/s on one card, 81.7 on EP2, 121.6 on EP4; prose (`fiction_lighthouse`, 44 tokens) 34.5 / 63.4 / 87.1 tok/s. Short outputs; decode tok/s, not end-to-end.
- **Flash-Next zab trunk-A4 tier ships as a `<model>.trunk-overlay` sidecar, applied by default on gfx1151 (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_OVERLAY`, default `auto`; `0` is the kill switch).** The client carries a compiled-in pin for the stock MQ4 XTS base (sha256 `8b15b6fe…`, 125,288,544,792 B; tags `qwen3.8:flash-next`, `-mq4`, `-gptq3`, `-mq4-xts`) naming `qwen3.8-flash-next.mq4-xts.trunk-overlay` in `hipfire-models/qwen3.8-flash-next` (305,543,168 B, sha256 `df49bc0a…66840fd`), which `hipfire pull` fetches and verifies next to the base (`rm` removes it; listings never show it as a model). There is no registry schema change: `registry/v1.json` is unchanged, so older clients keep parsing the fetched registry and simply do not fetch the overlay. The sidecar is an HFQM container holding the 108 GDN `linear_attn.in_proj_{z,a,b}` projections as the verified symmetric QT44 zab payloads (producer manifest sha256 `8d8bed9d…`), with a manifest that binds it to the stock base (file length, sha256 over header and index `8703bb16…`, recorded full-file sha256) and lists each payload's sha256. On gfx1151 the Qwen4 loader checks the binding, verifies every payload digest, substitutes the payloads for the base's MQ6 tensors through the same upload path as the `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_REQUANT …=qt44:<dir>` runtime map (449.3 → 305.5 MB resident), and arms the `gfx1151-zab-v1` trunk A4 default (`gdn.z,gdn.a,gdn.b`, ragged prefill rows included). Other architectures keep the base weights unless `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_OVERLAY=1`. A sidecar that does not bind (another base, a bad digest, a non-Flash-Next profile, an unknown default marker, a base that already carries one) is refused with one `REFUSED` line and the base weights run; an absent sidecar logs nothing. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_OVERLAY=0` restores the stock weights and route; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_IU4=0` keeps the QT44 weights with the A4 route off. `hipfire-quantize trunk-overlay` builds the file. The local-only baked `qwen3.8:flash-next-mq4-xts-zab` tier is unchanged and has no overlay.
  - Quality (the approved default tier; the overlay applies the same bytes as the runtime map and the baked artifact): S11 Top256×512 screen against the stock pin on gfx1151, WT2 0.10487 → 0.10994 (+0.0051) and code24 0.09891 → 0.10836 (+0.0094), both within the +0.015 cap; tied tasks (greedy, thinking off, paired against the stock route): HumanEval 159 vs 159, GSM8K-5shot 1,287 vs 1,286 (paired 95% CI −0.52 to +0.37 pp), MMLU-Pro 964 vs 970 (+0.43 pp, CI −0.52 to +1.38 pp), all noninferior at −1 pp. Receipts: `halo3000/zab-num-kld/summary.json`, `halo3000/zab-tasks/task-report.json` (sha256 `069d858b…`).
  - Exactness on gfx1151 (one binary, fresh processes, no tolerance): `qwen4_kld` kldseq with the overlay sidecar next to the stock base is byte-identical to the baked zab artifact on WT2 32×512 (sha256 `d10ebd32…`) and code24 24×512 (`359def70…`), and equal to the recorded runtime-map campaign. Greedy serve battery and the committed eight-turn coding chain (thinking off, MTP auto, seed 42) produce identical text with the overlay and with the baked artifact; with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_OVERLAY=0`, with the sidecar absent, and on the previous beta binary they are identical to each other (the stock tier).
- **Qwen3.5-family CPU offload: enabled MTP no longer needs `HIPFIRE_FORWARD_LOWERED=0`.** Single-token forwards automatically use the CPU-splice-capable executor when the loaded model owns CPU-executed host weights. This fixes MTP forced-token advances and terminal pending-seed flushes, and also covers single-row rollback replay and ordinary AR decode. Device-resident and PCIe-offloaded models retain the lowered executor; its host-read safety refusal and the existing MTP `auto` admission policy are unchanged.
- **Qwen35 DFlash cold prefill keeps the target hidden rows on device (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_PREFILL_DEVICE`, default on; `0` restores the per-piece host capture).** The prompt seed no longer stops after every ring-sized (2,048-row) trunk piece to download each extraction layer, interleave the rows on the CPU and append them to a full-prompt host shadow, unless the draft's long-reach backfill reads that shadow (a windowed draft with `w < w_full` and a prompt longer than `w`; `draft_seed_backfill_reads_host`). The paired all-sliding DFlash2 draft (the 27B default) and Legacy-mode drafts have no such reader: the unchanged hidden ring feeds the exact pitched prompt scatter, and decode (chain, tree, VMM lane) already reads only the device-resident draft context (`ctx_slice=None`). The unused persistent host shadow (`DflashState::target_hidden_host`, up to `ctx × dim` floats per state) is removed. No trunk, projection, attention, sampling or kernel arithmetic changes; piece boundaries are unchanged.
  - Exactness on gfx1201: the prompt hidden-state byte oracle runs in three fresh arms (both DFlash prefill switches `0`, `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_PREFILL_DEVICE=0`, default) over 19 cases, including six piece-capture cases that require the default to capture exactly when the long-reach backfill reads the shadow and check every captured host byte against an independent CPU mapping; the three artifacts are byte-identical. Greedy serve battery/chain and the committed 8.2K and 21.6K prompts, the sampled seed-42 battery in two fresh default processes, and medium-effort sampled thinking (10,813 tokens, naturally closed reasoning) are byte-identical to both switches `0`.
  - Measured **DFlash default** (paired draft present, empty config), Qwen3.8-27B XTS on one R9700, one binary, 3 fresh interleaved serve processes per arm, committed prompts, thinking off, one output token. Client TTFT medians with both switches `0` → default: 691 → 582 ms at 2.1K, 2,146 → 1,662 ms at 8.2K (prompt 3,809 → 4,921 tok/s, +29%), 5,996 → 4,691 ms at 21.6K and 8,115 → 6,403 ms at 28.5K; the paired sequential screen resolves a win at every length (8.2K CS95 +21.7% to +39.6%). Same binary and hold: spec off 685 / 1,653 / 4,714 / 6,346 ms and MTP auto 711 / 1,793 / 4,942 / 6,657 ms. DFlash default prefill is now faster than MTP auto at every length and within 1% of spec off (faster at 2.1K and 21.6K, +0.5% at 8.2K, +0.9% at 28.5K).
- **Qwen35 DFlash prompt hidden scatter uses stream-ordered pitched copies (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_PREFILL_SCATTER_FAST`, default on; `0` restores per-row copies).** Cold and cached-suffix prompt fills copy each retained extraction-layer span into the draft's interleaved target-hidden state with one `hipMemcpy2DAsync` per ring-contiguous span, splitting at both ring boundaries, instead of a synchronous memcpy for every row and extraction layer. The exact byte mapping, untouched rows, source state, draft arithmetic and decode/terminal-repair routes are unchanged; no new kernel is introduced.
  - Exactness on gfx1201: the prompt scatter oracle compares every draft target-hidden destination byte and every source-ring byte to an independent CPU mapping in both fresh switch arms (13 cases, cold/suffix offsets, ring wraps, overwritten prefixes, untouched rows and arbitrary F32 bit patterns). Greedy serve battery/chain and two committed long prompts, sampled seed-42 battery in two fresh fast processes, and sampled medium-effort long thinking (10,813 tokens, naturally closed reasoning) are byte-identical to `=0`.
  - Measured **DFlash default** (paired draft present, empty config), Qwen3.8-27B XTS on one R9700, 3 fresh interleaved serve processes per arm, committed prompts, thinking off, one output token: client TTFT medians with `=0` → default are 657 → 664 ms at 2.1K, 2,019 → 2,006 ms at 8.2K, 5,935 → 5,910 ms at 21.6K, and 7,857 → 7,827 ms at 28.5K. The 8K prompt tok/s median improves 0.6%; this is **not a statistically resolved ±1% TTFT win**, and the short-prompt median is 1.2% slower. No MTP result is attributed to this change.
- **Engine-level YaRN RoPE scaling; Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) can now run past its native 262,144-token context (#856 by @fivetide).** A family now declares its RoPE as a `rdna_compute::rope::Rope` (base + scaling) and passes it through. The engine (`hipfire_runtime::rope::rope_scaling`) reads the checkpoint's `rope_parameters` / legacy `rope_scaling`: `rope_type: "yarn"` with HF defaults (`beta_fast` 32, `beta_slow` 1, `attention_factor = 0.1 ln(factor) + 1` or the `mscale` pair, absent `factor` = `max_position_embeddings / original`); unknown rope types are refused. `Rope::yarn(rotary_dim)` lowers it to one by-value kernel argument (`kernels/src/rope_yarn.h`: the NTK-by-parts ramp plus the cos/sin factor), so no family implements YaRN itself. The new `attention.yarn_factor` (`HIPFIRE_YARN_FACTOR`, default null) applies YaRN to an HFQ whose embedded config says `default`, the same as the model card's `rope_parameters` edit for vLLM/SGLang; `1` turns a checkpoint's YaRN off. Qwen4 passes its `Rope` to every QSA rotation (queries, keys, indexer queries, pooled index keys; trunk and native MTP head), and admission bounds `max_seq` by the RoPE context (`factor x original`; `QWEN4_MAX_CONTEXT` is now the 1,048,576 `memory.max_seq` ceiling), as does `hipfire bench`'s declared-context check. Unscaled launches take the identity branch, so outputs without YaRN are unchanged. Static YaRN changes short-context outputs too; set it only for long-context loads. Other families still ignore `rope_scaling` and the override.
  - A YaRN `factor` below 1 is refused (it would invert the correction ramp and shrink the context). The Qwen4 host QSA references (`ops::rope_prefix_halfsplit` / `qsa_pool_block`, the compact reference forward and MTP head, the `qwen4_qsa_ctx` pool check) take the same `Rope` and follow the kernels' f32 operation order (`RopeYarn::sin_cos`), so a host-vs-GPU parity run under YaRN compares like with like. Both match HF within f32 rounding, not bit for bit: HF scales `inv_freq` before multiplying by the position.
  - MEASURED on gfx1151 (Strix Halo), `qwen3.8-flash-next-gptq3.mq4`, greedy, `yarn_factor = 4`, `max_seq 524288`: a passphrase needle in a 499,207-token prompt is retrieved at 90 % depth (needle past position 449K) and at 10 % depth (about 449K tokens from the question); prefill about 460 s per request. Without YaRN, 3 short-to-8K prompts give byte-identical output to beta.
  - The Strix Halo NPU-trunk prefill route rotates the same way: its BF16-arena QSA norm/RoPE twin (`indexed_attention_norm_rope_batch_arena`) takes the same `RopeYarn`. It replaces beta's unreleased Qwen4-only `memory.rope_yarn_factor` / `HIPFIRE_ROPE_YARN_FACTOR`, which is removed; use `attention.yarn_factor`.
  - Exactness on beta `a55d72ba3` without YaRN, one binary pair built the same way:
    - gfx1151 (PerfOpt kernel, default config): the `qwen4_kld` WT2 and code24 kldseq files are byte-identical to beta (sha256 `4baf96fc…`, `497d59c5…`).
    - gfx1151: NPU `on` == `emulate` greedy at p16k, including a session-cache hit (8,192 cached tokens).
    - gfx1151: the BF16-arena consumer oracle is byte-exact (62/62), with a new YaRN norm/RoPE arm.
    - gfx1201: the greedy serve battery (MTP on) is byte-identical to beta.
    - The arena twin equals the incumbent launch bit for bit, plain and with YaRN factor 4 at position 300,003 (GPU test on gfx1201).
  - With `HIPFIRE_YARN_FACTOR=4` on gfx1201, 4 of the 5 short battery answers change wording, and every answer ends cleanly (static YaRN at short context).
- **Linux HIP initialization: process-wide pageable weight-copy staging on all Linux systems, not architecture-restricted.** Before runtime loading, an unset `GPU_PINNED_MIN_XFER_SIZE` now defaults to `100000` on every load, including `serve --no-prewarm`. Beta's Qwen4 host-mapping `HSA_USERPTR_FOR_PAGED_MEM=0` scope remains unchanged. Pre-set operator values are respected; set `HIPFIRE_PINNED_XFER_DEFAULT=0` before process start for the exact opt-out: `GPU_PINNED_MIN_XFER_SIZE` is then left unset and clr keeps its own default (one `[hip-bridge] HIPFIRE_PINNED_XFER_DEFAULT=0: GPU_PINNED_MIN_XFER_SIZE left unset (clr default)` line is logged). `GPU_PINNED_MIN_XFER_SIZE=0` is not an opt-out: it pins every pageable copy source in place, including copies below clr's built-in threshold, and does **not** restore the previous unset CLR default.
- **Flash-Next (native Qwen4 MTP) on gfx1151 now defaults the MTP draft cap to 4.** `speculation.mtp_k` / `HIPFIRE_MTP_K` default is `auto` (was the integer `3`): `auto` resolves to 4 for native Qwen4 MTP (arch 16) on gfx1151 and to 3 for every other model and GPU, including Flash-Next on every other arch. An explicit 1–10 (env, config, or `mtp_k` load param) overrides on every model; `HIPFIRE_MTP_K=3` (or `speculation.mtp_k = 3`) restores the previous cap. Greedy output is byte-identical to cap 3 on the measured prompts. MEASURED on gfx1151 (Strix Halo), pinned Flash-Next XTS, 3 fresh serve processes per arm, untraced, thinking off: code geomean (Rust/Go/Java) 67.9 → 70.5 tok/s (+3.8%; Rust 69.0 → 73.9, Go 67.7 → 69.7, Java 67.1 → 68.1); a separate env-only replicate on the previous build gave 67.9 → 70.1 (+3.2%). 9K prose 45.9 → 46.5, short prose unchanged (52.4), a 1,200-token count-up 69.4 → 77.0. Sampled thinking requests close `</think>` at both caps. Sampled native MTP output is not seed-stable across fresh processes at either cap (the adaptive verify-route choice uses measured wall time); this is unchanged by the new default.
- **gfx1201 native-fp8 prefill: ragged widened chunks now route through the fp8q Q-resident attention (`HIPFIRE_FA_RAGGED_PACKET=0` is the developer kill switch).** Ordinary widened prefill chunks (`512 < n <= 32768`) whose tail `n % 512` is 64..=511 rows previously took the F32 prep and per-segment attention. With full fp8q eligibility (fused prep, fp8q prep, Q-resident and Q-resident v2 attention) and `start_pos + n == max_ctx_len <= physical_cap`, they now write K/V for all `n` rows once and attend the whole-512-row prefix and the tail as two range launches over the single two-plane Raw Q, into the whole-`n` F32 output that the existing sigmoid output producer consumes. Such chunks do not use the A4 attention epilogue. `=0` restores the previous admission exactly; aligned chunks, tails below 64 rows, other architectures, tiers, independent/tree/verify modes are unchanged. Host-side routing only; the kernel is the existing Q8-F32 Q-resident v2 kernel with plane offsets added to its launcher.
  - The plain q8 Q-resident v2 attention module and the `_gil_` slab sigmoid producer are now in the gfx1201 corpus and the 27B gfx1201 AR route table. Without the q8 module, `hipfire serve` refused the ragged route's post-ready module load ("outside this load's planned kernel route") and every admitted request failed; the same registration also lets `HIPFIRE_A4_ATTN_EPI=0` load on this route.
  - Measured on one R9700 (gfx1201), Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts (sha256 `3e38ccba…f6f8ae`), one binary with `=0` vs default, fresh processes interleaved. `hipfire bench --pp N` (synthetic ids, speculation off, 3 processes × 5 runs per arm): 5,943 rows 5,003 → 5,093 tok/s (+1.8%), 8,030 rows 4,958 → 5,041 (+1.7%), 8,177 rows 4,972 → 5,048 (+1.5%); all 9 pairs positive. Serve (speculation off, committed prompts with a per-request nonce, server `prefill_ms`, 4 processes × 6 passes per arm): `ttft_5900` (5,943 tokens) 1,186.0 → 1,166.3 ms (−1.7%), `deepseek4_mq2r_prose_ctx8192` (8,176 tokens) 1,648.0 → 1,620.8 ms (−1.7%). A prompt whose final-chunk tail is under 64 rows (`ds4-gfx942-ar-2048`, 2,088 tokens) is not admitted and is unchanged; at 21.6K and 28.5K tokens the difference is inside run-to-run noise (−0.7% / +0.8%, 3 processes).
  - Exactness: the `prefill_ragged_oracle` example (logits, final hidden and decode tokens) is identical with the switch on and off for 577, 1,962, 2,054, 8,030, 8,059, 8,064, 16,059, 32,055 and 8,192 rows on both the IU4 and FP8 routes (18 of 18). `serve_harness` greedy battery and greedy on three committed long prompts (5.9K, 8.2K and 21.6K tokens; final-chunk tails 311, 495 and 112 rows) are byte-identical on vs off.
- **Flash-Next trunk A4 (zab) now arms on ragged prefill chunks.** For >=512 rows not divisible by 128, the Z|beta|alpha projection pads to the V2B 256-row tile with zeroed rows, written in place in full-capacity scratch. Real rows are byte-identical to the aligned IU4 route; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_IU4_RAGGED=0` restores the previous exact-activation fallback for those chunks. MEASURED on gfx1151 (baked zab artifact, 8,186-token prompt that previously armed no A4 dispatch; 3 fresh processes x 5 client-TTFT runs per arm): TTFT 4,201 → 4,114 ms (−87 ms, −2.1%; 3/3 process pairs faster) versus `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_IU4_RAGGED=0`. Hardware oracle: Z/beta/alpha real rows are bitwise identical to the aligned 8,192-row IU4 route for 513–8,191 rows (synthetic and captured activations, GDN layers 0 and 46), padded rows are zero, and nothing is written past the padded tile.
- **Qwen35 MTP fast prompt fill: exact PeaceMaker MQ4-v1 GEMM on gfx1201 (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_PM_GEMM`, default on; `0` restores the incumbent).** Only eh_proj/wk/wv change, including shared-rotation wv; four weight rows share each activation load while preserving the incumbent f32 dequant, dot order, single accumulator and reduction tree. The kernel is preplanned for the native-MTP load, so serve does not need a post-ready module load. Synthetic guarded byte oracle: all 20 shapes identical (including every production slice size and non-multiple-of-four output rows). Card C, one correct-class process, HIP-event median of 20 launches: `(M,K,N)=(5120,10240,1688)` 130.92 → 27.07 ms; `(1024,5120,1688)` 11.33 → 2.35 ms. These are kernel timings, not serve TTFT measurements; non-MQ4-v1 weights, other callers and architectures retain their existing routes.
  - End-to-end PM on/off (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_FAST=1` in both processes): `mtp_fill_oracle_compare` reports `identical=true failures=0` for reset, head K/V, predecessor hidden, trunk logits, greedy/sampled continuations and warm suffixes through 16,059 tokens. Receipts: `/home/kaden/qcal/release-0.4.2/prefill6k/mtp-fill-pm/`.
  - Measured on one R9700 (gfx1201), Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts (sha256 `3e38ccba…f6f8ae`), default serve configuration with PM `=0` vs default on the same rebased binary, 3 fresh processes per arm interleaved × 2 uncached nonce-bearing passes, thinking off, `max_tokens` 1. Client TTFT / prompt tok/s medians: `ds4-gfx942-ar-2048` 756 → 705 ms / 2,761 → 2,958 tok/s (+7.1%); `ttft_5900` 1,382 → 1,267 ms / 4,297 → 4,689 (+9.1%); `deepseek4_mq2r_prose_ctx8192` 1,910 → 1,750 ms / 4,279 → 4,673 (+9.2%); `qwen38_issue693_longcode_20676` 5,326 → 4,896 ms / 4,058 → 4,414 (+8.8%); `prose-32k` 7,227 → 6,648 ms / 3,949 → 4,292 (+8.7%). All five arm ranges are disjoint. Prediction P0220 (1,777 ms for the 8K prompt) resolved at 1,749.74 ms (−1.53%, within its band). Timing receipts: `prefill6k/runs-ab/mtpfill-pm2/summary.json`.
  - Rebased product gates (binary source commit `2db50ff02`, daemon md5 `064b09f8…`, CLI `8484da3f…`): PM on/off fill oracle is identical (`failures=0`); `serve_harness` greedy battery and chain, sampled battery (registry sampling, seed 42; two fresh PM-on processes), and greedy on the committed 5.9K/20.7K prompts all print `transcript_byte_identical=true`. Long sampled thinking (medium effort, seed 42, fixed nonce, 16K cap) gives identical PM on/off response bytes (10,909 tokens, `</think>` closed as witnessed by the reasoning→answer SSE transition, `finish=stop`, text md5 `ce2d8fcb…`); the AR reference also closes and stops (10,900 tokens). Every cell verifies card C's UUID/BDF; no post-ready planned-route module refusal occurs. Receipts: `prefill6k/mtp-fill-pm/rb/` and `prefill6k/mtp-fill-pm/gates/`.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) on gfx1151: opt-in persistent per-layer F16 QSA K/V arena for prefill attend (BF-1, exact).** Opt-in (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_QSA_F16_ARENA=1`) for F32 QSA K/V with head_dim 256; the default keeps the existing per-chunk whole-prefix convert. Reuses the existing converter and gathered WMMA attend, converting only new/invalid rows; decode, MTP verify and the MTP head keep their F32 route. Both VMM and legacy storage account the arena in state/admission bytes, and rollback, reset and prefix restore guard its validity. Includes CPU watermark/sizing tests and a fresh-process byte oracle over real prompt attention, state, logits and greedy ids, including rollback-then-continue. MEASURED on gfx1151 (pinned Flash-Next XTS): hardware oracle with fresh arena-on and arena-off processes gives identical attention per QSA layer, selections, state, logits and greedy ids at 16,343 tokens (VMM and legacy KV backends) and 57,544 tokens (VMM), including snapshot-restore watermark clamps, reset and prefix-cache restore; serve battery and the eight-turn chain (with prefix-cache reuse) are byte-identical across arms. Uncached client TTFT (3 fresh processes per arm): 57,544 tokens 34,070 → 34,017 ms (−54 ms, within noise); 212,010 tokens 134.63 → 133.68 s (−0.95 s, −0.7%, all three process pairs faster but the paired confidence sequence still includes zero), below the projected −2.0…−2.4 s, so the arena stays opt-in.
- **PeaceMaker ISA coverage: gfx11/gfx12 opcode fill, pending numerical qualification.** Adds all 59 gfx11 and 37 gfx12 missing hipcc-emitted encoding identities, plus the requested Mesa-known BF16 WMMA, packed dots and gfx12 sparse WMMA alternatives: 64 new gfx11 identities (61 base rows and three gfx1151-only scalar-float deltas) and 51 new gfx12 identities. Each sample is checked against pinned Mesa opcode registration and ROCm assembly/disassembly; codec regression tests cover every added row. CPU emulation extends scalar/vector arithmetic, comparisons, wide conversions and memory operations. New numerical modes remain unavailable until their hardware comparisons pass; table/codec availability is not a numerical qualification claim. Existing committed kernel bundles and pinned kernel sources are unchanged.
- **Qwen35 MTP prompt fill: fast fill route (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FILL_FAST`, default on; `0` restores the prior fill).** The MTP head's KV-only prompt Append no longer runs a synchronous per-row concat memcpy (2n copies) or the unused Q branch, residual included. It writes K/V with two same-stream pitched D2D copies, runs the identical K-only RoPE kernel with `n_heads_q = 0`, and reuses the FWHT-rotated activation for eligible MQ4G256 wk/wv without AWQ. Fill preparation zeroes the head's KV buffers on device with ordered `hipMemsetD32` instead of host vectors plus H2D copies. On eligible gfx1201 Q8-KV heads, fill-only scratch borrows inactive fields and head slices grow (default 512 → 1,688 rows; 384 → 1,264), capped so scratch stays within the previous full scratch plus rotation bytes. The scalar GEMM kernel is unchanged, so this does not claim GEMM acceleration. Prompt fill only; native decode and the MTP auto sidecar/AR prompt route are unchanged.
  - Measured on one R9700 (gfx1201), Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts (sha256 `3e38ccba…f6f8ae`), default serve config (MTP `auto`; no paired DFlash draft next to the model), one binary with `=0` vs default, 3 fresh `hipfire serve` processes per arm interleaved, committed prompts with a per-request nonce, thinking off, `max_tokens` 1, client TTFT medians: `ds4-gfx942-ar-2048` (2,086 tokens) 835 → 752 ms (+11.0% prompt tok/s); `ttft_5900` (5,941) 1,617 → 1,429 ms (+13.1%); `deepseek4_mq2r_prose_ctx8192` (8,176) 2,317 → 1,951 ms (+18.7%); `qwen38_issue693_longcode_20676` (21,615) 6,113 → 5,379 ms (+13.7%); `mtpfloor/prose-32k` (28,536) 8,253 → 7,274 ms (+13.5%). Every arm range is disjoint. Speculation off on the same prompts (the fill-free floor): 693 / 1,241 / 1,697 / 4,710 / 6,371 ms; the remaining gap is mostly the head's eh_proj and wk/wv `gemm_hfq4g256` launches.
  - Exactness: `mtp_fill_oracle_spawn` (cold fills of 1–16,059 rows, warm suffix fills, the reset poison proof, greedy and sampled K = 3 generation; 55 rows) is identical between `=0` and default. `serve_harness` greedy battery and chain, the sampled battery (registry sampling, seed 42) in two fresh fast processes, and greedy on two committed long prompts (5.9K, 20.7K tokens) are byte-identical to `=0`. Sampled long thinking (c1bench 1.9K Rust spec, medium effort, seed 42, fixed nonce): fast and `=0` produce identical bytes (10,909 tokens, `</think>` closed, `finish=stop`), as does AR with speculation off on its own text (10,900 tokens, closed, `finish=stop`).
- **Flash-Next zab XTS artifact: trunk A4 is the artifact-scoped default on exact gfx1151.** HFQM metadata `qwen4_trunk_iu4_default=gfx1151-zab-v1` selects `gdn.z,gdn.a,gdn.b`; only its 108 GDN z/a/b weights are replaced by symmetric QT44 payloads. Stock registry aliases and other architectures are unchanged. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TRUNK_IU4=0` disables A4 activations but keeps the baked QT44 weights; load the stock XTS artifact for byte-exact rollback. The named zab tier is staged locally only (no Hugging Face upload); canonical promotion remains gated separately.
- **`hipfire ple rebind`: safely reuse q4 PLE payloads after a monolithic base is repacked.** The command proves the old/new embedded PLE descriptors and Q8 bytes are unchanged, validates the source q4 segment digests, and writes the new base-size/index binding into a separately copied sidecar. Readback and attachment verification precede atomic replacement of `<new-base>.ple-q4`; the stock source sidecar is never modified. This supports the zab artifact without bypassing the loader's base-binding guard or requantizing the PLE rows.
- **Flash-Next (native Qwen4 MTP) on gfx1151: the MTP *prompt fill*'s head Append runs all seven projections through fixed-row native PM F16 WMMA kernels by default (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_APPEND_DET`, on unless `0`; exact gfx1151 only; prompt fill only).** The seven projections are `fc_embedding`, `fc_hidden` (`rows * hc_count` rows), HC down, HC up (`gemm_bf16_xf32_mtp_dense_det_pm`, the `mtp_dense_det_pm_gfx1151_w8` kernel that `peacemaker-isa emit --kernel mtp_dense_det_gfx11` builds: `Y[N,M] = X16[N,K] . W16[M,K]^T`, F32 output, 128 channels x 256 rows per 256-thread workgroup, 40,960 B static LDS, `--co` M7 certified) and the Q8_0 index, K and V projections (`mtp_q8_det_pm_launch`, after one shared F32 to F16 conversion of the mixed row). Each output is one ascending-K16 WMMA chain with F32 accumulate (no split-K, padding- and tiling-independent), so a row's bytes do not depend on its batch or sub-chunk size. HC down is the raw WMMA followed by the existing in-place `hc_activation_fused_f32` (scale `1/hc_count`) instead of the K4 GEMV epilogue. `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_APPEND_DET=0` is the kill switch and restores the incumbent kernels. On any other arch (including gfx1150, gfx1152 and gfx1201), or on a model whose head does not support the batched Append, every route, kernel and byte is unchanged whatever the flag says.
  - **Measured, isolated kill:** on gfx1151 (0000:c0:00.0), `fc_hidden` M2560 K2560 N4096: incumbent `gemm_bf16_xf32_multirow` 10,362.894 us vs the native DET path including its X conversion 1,988.869 us, median paired 5.200x (the >= 5x gate). The F16 oracle's full bytes, guards, conversions and row invariance pass.
  - **Measured, model (Strix Halo gfx1151, kernel 7.3-rc7 + PerfOpt, IOMMU on, NPU alive; Flash-Next XTS md5 `be007fc3…`; one binary, DET vs incumbent kernels, 3 fresh `hipfire serve` processes per arm interleaved, greedy, 256 output tokens, `cached_tokens=0`):** TTFT on committed real-text prompts p16k (16,343 tokens) 8,067.5 → 7,932.5 ms (−1.67%, prefill 2,025.8 → 2,060.3 tok/s) and p9k3 (9,319 tokens) 4,793.2 → 4,659.4 ms (−2.79%, 1,944.2 → 2,000.0 tok/s); every arm range is disjoint (repeat spread ≤ 0.20%). Decode on the same requests 48.5–48.7 → 48.8–48.9 tok/s (not worse). Greedy output is byte-identical to the incumbent on all 5 regress prompts × 3 fresh processes per arm and in every timing cell. MTP τ: 9.3K prose 1.68 → 1.66 (deterministic), java 3.05 → 3.11, go/rust/short unchanged. Batched-fill state invariance (Q8 oracle, DET0/DET1) passes.
  - **Numerics are explicitly not the incumbent's and not byte-equal to it.** The G1 byte-identity of the batched fill (against the per-row Append) governs the incumbent kernels only (gfx1201, and gfx1151 with `0`). DET converts BF16 weights through a model-lifetime F16 shadow (BF16 widened to F32, rounded to F16 RNE, once) and X from F32 to F16 RNE on every call; F16's narrower exponent range can underflow or overflow values BF16/F32 hold, and the gfx11 WMMA's internal dot order is its own. The head's QSA rows, draft state and pending hidden therefore differ from the incumbent's bytes (deterministic run to run); session snapshots carry whichever arithmetic built them. Target outputs are unaffected (speculative verification); only the draft head's acceptance moves (τ above).
  - **Prompt fill only.** The decode takeover step's head fill (`append_rows` is also called from `mtp_takeover_step`) is an explicit `DecodeFill` context and always runs the incumbent kernels; every caller states the context, it is never inferred from the hidden row or options.
  - **Lone prompt row and `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL=0`.** Under DET a one-row prompt tail takes the same rows kernels instead of the per-row `append_token`. The batched-fill scratch is allocated and charged in admission under DET even with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL=0`; there the prompt fill runs one row per pass, which row invariance makes byte-equal to the batched passes. The admission predicate `native_mtp_batched_fill` is renamed `native_mtp_fill_scratch` (no alias; loader callers migrated).
- **Qwen3.8 and Ornith 1.5 now default to `medium` reasoning effort when none is set.** Previously the bundled registry cards (and so unset/`auto` effort) supplied `xhigh`; all thirteen `qwen3.8:27b*` tiers and both `ornith-1.5:35b-a3b*` artifacts (including their `general` profiles) now supply `medium` instead. The official templates and loader adapters are unchanged, so with no effort at all they still default to `xhigh`. `auto` means no preference: an explicit non-`auto` value from user config, per-model config, CLI, or HTTP `reasoning_effort` / `reasoning.effort` always wins, and thinking off still drops effort. To restore the previous behavior, set `reasoning_effort = "xhigh"` in config or send `"reasoning_effort": "xhigh"` on the request. No think-token cap is implied: the span stays uncapped unless `max_think_tokens` is set. Other Qwen contracts, DeepSeek V4, Muse Glimmer, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4), draft/sidecar cards, and the explicit Ornith `coding` (`xhigh`) and `instruct` (`none`) profiles are unchanged.
- **Qwen4 long-context all-row logits no longer exceed the pinned PM head's addressable output range.** The gfx1151 MQ4/MQ6 PM kernels use a 2 GiB raw-buffer descriptor and 32-bit byte offsets; a single 8,192-row, 248,320-token-vocabulary head silently left scored logits unwritten. The all-row head now projects contiguous descriptor-bounded row tiles without changing trunk prefill chunks, context, or scoring windows. A checked launch guard refuses oversized PM outputs instead of silently dropping stores (also covering the affected gfx1201 PM entry, region outputs, and MQ4 HC streams). The symmetric PM MoE down launcher also refuses BF16 grouped outputs reaching 4 GiB, where its separate unsigned byte-offset addressing would wrap; default 8,192-row prefill remains admitted. Final-row serving and ordinary small verify heads retain their existing path. PM kernel bytes are unchanged. CPU boundary/tiling regressions and opt-in large-output hardware tests cover the cutover.

- **Windows installer detects named Radeon iGPUs.** Phoenix/Hawk Point (780M/760M/740M), Strix Point (890M/880M), Strix Halo (8060S/8050S/8040S), and Krackan Point (860M/840M/820M) now map to their gfx architecture. Detection does not certify hardware support: gfx1103/gfx1150/gfx1152 still require local hipcc, while gfx1151 is admitted for release kernel packs. The fallback prompt distinguishes name mappings from shipped packs; discrete-GPU detection is unchanged.
- **Radiowave Windows HIP SDK tool lookup:** inspection now probes the host executable suffix for `llvm-objdump`, `llvm-readobj` and `clang-offload-bundler` in each SDK directory before falling back to `PATH`. Explicit tool overrides and Linux probe order are unchanged. Document the historical `HIPFIRE_TEST_ROCM_LLVM_BIN` harness spelling and regenerate lifecycle inventory; current oracle tests no longer read it.
- **ISA M7 test evidence is portable (test-only).** The xbatch, residual-xbatch, MQ6 lane-row and Q8 lane-row tests write assembly and code objects to temporary directories and remove them after certification instead of requiring `/home/kaden/qcal`. PR850's registry part is superseded by `5c95f78d3`; beta's draft-excluding vision-tier test is retained unchanged. No runtime defaults change.
- **Tooling: `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen4/reference_oracle/kld_teacher.py`, the source-precision Qwen4 KLD teacher that `qwen4_kld import` reads.** It runs the pinned upstream operators (BF16 weights, F32 activations) layer-major over a local copy of the pinned checkpoint, which is refused unless its index and config match the pinned revision, and writes raw F32 logits for an HFKLDR v3 reference's scored window. KLD then covers expert quantization too; an engine teacher (e.g. a BF16-trunk HFQM) still runs MQ4 experts and measures trunk, head and PLE damage only. On gfx1151 (WikiText-2, 32 × 512, window [256, 511)) the teacher PPL is 5.4541, at about 62 min per reference, bound by NFS bandwidth.
- **Device-mesh fixture lock and route-oracle tests repinned to the canonical `qwen3.8:27b-mq4-xts` (test/harness only; no production change).** `scripts/device-mesh/matrix.json` `fixture_lock`, `route_oracle_mesh.rs` and `route_oracle_single.rs` still pinned the superseded 14,980,361,216-byte upload (`9f91556f…`), so on any host holding the registry artifact the matrix filed `hardware-blocked` on a sha256 mismatch and the oracles refused the fixture. They now pin `qwen3.8-27b.mq4-xts` at size 14,987,185,152 and sha256 `3e38ccbae3776470eb5a89344d300e9279d6b9ab6c31fd40ca1758c4f7c6f8ae` (as `registry/v1.json` lists for `qwen3.8:27b-mq4-xts`), with the Hugging Face source pinned to the commit (`aa80d2cf…`) that last wrote that file. Every dense matrix row (fixture tags, `$MODELS_DIR` paths, including the `HIPFIRE_PP_RESET_MODEL`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_RESET_MODEL`, `HIPFIRE_QWEN35_FIXTURE` and `HIPFIRE_VISION_FIXTURE` envs) and both oracles' default fixture paths use that file name, and the lock note no longer tells operators to avoid `hipfire pull` for it.
- **Qwen3.6 MoE single-route oracle (test/harness-only; no production change).** Adds the production `qwen3.6:35b-a3b-mq4r` fixture to the fresh-load, bitwise single-route oracle via `HIPFIRE_QWEN36_MOE_FIXTURE`. The `g1-route-oracles-nonllama` harness now requires three passing tests and the `g1-single-oracle[moe-qwen3.6]: PASS` marker, and forbids `skip:` output.
- **Flash-Next sampled MTP: resident target rows and ordered Leviathan acceptance (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DEVICE_ACCEPT`).**
  - Builds target distributions directly from resident verify logits with the parallel CPU-AR row builder, uploads the existing host draft distributions (or n-gram point masses) through reusable staging, and downloads only the two-word (8-byte) acceptance result: once per batched window, once per visited incremental row. Native batched and incremental verification and n-gram takeover retain their target/head transaction conventions.
  - Eligibility: sampled Leviathan, neutral penalties (no history window or penalty prepass), positive finite temperature, exact gfx1151 or gfx1201 with device sampler support, at least one draft, pool `1..=64` and cap `1..=pool`. Greedy, naive, penalized, target-only AR-floor and unsupported requests keep the host path and are not device coverage.
  - Opt-in (default off): `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DEVICE_ACCEPT=1` enables it; unset keeps the existing host target-row and acceptance path.
