End-to-end A/B on gfx1201 (one binary, `=1` vs unset, 4 fresh processes per arm interleaved, 3 committed prompts × 2 seeds, 512 tokens, T 0.7 / top-p 0.95 / top-k 20): decode 38.61 vs 37.97 tok/s, `predict ab` +1.83 % (CS95 −2.76 % … +6.63 %), a tie with the host path; greedy bytes identical in all 10 processes. Not default: in the default config (AR floor and n-gram takeover on) sampled bytes per seed are not stable across fresh processes on either path, so the per-seed criterion is blocked by the pre-existing timing-dependent routing.
  - The enabled configuration uses a new canonical target law: higher logits first, equal logits to the lower token id, fixed-tree sums; it is not byte-equal to the host target law. Ordered acceptance retains the host accept law and exact `SpecRng` chronology: draft draws stay in place, verdict/residual/bonus uniforms come from a clone, and only consumed draws advance the authoritative stream.
  - Gates pending: distribution oracles, fresh-process per-seed stability with `HIPFIRE_MTP_AR_FLOOR=0 HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=0`, unchanged greedy bytes, and sampled thinking closure against plain AR on the same binary. No performance improvement or hardware pass is claimed.
- **Flash-Next sampled MTP: omit unused verify argmax readbacks (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_SAMPLED_PICKS`).**
  - On the device-accept-eligible Leviathan route, private resident verify/capture entrypoints enqueue the same heads and argmax arithmetic without downloading unused greedy picks: one fewer D2H boundary per batched window or visited incremental row. The public `SpecTarget::verify_block` contract still returns real picks.
  - Opt-in (default off): `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_SAMPLED_PICKS=1` omits the pick downloads (only on the device route, so it needs `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_DEVICE_ACCEPT=1`); unset keeps them. Tracing (including phase-timing row traces) retains real pick downloads; host fallback, greedy, naive, penalties and target-only AR-floor paths are unchanged.
  - Gates pending: unchanged head arguments/logit bits and transaction state, truthful trace picks, fresh-process sampled stability, greedy identity and thinking closure. No measured speed improvement is claimed.
- **Tooling: `scripts/pre-commit-ci.sh [BASE_REF]` runs the fast GPU-free CI checks before a commit.** It runs the same commands as the CI `gates`, `supply-chain` (bans/licenses/sources), `fmt` and `clippy` jobs, plus `check-env-docs.py` and `check-lifecycle.py` from `no-gpu-ci.sh`. It skips tests, the release build, the cargo-deny advisories fetch and hw-gate. Every check runs, and the script exits 1 if any failed. Missing `cargo-deny` is an unmet prerequisite: the script fails closed with its install command, rather than reporting a supply-chain finding. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` now require it before every commit. When run locally, `scripts/ci-rustfmt-changed.sh` now diffs against `HEAD`, so it also checks staged files; before, it checked only unstaged ones.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) on gfx1201: the native MTP prompt fill's batched head Append now also runs on exact gfx1201 (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MTP_BATCHED_FILL=0` keeps the per-row loop; gfx1200 and other architectures are unchanged).** Eligibility is exact gfx1151 or exact gfx1201, and the same gate feeds expert placement. The batched pass allocates about 247.77 MiB (259,805,184 bytes) of scratch per drafter (1024 rows, 4096 token IDs); it enters the MTP reserve as part of the per-request bytes, so the auto VRAM reserve can rise by up to that amount and can leave one routed-expert layer fewer resident, depending on slack. The `=0` kill switch removes the allocation and the charge.
  - Identity (gfx1201, deterministic oracle with `HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=0` and `HIPFIRE_MTP_AR_FLOOR=0`): `=0` and `=1` give identical logits, draft state and fill/window state families at synthetic lengths 1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 9, 17, 64, 65, 1023, 1024, 1025, 2049, 4095, 4096, 4097 and 8192, on the real Ciru prompt and a 7,936-token code prompt, and warm at suffix lengths 1, 2, 7, 9, 1024, 1025 and 4097; only the oracle's root route-knob field differs. The takeover hardware test passes, and serve greedy 128-token output md5 is identical for base, current and the kill switches.
  - Speed (card B gfx1201, native serve MTP on, ~8K-token Ciru and code prompts; four interleaved fresh processes per arm, fixed binaries, auto placement with 14 expert layers in every MTP arm and 15 with speculation off): K0 = base, K1 = with the expert-staging extension above, K2 = K1 plus batched fill. Request time, first / steady (median of the 2 later requests per process): Ciru base 3,509.2 / 3,394.4 ms (2,285 / 2,363 tok/s) → K2 2,296.8 / 2,222.2 ms (3,492 / 3,609 tok/s); code base 4,192.0 / 4,132.9 ms (1,911 / 1,938 tok/s) → K2 2,268.9 / 2,224.8 ms (3,531 / 3,601 tok/s). Speculation off: Ciru 2,247.7 / 2,151.7 ms (3,568 / 3,728 tok/s), code 2,207.7 / 2,153.2 ms (3,629 / 3,720 tok/s). `predict ab` steady, batched fill alone (K1 → K2): Ciru +46.41 % (CS95 +44.76..+48.07 %), code +46.00 % (CS95 +42.74..+49.34 %). Receipts: `/home/kaden/qcal/release-0.4.2/fn-mtp-prefill/runs/serve-t-*`. Strix Halo figures in the entry below do not transfer.
- **Singleton sampled DFlash: device accept / residual / bonus (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_DEVICE_ACCEPT=0` is the developer kill switch).** The DFlash2 selector path (and PLD) decided each window on the host: after the verify softmax it downloaded tau / Z and either the compacted sparse rows (counts, ids, values: four D2H syncs) or, for identity and overflow rows, the dense `[rows × vocab]` probabilities, then ran the host accept loop.
  - One new single-block kernel, `dflash_sampled_accept_sparse_q_f32`, takes the accept tests, the residual and the bonus over the resident softmax rows. The host uploads one payload (proposed tokens, sparse selector q, `max_accept`, `b` pre-drawn uniforms) and reads four words. No target-row D2H, no compaction, no dense q; full-support (`tau <= 0`) and overflow rows are read in place on the device. The softmax rows, payload and result buffers are session scratch (`VerifyScratch.device_accept`, grown on first use), so a steady-state window allocates no device memory.
  - Law and determinism: rejection sampling against the normalized `p >= tau` row and the last-write sparse q; per-row accept tests are independent and `accept_len` is the first rejection (bounded by `max_accept`); the residual / bonus use one uniform (they are mutually exclusive) and a fixed 256-segment CDF order, so a seed reproduces its window. The RNG draws differ from the host loop (`b` fixed draws per window), and a CDF fall-off now returns the last support id instead of `vocab - 1`.
  - Production kernel is certified builder (PeaceMaker) code, gfx1201 only: `kernels/dflash_sampled_accept_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` (`dflash_sampled_accept_sparse_q_f32_pm_gfx1201`; emitter `peacemaker-isa/src/kernels/dflash_accept.rs`, `peacemaker-isa emit --kernel dflash_accept --arch gfx1201`; shape contract `peacemaker-isa/kernels/dflash_sampled_accept.gfx1201.sparse_q.contract.json`, M7 lift-identity obligation-free). Same 48-byte kernarg ABI, grid `[1]`, block 256, payload and four output words as the hipcc kernel, which is retained only as a non-production oracle (`dflash_sampled_accept_sparse_q_f32_hip_oracle`); other architectures decide the window on the host (no hipcc production fallback). One wave-0 chain of the 256 segment sums, 16 raw-buffer loads in flight per lane, exact-extent descriptors (a masked lane never reads memory), 6208 bytes static LDS, four barriers. The peacemaker interpreter whitelist gains the eleven opcodes the module uses (`isa/semantics.tbl`). CPU gate: `cargo test -p peacemaker-isa --features toolchain --test dflash_accept` and `cargo test -p rdna-compute --test dflash_accept_emu` (builder kernel == host twin on the interpreter over nucleus / full-support / degenerate / tie / NaN fall-off / 300-row / vocab 32768 windows); hardware: the `sampled_dflash_device_accept_hw` test also asserts builder kernel == hipcc oracle == twin. The `tie_rows` hardware case stride is corrected to 800 (it indexed past the 32768 vocabulary).
  - Continuous-batching sampled lanes now use the same device decision and fixed-`b` RNG law as C1: verify decides each eligible lane from its packed exact or private Product-b logits before scratch reuse, skips host target-row construction, and commit consumes the saved decision. CACTUS, dense q and `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_DEVICE_ACCEPT=0` retain host acceptance; greedy, `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_FAST_SAMPLE=0` and the non-selector C8 `chain_accept_spec_f32` branch are unchanged.
  - Oracles: host-only twin-vs-sparse-host-loop and admission tests (`device_accept_tests`, `dflash_sampled_accept` unit tests); hardware `sampled_dflash_device_accept_hw` (`-- --ignored`): kernel equals its host twin bit for bit on serve / point-mass / full-support / budget / degenerate / tie rows, the committed-token law equals the truncated target row (per-cell 6-sigma bounds plus a Pearson chi-square over the kept cells), and the end-to-end entry is twin-exact and deterministic per seed. Gate commands: `cargo test --release -p rdna-compute dflash_sampled_accept`, `cargo test --release -p hipfire-arch-qwen35 --lib device_accept_tests`, `cargo test --release -p hipfire-arch-qwen35 --test sampled_dflash_device_accept_hw -- --ignored --nocapture`.
- **CPU-AR parallel target rows and ordered acceptance now run certified PeaceMaker (builder-emitted) kernels on gfx1151 and gfx1201 (no hipcc fallback; the hipcc kernels stay as explicit oracle twins).** New `peacemaker-isa` emitters `spec_sampling_rows_pm` (`spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_f32_pm_<arch>`: per-lane register top-64 scan, `pool` extraction rounds over a DPP/`v_permlanex16` butterfly plus 64 bytes of LDS, then a wave-0 finish with the fixed 64-leaf tree, the builder's hipcc `expf` DAG and IEEE division, rank-scatter sort, `min_p`/`top_p` cut, residual correction and `p > 0` compaction) and `spec_accept_pm`, shipped as `kernels/spec_sampling_rows_parallel_pm_<arch>.hxaco` / `kernels/spec_accept_ordered_pm_<arch>.hxaco` with shape contracts and M7 receipts. `Gpu::spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_enqueue` / `spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_precompile` and `spec_accept_ordered_enqueue` (+ new `spec_accept_ordered_precompile`) run the PM images on exactly those two architectures and error elsewhere (`Gpu::spec_cpu_ar_device_supported`). `spec_cpu_ar_precompile` now binds only the PM rows and accept kernels; the previous HIP binding is `spec_cpu_ar_oracle_precompile` (serial rows, candidates, HIP accept) and `spec_cpu_ar_candidates_precompile`. The hipcc twins are `spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_hip_twin_{precompile,enqueue}` and `spec_accept_ordered_hip_twin_{precompile,enqueue}`. Bit equality with the HIP twin (including the builder `expf` DAG versus hipcc `expf`) is established only by the hardware comparison gate. Generation: `peacemaker-isa emit --kernel spec_sampling_rows_pm|spec_accept_pm --arch ARCH --out X.s --proof X.proof.json --co X.co --bundle kernels/<module>.hxaco --host-target host-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu- --shape crates/peacemaker-isa/kernels/<kernel>.ARCH.contract.json --m7 X.m7.json` (build with `--features toolchain`).
- **Experimental CPU-AR speculative sampling primitives (API-only; no production route).** Adds resident 64-entry row construction, eight-candidate draft construction/draw and ordered sparse acceptance using a host-supplied uniform tape. The unchanged literal serial reference preserves slot replacement, accumulation, stable sorting and truncation order, but is diagnostic-only: the CPU probability bit-equality gate was retired after the first gfx1201 synthetic row differed at token 42939 (GPU `0x3d66ae67`, CPU `0x3d66ae68`, 1 ULP). There is no exact-host probability claim. Enqueue methods perform no readback and support recorded launches; the serial code and API are unchanged. The canonical PeaceMaker candidate still requires its twinHW gate against the HIP twin on gfx1151 and gfx1201; retiring the CPU comparison does not establish that parity. These primitives do not enable Flash-Next routing or claim a performance win; no environment switch is introduced.
- **Parallel CPU-AR sampled target-row API candidate (API-only; ungated; no production route).** Adds `spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_precompile` / `spec_cpu_ar_rows_parallel_enqueue` and a separate `spec_sampling_cpu_ar_parallel` module (one 256-thread workgroup per row, ten kernel arguments, no global scratch, no readback) alongside the unchanged serial reference. It follows a new target law, not byte-equal to the reference: canonical selection (higher f32 logit first, equal numeric logits to the lower token id, so tied supports can differ from the reference's slot ties) and fixed-tree aggregate sums. Candidates, draw and ordered acceptance remain the unchanged reference kernels. Performance is unmeasured; distribution, fresh-process seed stability, unchanged-greedy and long-thinking `</think>` closure gates are pending; no environment switch is introduced.
- **Flash-Next sampled primitive reference oracle (experimental, no production route change).** The lab-only `fn_sampled_plumbing_oracle` compares resident target/candidate distributions and ordered acceptance with the existing CPU law bit-for-bit, exercises real graph capture/replay, and records device identity, transfers, synchronization and raw timing. It fails on numerical or capture discrepancies; no performance improvement or hardware pass is implied.
- **Flash-Next adaptive sampled distribution coverage (tests only).** Adds default-route Leviathan arms and a separate late-position oracle with calibration/probe/retirement/post-retirement-AR evidence, retaining the existing statistical thresholds. Replay-byte assertions apply only to forced floor-off arms. Sampled and greedy receipts identify configuration, executable/source/model and GPU; incompatible cached evidence is rejected. Late trials can be split into explicitly recorded disjoint shards.
- **Flash-Next sampled thinking-termination oracle (tests only).** A fixed thinking-enabled prompt is decoded by plain AR and adaptive Leviathan in fresh processes of the same binary. Every declared seed must close `</think>` within the same token budget; decoded text and configuration identities are retained. Budget exhaustion is not a pass.
- **Recording inventory covers the recent continuous-batching DFlash admission predicates and MoE row gather.** Classifies all ten previously unregistered recording/capture checks, including lane-table upload refusal, so the offline `railgun-cert` recording-inventory gate remains synchronized with the source tree. Gate command: `cargo test --release -j 12 -p railgun-cert every_recording_predicate_in_the_tree_has_an_inventory_row`.
- **Qwen4 state-parity `cache_suffix_refusal` test expectation corrected (test-only; no production change).** The scenario deliberately passes `cache_hit = true` at `start_pos = 0` with a complete 3-token fill: an invalid zero-length cached prefix, not an empty fill. Native MTP prefill validation supports valid cache-hit suffixes (`0 < start_pos < prompt_len`, fill exactly the prompt suffix) and refuses position zero with `Qwen4 native MTP cache hit at 0 needs a non-empty suffix of a 3-token prompt`. The GPU compact test now asserts that exact message plus `prompt_len = fill_len = 3`, and a GPU-independent unit test pins the same report so a stale message fails in GPU-less gates.
- **`dflash_convert --mq4v2 --mq4v2-symmetric` / `--mq3v2 --mq3v2-symmetric`: symmetric-grid DFlash draft artifacts.** The draft converter could only write the asymmetric MQ4V2/MQ3V2 headers. The two new flags (each requires its own format flag, else exit 1) encode the 2-D draft projections with the per-128 symmetric grid the main `hipfire-quantize --format mq4v2 --mq4v2-symmetric` / `--format mq3v2 --mq3v2-symmetric` writers use: the stored zero is exactly `-8*d` (MQ4V2) or `-4*d` (MQ3V2) in the unchanged qt=44/qt=49 affine fp16 wire format. Both writers and the converter now call one routine, `hipfire_quantize::mq_symmetric::quantize_symmetric_half`, so there is a single copy of the scale search (the former inline branches in `quant_fwht.rs` / `quant_mq.rs` were replaced by calls to it with identical arithmetic). Default output without the flags is unchanged; norms stay F32, base kernels and codebooks F16. The `dflash` metadata gains `"draft_symmetric": true` only when a symmetric flag is passed; `draft_dtype` stays `mq4v2`/`mq3v2`.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify on gfx1201: one target-row build per run of sampled lanes (`HIPFIRE_CB_SAMPLED_TARGET_BATCH=0` is the developer kill switch).** Sampled lanes built their target rows one lane at a time. Each lane ran a temperature/top-p softmax and a compaction over its own b rows, plus five synchronous downloads; at C8 that is 16 launches and about 40 host syncs per step. The softmax ran one block per row, so each of those launches used only b blocks.
  - With the packed sampled head, the run's logits are contiguous. Consecutive sampled lanes with bitwise-equal sampling configs now share one softmax, one compaction and one download set, split back into each lane's rows.
  - Both kernels compute each row in its own block from that row alone (block-local reductions; the softmax's shared-memory integer atomics are block-local, and ties are sorted before float sums), so rows are bit-identical to per-lane calls.
  - Same setup as the entries below, interleaved on one binary against `=0`, 4 per arm: verify phase 54.0 → 49.3 ms/step (+9.5 %, CS95 +8.32..+10.61 %, `predict ab` win); BetterBench C8 bb_agg 315.5 → 329.2 (+4.3 %, CS95 +0.70..+8.10 %).
  - Exactness: BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…` at C8 and C1, C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…`.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify on gfx1201: admit pool-rounded EF banks for save-free rollback (`HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0` remains the developer kill switch).** EF residuals are pooled `gpu.zeros` allocations, like scales and conv rings, not direct exact-sized mallocs like S. Requiring EF's allocation to equal its logical extent refused valid equal-sized bank pairs.
  - EF now requires equal live/snapshot allocation sizes at least the logical extent; owner capacity is preserved and kernels touch only that extent. S stays exact-sized, and ownership/alias checks are unchanged. The exchange test admits equal rounded EF banks and still refuses unequal banks.
  - An opt-in, default-off `HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE_DIAG=1` prints the first failing admission predicate per chunk, without changing admission or adding diagnostic formatting/allocation/family walks when off. It attributed 418/418 refusals to `owned_pair_refused`: a reused EF bucket of 1,986,560 bytes for a logical 1,572,864-byte extent. After this fix those refusals fell to zero.
  - Same setup as the entries above, all sampled levers enabled: sampled C8 299.1 → 322.5 tok/s (+7.8 %). The tok/s A/B is unresolved at n=3 per arm.
  - Exactness: BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…` at C8 and C1 and with `HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0`, C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes; sparse/dense host oracles cover 10k cases each.
- **DFlash continuous-batching draft on gfx1201: batched sampled lanes (`HIPFIRE_CB_SAMPLED_DRAFT_BATCH=0` is the developer kill switch).** Sampled lanes previously ran the whole draft forward and target head once per lane, even though the normal F16 overwrite route preserves each lane's arithmetic when packed.
  - Each contiguous eligible sampled run now shares one draft forward and head. Each lane retains its own selector, RNG and genuine q receipt, in original traversal order; greedy or ineligible sampled lanes end a run. The batched-draft master switch `HIPFIRE_CB_DFLASH_DRAFT_BATCH` must also be enabled.
  - Admission requires at least `max(draft_min_lanes, 2)` lanes, positive temperature, selector and receipt, `3 <= b <= block_size`, shared draft weights/config/layout, and normal F16 overwrite admission for every MQ4G256V2 draft/head weight at the packed and original lane `b` / `b - 1` row counts. Refused or short runs retain the singleton selector draft.
  - Same setup as the entries above, interleaved on one binary against `=0`: sampled C8 278.4 tok/s (+26.1 %, CS95 +2.77..+54.68 %, `predict ab` win).
  - Exactness: BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…` at C8 and C1 and with `HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0`, C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes; sparse/dense host oracles cover 10k cases each.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify on gfx1201: one shared exact head for sampled lanes (`HIPFIRE_CB_SAMPLED_PACKED_HEAD=0` is the developer kill switch).** Each sampled lane of a packed verify chunk ran its own Product head at its singleton row count, because Product dispatch is not row-count independent above 63 aggregate rows: one head GEMM, argmax and download per lane per step, while greedy lanes already shared one.
  - Admitted chunks now run the whole chunk's rows through the explicit exact F16 head (`DenseBatchMath::SingletonWmma`), which is per-row identical to every lane's singleton Product-b logits at any row count, with one argmax download. Each lane's rows are then normalized in the original lane order, and a SparseRows lane keeps its request-owned representation.
  - Admission is checked on the host before any device write: normal F16 head chain, MQ4G256V2 head, 1..=128 rows in the shared scratch, lane windows of 2..=16 rows, and no FP8 / IU4 verify scope, A8 route, capture, recording or G0 observation; otherwise each lane keeps its private Product-b head.
  - Same setup as the entries above, interleaved on one binary against `=0`: sampled C8 239.4 tok/s (+8.4 %). The tok/s A/B is unresolved at n=3 per arm.
  - Exactness: `cb_verify_gemm_oracle --tests sampled-head` 373/373 with `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_PM=1` and `=0` (fresh processes): the packed exact head against each lane's Product at its own b over 2..=16-row partitions up to 128 rows, with eager and graph replay, padding canaries and negative controls. Serve BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…` at C8 and C1 and with `HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0`, C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes.
- **DFlash sampled verify on gfx1201: sparse target rows and selector q (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_SPARSE_TARGET=0` is the developer kill switch).** A sampled window downloaded the whole `[rows × vocab]` softmax of every verify row and kept every selector q row as a vocab-length vector, only for the accept loop to read a few hundred non-zero entries per row.
  - The device now compacts each row's kept set `p >= tau` (`sampled_rows_compact_f32`: one block per row, ascending ids, no atomics, no float arithmetic, 256 entries per row) and only the counts, ids and values are downloaded. Identity (`tau <= 0`) and overflow rows keep their dense row; CACTUS stays dense.
  - The selector q is kept as its (id, value) support, with the dense row's last-write rule for duplicate candidate ids, and the PLD point mass is one sparse entry.
  - Accept, residual, bonus and the degenerate argmax walk the ascending union of the supports: the dense loop only ever adds, compares or returns where its summand is non-zero, so every f32 operation and its order are unchanged.
  - Same setup as the entries above, interleaved on one binary against `=0`: sampled C8 220.7 → 236.8 tok/s (+7.3 %, CS95 +1.46..+13.48 %, `predict ab` win).
  - Exactness: host oracles with sparse against dense rows: accept 10k cases, residual / categorical 10k, point-mass residual 10k, each with identical decisions and RNG state. Serve BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…` at C8 and C1 and with `HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0`, C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) singleton native MTP: the AR floor may retire a request only after 16 observed windows (`HIPFIRE_MTP_FLOOR_MIN_WINDOWS`, developer var, default 16; `3` is the kill switch and restores retirement at the probe budget). Route-only; greedy output is byte-identical.** Measured routing still starts after the 3-window probe. Inside the grace, a request whose options lose to AR keeps drafting on its cheapest measured depth instead of retiring stickily to AR. On Strix Halo real-text serve, retirement at the probe budget retired 5 of 9 sampled 32K requests within their first windows; those requests decoded at 28–29 tok/s against 40–46 when drafting continued. The A/B of an earlier form (the whole probe budget raised to 16) on a single binary removed every early retirement: sampled 32K went from 35.8 to 42.9 tok/s mean. But it cost 1–5% on short requests because the provisional route ran longer. This form leaves those requests' routing unchanged, and its own silicon A/B is pending. Continuous-batching lanes are unchanged.
- **Qwen3.8-27B: DFlash is the registry default when the paired draft is present (`speculation.dflash = "auto"` from the registry layer; `dflash_mode off` is the kill switch).** The schema default stays `dflash_mode = off` and `mtp_mode = auto`. The registry config layer now lowers `speculation.dflash = "auto"` for the Qwen3.8-27B trunk tags (`qwen3.8:27b` and `qwen3.8:27b-*`, never a longer lookalike) whose registry entry declares a `dflash` sidecar: `qwen3.8:27b`, `-mq3`, `-mq3-xt`, `-mq3-pro`, `-mq4-xt`, `-mq4-xts`, `-mq4-pro`. A `-draft-*` sidecar tag, a tier with no `dflash` declaration (`mq5*`, `mq6*`) and every other model get nothing.
  - A present paired draft file activates DFlash with no `hipfire config`. An absent file wires nothing and the load keeps MTP `auto` (no failure). Explicit global, per-model and one-shot config rank above the registry layer: `hipfire config qwen3.8:27b set dflash_mode off` (canonical key `speculation.dflash = "off"`, also `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_MODE=off`) restores the previous MTP-only load.
  - Unchanged: every other model's `dflash_mode` default (`off`), the `dflash_block` `auto` width (16 singleton, 8 on a `serve.vmm_batch` serve with `continuous_batch_size` ≥ 4) and the IU4 verify gates. - **DFlash continuous-batching draft on gfx1201: lane-batched context fill and fc (`HIPFIRE_CB_DRAFT_LANE_CTX=0` is the developer kill switch).** The batched draft still projected each lane's newly committed target-hidden rows (fc) and each layer's context K/V rows lane by lane: rotate + GEMM (or GEMV) + norm + RoPE per lane, per layer.
  - Rows are now packed by the arithmetic class of their ORIGINAL per-lane segment:
    - ≥ 2-row segments go into shared overwrite-WMMA chunks of 2..=63 rows, per-row identical to the one-tile route.
    - 1-row segments, whose singleton route is the R2 GEMV, go into one `gemv_mq4g256v2_multirow_r2_lanes` launch: the R2 body per z-plane, in its own source so the sha-pinned GEMV source is untouched.
    - Then one norm and one K RoPE run over the packed rows, with one gather and one scatter.
  - Same setup as the entries above, interleaved on one binary against `=0`, 4 per arm: draft phase 13.42 → 11.31 ms/step; C8 greedy 486.5 → 501.8 tok/s (+3.2 %, CS95 +1.82..+4.61 %, `predict ab` win on the 3 fast-mode rounds).
  - Exactness: `draft_lane_rows_oracle` (lab) 640/640: the R2 twin bit-identical to the R2 GEMV per row on real draft shapes, gather/scatter bit copies with guards, and the R2-vs-WMMA negative control differs as expected. Serve C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…` (and C1), BetterBench sampled `91bde7c5…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes.

Observed identity (gfx1201, deterministic oracle): the fill oracle's state families, final logits and draft state matched with the extension on and off at synthetic lengths 1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 9, 17, 64, 65, 1023, 1024, 1025, 2049, 4095, 4096, 4097 and 8192, on the real Ciru prompt and a 7,936-token code prompt, and warm (prefix-cache) at suffix lengths 1, 2, 7, 9, 1024, 1025 and 4097; the takeover hardware test passes; serve greedy 128-token output md5 is identical for base, current and the kill switches. Measured with the batched-fill entry below, native serve MTP on, card B gfx1201, ~8K-token Ciru and code prompts: step K0 → K1 (this extension alone) steady request time improves +4.87 % on Ciru (CS95 +4.23..+5.51 %) and +27.42 % on code (CS95 +26.10..+28.77 %), per `predict ab` over interleaved fresh processes.

Strict identity reruns disable the timing-calibrated AR floor (`HIPFIRE_MTP_AR_FLOOR=0`) and force batched verify (`HIPFIRE_MTP_INCREMENTAL=0`), since the default chooser can change post-window draft state. With those controls, every compared value matched except the oracle's root route-knob field, which differs by construction. The controls remove wall-clock route adaptation only in the oracle; serve keeps the defaults.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify on gfx1201: lane-batched DeltaNet and save-free rollback for unequal lane row counts (`HIPFIRE_CB_LANE_GDN_VARROWS=0` is the developer kill switch).** The lane-batched DeltaNet verify and the save-free rollback needed every lane of a chunk to verify the same number of rows. Any lane with a clipped window near its request's end therefore sent the whole chunk back to the per-lane route: 1,344 more launches plus the ~120 MB-per-lane state SAVE on that step.
  - Now each lane carries its own row count (1..=16; 2..=16 for save-free) in a new 80/104-byte lane table row. The new recurrence symbols `gated_delta_net_q8_fast_lanes_varrows[_from]` are the same kernel body, with `n_tokens` and the stochastic-rounding `frame_off` read from the row.
  - `frame_off` is the prefix sum of the earlier lanes' rows, i.e. exactly the frames the per-lane launch order reserves, and the host reserves the chunk total once.
  - The D8 replay tables and ABI are unchanged.
  - Same setup as the entries above, interleaved on one binary against `=0`, 5 per arm: verify phase 48.8 → 47.6 ms/step (+2.7 %, CS95 +1.16..+4.31 %, `predict ab` win); fast-mode C8 greedy medians 474.3 → 486.1 tok/s. The tok/s A/B itself is unresolved because of host slow-mode runs in both arms.

Serve C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…` (and C1), BetterBench sampled `91bde7c5…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes.
- **DFlash continuous-batching draft on gfx1201: lane-batched dynamic causal convolution (`HIPFIRE_CB_DRAFT_LANE_CONV=0` is the developer kill switch).** Each DFlash2 prepare/finish convolution of the batched draft ran once per lane, 160 launches per C8 step. It now runs as one `dynamic_causal_conv_f32_lanes` launch per stage (20 per step). That kernel is the `dynamic_conv_f32` body per lane, with grid.z = lane, rows rebased to the lane and causal guards on the lane-local row, so lanes never see each other's predecessors.

- **Singleton DFlash chain verify: one batched rollback-tape copy per DeltaNet layer (`HIPFIRE_VERIFY_TAPE_COPY_BATCH=0` is the developer kill switch).** A lone request's B=16 verify snapped each DeltaNet layer's GDN rollback tape (qkv, α, β rows) with three `hipMemcpyDtoD`, 144 copy nodes in every captured verify graph on the 27B. It now issues the continuous-batching route's three descriptors as one `tape_copy_batched` launch at the same point (48 nodes). Pure byte copies, so the tape is identical by construction.

End-to-end C1 decode is within run-to-run noise (`predict ab` +0.1 %, unresolvable, 3 rounds).

- **DFlash continuous-batching draft on gfx1201: one batched noise-embedding lookup (`HIPFIRE_DRAFT_NOISE_EMBED_BATCH=0` is the developer kill switch).** The batched draft looked up each row's noise embedding (the lane's seed, then the mask token) with its own `embedding_q8` launch: 64 per step at C8. It now uploads the row ids once and runs one `embedding_q8_batched`. Both kernels compute every element as `f16 scale × int8` with the same thread striding, so rows are byte-identical to the singleton draft's per-row lookups. gfx1100 keeps its fused-collapse builder.

- **VMM continuous-batching serve: fewer synchronous uploads per step (`HIPFIRE_CB_UPLOAD_COALESCE=0` is the developer kill switch).** Every step re-uploaded each KV layer's slot-descriptor table with a blocking `hipMemcpy`, 16 per step on the 27B, even when nothing changed. The multi-request verify trunk also uploaded positions once per lane. Each blocking upload drains the device before the host can queue more work.
  - Descriptor tables are now uploaded only when their bytes differ from a host mirror of the last successful upload (mapping or owner changes).
  - The verify trunk writes all lanes' positions in one copy.
  - Same device bytes at the same addresses before every reader.
  - Same setup as the tape-copy entry below, on top of the entries above; interleaved on one binary against `=0`, 5 per arm: conc_steady C8 greedy 471.8 → 473.8 tok/s, +0.45 %, CS95 +0.12..+0.79 % on the 4 rounds whose base ran in fast mode (`predict ab`, below its 1 % default bar).

- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) expert parallelism across 2 or 4 R9700s: `topology.ep` (`HIPFIRE_TOPOLOGY_EP`, default 1; `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_EP=0` forces 1).** Opt-in and experimental; ep = 1 is unchanged. The routed experts of every MoE stack (the 48 trunk layers and the MTP head) are sharded by id, `e % N` (`ExpertSharded { 512, Stride }`). Rank `r` uploads its 512/N experts compactly in ascending `e`, to VRAM or pinned host RAM. Every rank applies the same expert VRAM split: the minimum over ranks, printed on each rank's `qwen4 EP rank r/N` fit line. Every rank holds the whole trunk and runs the unchanged single-device program on its own worker thread.
  - Decode: each rank's one-row grouped QT44/QT53 down writes the expanded rows of the experts it owns. Non-owned slots read a zero dummy expert. The copy-only `ep_gather_rows_by_owner` exchange then overwrites every non-owned slot with its owner's bits, before the unchanged ascending-expert combine and the shared expert.
  - Prefill chunks read non-owned experts over P2P. EP decode is eager: there is no graph capture or Redline.
  - Host results such as the argmax are compared across ranks. A disagreement or any rank error poisons the mesh until the model is reloaded.
  - Admission takes `pp = tp = 1` and `ep` in 1/2/4, on gfx1201 only, with every directed card pair peer-accessible. It refuses native MTP, DFlash/DSpark/n-gram/DDTree, adaptive KV, drafters, CASK, PFlash, lane batching, snapshots and the retained Redline default, naming `topology.ep`.
  - The load ack reports `ep`, `ep_devices` and `ep_rank_resident_bytes`. The Qwen4 carrier's bundle load is `load_qwen4_rank`, callable per rank on a peer `Gpu`. Each rank derives its forward scratch plan from its own source, and the EP split charges that plan.
  - Exactness (R9700, Flash-Next gptq3, `fn_ep_oracle`, eager): EP2 and EP4 are byte-identical to one card. The MoE stage dumps match on every rank at decode positions 0 and 1 in all 48 layers: route ids and weights, the ten expanded rows after the exchange, fold in/out, shared in/out and the residual. Over 256 greedy tokens, the logits match at every step at ctx 512 (3 prompts), 8192 and 32768, and so does the whole per-request state on every rank at steps 1, 64 and 256. A one-bit flip in rank 1's state fails the oracle from the next step, and the mesh's own agreement check stops the run.
  - ep = 1 is unchanged: `qwen4_qsa_ctx` ctx 8192 / chunk 4096 final-row logits md5 `8eb0e14a…`, the same as beta.
  - Fit at 32K context, per rank: EP4 keeps all 49 routed layers in VRAM with +3.77 GiB headroom. EP2 keeps 30 of 49, with 19 host-mapped and +0.34 GiB headroom.
  - Speed, one timing admission, `hipfire bench` tg128 `--spec off`, 3 fresh processes per arm interleaved: EP4 AR decode 61.2 tok/s against 36.1 on one card (graph AR, host-mapped experts), 1.70×. Time to first token on a 42-token prompt is slower, 408 ms against 286 ms: prefill is replicated on every rank and reads non-owned experts over P2P.
- **JIT kernel cache: one kernel's compile and publish are serialized per process.** Every EP rank compiles the same kernels on first use. On a cold cache, one rank's republish removed the hash sidecar while another rank was checking it (`post-publish pair invalid`), and rewrote the `.hip` source while another rank's hipcc was reading it. A rank that waited now takes the object the first rank published, so hipcc runs once. A forced recompile after a driver rejection still recompiles.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify on gfx1201: save-free DeltaNet rollback (`HIPFIRE_CB_DN_SAVE_FREE=0` is the developer kill switch).** Before every verify, each lane copied its whole DeltaNet state into the rollback snapshot: S, scales, EF residual and conv ring, about 120 MB per lane per step on the 27B. Now each lane's live and snapshot buffer owners are exchanged on the host, so the pre-verify state already is the snapshot. The verify trunk reads it as its source and writes the new live bank:
  - The conv ring is byte-copied A → B inside the layer's existing `tape_copy_batched` launch (~5 % of the bytes the save moved). The unchanged in-place lane conv then runs on B. A `_from` conv twin was rejected because its gfx1201 codegen differed by a few ulp.
  - The recurrence runs `gated_delta_net_q8_fast_ml_from`.
  - Commit and terminal repair are unchanged: a short accept replays from the snapshot (D8).
  - The snapshot-source tables (verify lane table and D8 replay tables) are cached in two slots, so the alternating banks re-upload nothing.
  - Admission is all-or-nothing per chunk: lane-batched GDN admitted, equal row count 2..=16, D8 replay admitted, Q8 + EF, equal-size HIP-owned banks at least the logical extent, pairwise distinct owners, no Redline/replay graph. Otherwise the save runs as before.
  - Same setup as the tape-copy entry below, on top of the entries above; interleaved A/B on the same binary with `=0`, 6 per arm, `predict ab`: conc_steady C8 greedy 453.8 → 471.0 tok/s (+3.69 %, CS95 +3.07..+4.31 %), verify phase 51.9 → 48.9 ms/step (CS95 +5.55..+6.13 %), accept phase unchanged. BetterBench v1 T0.7 C8 is unresolved at 3 per arm (sampled lanes take the per-lane draft path).
  - Exactness:
    - `lane_gdn_oracle` 40/40: arm C = ring copy + in-place conv + `_from` recurrence from a separate source bank, against the per-lane sequence, every output row and state byte, source bank untouched.
    - `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (block 8, IU4, 8 lanes) passes.
    - Serve md5s: C8 greedy `fa168ee2…` (and C1), BetterBench sampled `91bde7c5…`.
    - `dn_exchange` / `two_slot` unit tests pass.

- **DFlash continuous-batching draft on gfx1201: one 64-row draft forward at C8 (`HIPFIRE_CB_DRAFT_ROWS64=0` is the developer kill switch).** The batched greedy draft packed at most 48 rows per forward, so C8 with 8-row lanes ran two draft forwards and two 248K-row head GEMMs per step (48 + 16 rows), reading the draft weights and the head twice. The cap is now 64 where that is exact: exact gfx1201 with the draft overwrite route on.   - Exactness: `lane_gdn_oracle` (lab example) 18/18 cases bit-identical against the per-lane sequence for every output row and every conv / Q8 / scale / EF state byte (1, 3, 8 lanes × 2, 9, 16 rows, EF on/off); serve C8 greedy md5 `fa168ee2…` (and C1), BetterBench sampled md5 `91bde7c5…`, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (block 8, IU4, 8 lanes) passes.
- **DFlash draft on gfx1201: overwrite GEMM twins replace the per-call pre-zero fill (`HIPFIRE_DRAFT_COLLAPSE_OFF=1` is the developer kill switch, shared with the gfx1100 S7 collapse).** Every MQ4G256V2 draft-body projection and the draft lm_head ran `hipMemset(y, 0)` and then the residual WMMA (`Y += acc`). On exact gfx1201, where that route ends in the one-tile `gemm_mq4g256v2_residual_wmma_gfx12` or the 128-K `_vt` verify-tile kernel (2..=63 rows; no fp8/IU4 verify scope, capture or recording), the draft now launches overwrite twins (`gemm_mq4g256v2_overwrite_wmma_gfx12[_vt*]`). They store `acc + 0.0f` once per output, which is bit-identical to `+0.0f + acc` including signed zero, and the fill goes away: 184 fewer launches per C8 step.
  - Same setup as the tape-copy entry below: BetterBench v1 T0.7 C8 steady 241.1 → 243.8 tok/s (+1.15 %, CS95 +0.72..+1.58 %, 4 per arm); conc_steady C8 greedy +0.96 % (CS95 +0.69..+1.24 %), draft phase 18.4 → 17.6 ms/step. Both changes together: BetterBench +3.20 % (CS95 +2.72..+3.69 %, 241.1 → 248.8), conc_steady greedy +3.88 % (374.5 → 389.0).
  - Exactness: `draft_overwrite_gfx12_oracle` (lab example) 35/35 shapes bit-identical against memset + residual (5 draft (M, K) × N ∈ {2, 9, 16, 17, 33, 48, 63}, vt and one-tile; also 35/35 with `HIPFIRE_WMMA_BATCH_TILES=0`); serve text md5s and `cb_vmm_state_oracle` as in the tape-copy entry.
  - The draft-phase slow mode of the IU4 entry below did not occur in any process of this campaign: 0 of 40 fresh C8 processes without this change and 0 of 40 with it (at the earlier ≈8 % incidence, 0 of 40 has probability ≈4 %). Whether the fill triggered it cannot be decided on this tip.
- **DFlash continuous-batching verify: one batched tape-capture copy per DeltaNet layer (`HIPFIRE_CB_TAPE_COPY_BATCH=0` is the developer kill switch).** The multi-request verify trunk (`forward_prefill_batch_multi`) snapped each lane's GDN rollback tape (pre-conv1d qkv rows, post-sigmoid α/β rows) with three `hipMemcpyDtoD` per lane per layer: 1,152 `__amd_rocclr_copyBuffer` launches per C8 step on the 27B, each paying a dispatch bubble. The copies are now queued and issued after the layer's per-lane recurrence as one `tape_copy_batched` launch per layer (descriptor table in the kernarg segment, pure byte copy; 48 launches per step). Nothing writes the source rows between the sigmoid and the deferred copy, so the tape bytes are identical by construction.
  - Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on one R9700, `serve.vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size = 8`, `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1`, L8 + IU4 verify, interleaved fresh processes, `predict ab`: BetterBench v1 T0.7 C8 steady 241.1 → 245.4 tok/s (+2.09 %, CS95 +1.73..+2.44 %, 4 per arm); conc_steady C8 greedy (`HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1`, 3 per arm) 374.5 → 385.6 (+2.97 %, CS95 +1.82..+4.13 %), verify phase 64.2 → 61.3 ms/step. rocprofv3 + HIP-API trace: verify launches 4,325 → 3,221 per step, verify idle gaps 14.7 → 10.7 ms (traced).
  - Exactness: C8 greedy text md5 `fa168ee2…` equals the base configuration (and C1), BetterBench sampled text md5 `91bde7c5…` equals base, `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (DFlash block 8, IU4, 8 lanes) passes.
- **railgun runs again on current beta (gfx1201, developer-only `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_BACKEND=railgun`).** The JIT receipt corpus had not been rebuilt since railgun/m1p (HIP 7.15), so every node of the 27B tape was `receipt_missing`. Rebuilding exposed two gaps, both fixed: the PeaceMaker gfx12 table lacked two compares clang 24 emits (`v_cmpx_le_i32_e32`, `v_cmpx_le_f32_e64`), and `railgun-jit-corpus build` never recorded the five embedded PM decode twins the default loads (now certified as `pm_native` records keyed by image digest; corpus schema v2). The pack also ships a Radiowave sidecar per object, so Redline types every launch. On the 27B (qwen3.8-27b.mq4-xts, R9700): 755/755 launches certified, G7 PASS with 0 weaker boundaries, `HIPFIRE_RAILGUN_CHECK=always` 257/257 steps equal; logits, hidden, KV and DeltaNet state byte-equal to the Redline default and the HIP graph at ctx 512 and 8192. Decode tg128@512 is 41.06 tok/s against Redline's 41.38 (−0.8%) and the HIP graph's 38.65. Rebuild steps: REDLINE.md "railgun shadow diff".
- **DFlash IU4 verify, on by default where `speculation.dflash_block` resolves to 8 (a batching serve: `serve.vmm_batch` with `continuous_batch_size` ≥ 4); `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_IU4=0` is the kill switch, `=1` forces it on. Numerics differ from the F16 verify (4-bit A4 verify activations); quality is tied on task evals.** F16 == IU4 is not promised; batched == singleton stays byte-identical within the IU4 configuration. Singleton-only loads (`auto` = 16) keep the F16 verify. On exact gfx1201 with a symmetric dense MQ4G256V2 target (IU4 prefill and both gfx1201 A4 producer fusions on), the singleton chain verify, the VMM continuous-batching verify trunk (any aggregate row count, including the `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128` chunk) and the batched rollback replay run every projection through the gfx1201 A4 producer and its IU4 GEMM (`_b1s` SET/ADD, F1-lite gate/up+SwiGLU). `DenseBatchMath::Iu4Verify`; the A4 producer gates admit any batch inside `Gpu::iu4_verify_scope`; A8/FP8-stream routes, the S4 F16 residual arm and the fused QKVZ+GDN-prep epilogue are off there; a missing A4 producer is an error, not a mixed route. The lm_head stays F16 and the GDN scan plane F32. The IU4 verify runs eager (no HipGraph verify) and retained PM4 verify is disabled with it. The decision is per DFlash load (`DFlash verify IU4: on|off` in the load log); `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_FP8=1` wins the default, and `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_IU4=1` with `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_FP8=1` fails the load.
  - Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on one R9700, `serve.vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size = 8`, `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1`, L8 (`auto`), IU4 default vs `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_IU4=0`: conc_steady C8 (64 code prompts × 256, 8 fresh processes per arm, interleaved, `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1` in both arms, `predict ab`) +9.9 % steady tok/s (CS95 +5.3..+14.7 %; medians 374.5 vs 340.1; uninstrumented single pair 363.8 vs 341.9); BetterBench v1 T0.7 C8 (3 per arm, sampled requests batched) 192.3 → 199.9 aggregate tok/s (+3.9 %, CS95 +2.8..+5.1 %). **A lone request on that batching serve runs the IU4 verify at 8 rows: 107.6 → 99.3 tok/s (−7.7 %), the price of the C8 gain** (a narrow 16-token IU4 tile would recover it). Opt-in at L16 (`HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_IU4=1`): C8 252.4 → 326.0 (+29 %), BetterBench T0.7 C8 169.1 → 201.9 (+19.4 %), C1 114.2 → 105.2.
  - Exactness: `iu4_verify_oracle` 112/112 row-count checks byte-identical for every verify producer + GEMM incl. the F1-lite fused SwiGLU and the whole A4 FFN (N=1…128, 64+64, 48+48+32); serve C8 texts == C1 texts at L8 (64/64, `fa168ee2…`) and at L16 (`44d70b63…`); the kill switch at L8 reproduces the F16 L8 text (`2f262d21…`); `cb_vmm_state_oracle` is IU4-aware (no F16 wide symbol, IU4 GEMMs per chunk) and its direct arm opens the scope like `dflash_cb_verify`. Flag unset at L16, C1/C8 text and `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (peer) are byte-identical to beta.
  - Quality: verify-logit KLD vs F16 mean 8.8e-3, top-1 97.2 %. Paired greedy DFlash task eval (singleton, same binary, F16 vs IU4): HumanEval 157 → 156 of 164 (2 vs 1 items), GSM8K 5-shot 1,282 → 1,282 of 1,319 (5 vs 5), MMLU-Pro subset 871 → 871 of 1,400 (identical text).
  - Run-to-run spread: some fresh processes sit in a DFlash **draft-phase** slow mode (draft ≈27 vs ≈18 ms/step at C8 L8; verify unchanged at 63 ms IU4 / 72 ms F16), the bimodal draft time first recorded in `cb/chunk128/GATE0.md` §6. It occurs with the F16 verify too (same magnitude), can clear mid-process, and costs a slow process ≈8–10 % (C8 L8 IU4 ≈340–352 vs ≈374 tok/s). Under a kernel trace the slow mode is small draft kernels that follow a `hipMemset` fill running ≈10 µs longer; total kernel time is unchanged. Not caused by the IU4 verify; cause not fixed.
- **radiowave: CPU-only union identity records (`radiowave::union`), developer-only, no runtime behavior change.** A new, separately versioned serde schema (`schema_version` 1, unknown fields and other versions rejected) with a distinct Rust type per identity domain: a certified-executable record (semantic recipe, producer/compiler/driver/Mesa-commit-and-patch pins, exact arch/wave, raw code/data/config/entry-ABI/sidecar digests and ranges, static-certificate binding, adapter prologue/body ranges with an unchanged-body digest check, and HSA / original-RADV / private-RADV / Windows envelope namespaces), a loaded-executable receipt binding, a four-arm (V0/Hpm/Vpm/Hllvm) pair-oracle receipt, `VulkanExecutableRecord`, and serialized-only boundary/resource-generation/plan records. It validates completeness and consistency on the CPU only: it loads no code, claims no PM proof and fabricates no loaded or oracle receipt. `CompileManifest`, `PeacemakerRecord` and `OracleReceipt` are unchanged. Landed upstream first (redline `65b6793`) and re-vendored as a scoped addition; `crates/radiowave/VENDOR.md` records exactly what was and was not re-pinned.
- **Developer-only RADV/ACO MQ4 import experiment.** A pinned offline SPIR-V/RADV producer captures executable words, config and an explicit entry-ABI sidecar. PeaceMaker's raw lift uses the existing typed codecs and rejects unknown encodings, undeclared entry state and altered artifact identities. A finite HSA entry adapter uses the native code-object writer and preserves the original ACO body words. The claim-scoped `pm_aco_oracle` compares captured real buffers and guards against Vulkan, hipcc and the accepted native twin in eager and hipGraph modes; its separate timing mode requires a passing parity receipt. This does not enable an ACO product route or establish hardware parity or a performance win by itself.
  - First gfx1201 card-B MQ4 decode GEMV experiment: the 5,456-byte ACO body is unchanged behind a 28-byte HSA prologue; Raw/HSA certificates have zero open obligations. Vulkan-original, PM/HIP, hipcc and the accepted native twin agree byte-for-byte on captured real inputs, outputs and guards, including negative controls, eager/hipGraph and the odd/tail case. One exclusive warm-cache diagnostic (prediction P0062) measured medians 21.743 µs ACO/HIP, 18.243 µs hipcc and 21.857 µs native PM: no win over hipcc and no resolved advantage over the native twin. Performance spending/default promotion for this pilot stops; portability parity is retained. These are kernel diagnostics, not product-speed evidence.
- **Dense Qwen3.5 PM4 staging ordering is protected by an ignored hardware regression.** The synchronous position upload drains legacy-NULL and blocking-stream embedding/staging work before the independent PM4 queue submits. A load-bearing comment prevents an accidental async-copy conversion; held-stream tests check real 27B decode against ordinary HIP before any host readback. No production synchronization or arithmetic changes.
- **Flash-Next retained PM4 replay (forced/shadow plan, developer-only) synchronizes the staging stream first.** HIP PLE staging is outside the PM4 queue's ordering, so each Qwen4 PM4 replay now drains the active stream (the default stream when none is set) first; a sync failure poisons the route and returns the forward error instead of replaying. The HIP route, Qwen3.5 and device-wide ordering are unchanged. New `hip_bridge::HipRuntime::stream_synchronize_default`.
- **Flash-Next retained capture fails closed on a launch-census mismatch.** A typed refusal records the launched and recorded counts, discards the incomplete tape and keeps later forwards on the existing HIP route. The shared `silu_f32` launcher now uses the capture-aware kernarg-blob funnel, preserving its kernel and arithmetic while owning the arguments for graph capture and retained recording.
- **Shared-weight host v1 (developer, Linux, gfx1201, default off).** New binary `hipfire-weight-host` loads a dense Qwen3.5 HFQ once with the ordinary loader, promotes each of its final weight owners (1,347 for the 27B) into a read-only exportable VMM allocation (`round_up(logical + 64 KiB, minimum granularity)`, zeroed tail) and leases the POSIX allocation FDs of one generation (random 128-bit epoch) to tenant daemons over a private Unix socket. A daemon started with `HIPFIRE_WEIGHT_HOST=1 HIPFIRE_WEIGHT_HOST_SOCKET=<path>` imports and maps them instead of uploading its own copy, keeps its own KV/state/scratch/graphs/kernels, and refuses anything outside the v1 envelope (no private-load fallback). Host and tenants take a shared, dev-fabric-admitted claim on the machine-wide GPU lock (`HIPFIRE_FABRIC_ADMISSION`); the default exclusive claim and private load are unchanged, and `HIPFIRE_WEIGHT_HOST=0` is the kill switch. Tenants send `Release` only after a verified clean unload; a host SIGTERM stops admission, half-closes leases and drains before freeing. Loads now print one `QWEN35_LOAD_PHASES` line. See CONFIG.md "Shared-weight host" and the VALIDATION.md row; correctness acceptance (G3) is pending, and nothing here is a performance claim.
- **`speculation.dflash_block` (env `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_BLOCK`): the DFlash2 proposal width L, `auto` or 4–16, default `auto`.** L is fixed per serving configuration and never follows the live batch, so a batched VMM lane and a singleton of the same load run the same L and stay byte-identical, and text never depends on server load. `auto` = 8 on a `serve.vmm_batch` serve with `continuous_batch_size` ≥ 4 (a full C8 verify step stays at 64 rows instead of 128), else 16, so singleton-only loads keep the previous width. Default on because C4 gains +20 %, C8 +27 % and BetterBench T0.7 C8 +17 % with task scores tied (below); the cost is about −6 % for a lone request on such a batching serve. Kill switch: `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_BLOCK=16` (or `speculation.dflash_block = 16`) restores the fixed width 16. The daemon resolves `auto` from the route it stages (a load without the param also resolves `auto`); legacy DFlash drafts keep their declared width; experimental multi-slot admits only 16. `cb_vmm_state_oracle --dflash-block N` runs Gate 0 at width N.
  - Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on one R9700 (`conc_steady.py`, 64 code prompts × 256 tokens, `serve.vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size = 8`, `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1`; 3 fresh processes per arm, interleaved after a discarded warm-up), steady tok/s, L16 → L8: C1 114.8 → 107.8 (−6.1 %), C4 227.4 → 272.6 (+19.9 %), C8 254.6 → 324.6 (+27.5 %). BetterBench v1 T0.7 C8 (5 processes per arm): 168.5 → 195.2 aggregate output tok/s (+17.3 %).
  - Exactness at L8: C8 text equals C1 on 64/64 prompts, both greedy and T0.7 seed 42 sampled; C4 also equals C1. `auto` on that serve gives the same text as L8. `cb_vmm_state_oracle --dflash-block 8 --max-lanes 8` with CHUNK128 + PM verify passes, including the 64-row aggregate as one wide chunk. L16 text equals the previous binary's on 64/64 prompts and on all 2,883 task items.
  - Task evidence (greedy DFlash singleton, same binary, L16 → L8): HumanEval 157 → 157 of 164 (1 item each way); GSM8K 5-shot 1,282 → 1,280 of 1,319 (3 items pass only at L16, 1 only at L8; McNemar p = 0.63); MMLU-Pro subset 871 → 871 of 1,400, with identical text on all 1,400. Greedy text differs between L values: 15 of the 64 code prompts differ.
- **Qwen4 developer dense profiles execute (developer builds only, `qwen4-dev-profiles`).** `Qwen4LayerDescription.ffn` is `Qwen4FfnBinding::{Dense, Moe}`; a dense layer runs the new dispatch `Step::DenseFfn` (gate/up/down projections, SiLU, BF16 storage after each projection, the SiLU, the product and the down projection) between the unchanged HC read and HC write. Widths come from the profile (HC inject rows = `hc_count`, PLE channels = `hidden * hc_count`, PLE row geometry, QSA RoPE width `head_dim * partial_rotary_factor`); PLE and the native MTP head are optional; the loader charges a dense FFN as resident non-expert weights and threads the admitted recipe into assembly. Refused before allocation: synthetic MoE (no sealed small-MoE route) and a full-attention or MTP RoPE base other than the 1e7 the QSA kernels implement; fn-lanes refuse non-Flash-Next profiles. Developer profiles decode their source BF16 weights (no Q8 decode copies) and never record a retained (Redline) body. Flash-Next routes, scratch bytes and step order are unchanged.
- **Flash-Next prefill: the MQ6 trunk GEMM on gfx1201 dequantizes weight codes two at a time** (`qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1201`, `v_pk_add_f16` / `v_pk_fma_f16`; 18 instead of 34 VALU per K-step). Output bytes are unchanged (twin harness: 0 differing bytes over random, boundary, special and residual cells, w4 and w8, ragged M/N). R9700 pp8192 at the default 4096-row chunk: 3,837 → 3,841 tok/s; at `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS=8192`: 4,895 → 4,926 tok/s.
- **Flash-Next compact forward scratch on gfx1201 (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_COMPACT_SCRATCH=1`, developer var, default off; storage only).** When every routed expert header in the artifact is symmetric IU4, the MoE buffers are sized for the IU4 route plus a 512-row F32 fallback, and phase-disjoint per-layer temporaries share storage. The same plan is charged to `auto` expert placement and allocated at attach. Dispatch and the launchers check buffer spans, so a route without a buffer returns an error instead of writing out of bounds. On an R9700 at an 8192-row chunk the scratch is 4,240 MiB smaller, so `auto` keeps routed layers 0..15 in VRAM (0..11 with the full plan). Logits are byte-identical to the full plan at fixed placement (16K context, 4096- and 8192-row chunks). AR and MTP decode are unchanged (35.13 / 35.14 tok/s AR, same greedy tokens), and pp8192 at `HIPFIRE_PREFILL_CHUNK_ROWS=8192` is 4,948 tok/s.
- **`serve -d` no longer reports a false success.** It first checks `serve.pid` and `/health`: a tracked server on the same port prints `hipfire serve already running (pid N, port P, …)` and exits 0; a tracked server on another port, or another responder on the port, exits 1 with the reason. Readiness now requires `/health` to report the spawned child's PID, and a child that exits early fails with the last `FATAL`/`hipfire:` line it wrote to `serve.log` (e.g. the bind or GPU-lock error) instead of only "see serve.log".
- **Auto-attached speculation says so and how to turn it off.** When MTP (or a registry DFlash draft) attaches in `auto` mode on a Qwen3.5-family load, the load log prints one line, e.g. `MTP: on (auto, sidecar qwen3.8-27b.mtp) — disable with --spec off or speculation.mtp off`. Forced `on` and `off` print nothing new.
- **The lazy kernel path says it is compiling.** `kernels: no closed route plan (…); loading kernels lazily` becomes `kernels: compiling kernels on first use (one-time, cached afterwards)… [no closed route plan: …]`, so a cold first run's wait is explained.
- **`hipfire stop --force` (and `restart`) no longer runs `pkill -x daemon`.** It signals only PIDs from this install's daemon pidfiles, GPU-lock holders and serve children whose `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is this install's daemon binary and whose `HIPFIRE_HOME` is this home: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5 s. It no longer runs `fuser -k <port>/tcp`; if something else still holds the port, it says so and leaves it alone (#10).
- **`serve --spec off|auto|ngram|dflash|mtp|dspark` and `serve --max-seq N`, plus `run --max-seq N`.** These are applied to every model load the same way `run --spec` and `bench --max-seq` already are, and they override `speculation.mode`, per-model speculation and `memory.max_seq`. `serve -d` passes them to the detached child, and `run --max-seq` always loads locally.
- **`serve --max-seq N` now also sets the per-slot context of multi-slot serving.** With `serve.multi_slot` on, the flag replaces `serve.multi_slot_ctx` (which still applies when the flag is unset), so total KV scales with `serve.multi_slot_slots`; before, only ordinary model loads honored it. `serve -d` passes it to the detached child.
- **`hipfire ps` and `run`'s check for a running serve use the port in `serve.pid`.** A server on a non-default port used to show as "alive but HTTP is not ready", and `run` fell back to a local load.
- **The streamed `run` answer ends its line before the commit.** The daemon also drops its duplicate `[daemon-control] received …` stderr line (tracing still logs it), so stderr no longer runs onto the end of the answer.
- **An empty `hipfire list` now suggests `hipfire pull qwen3.5:4b`** instead of an 18.7 GB model.
- **The DFlash "not a registry-managed artifact" error names `--model-draft <draft.hfq>`** instead of `developer.dflash_draft`.
- **Unknown model tags in `pull`, `registry show`, `run` and `rm` list up to three close matches and `see: hipfire list -r`.** `rm` of a known tag that isn't downloaded says `<tag> is not downloaded`.
- **Print-only commands (`list`, `registry`, `config`, `ps`, `version`, `rm`) exit quietly on a closed pipe** (`hipfire list -r | head -1`) instead of panicking.
- **Removed config keys get the removal message from the CLI's own key lookups too.** That covers `config get` and `explain`, the per-model `config <tag> …` actions, and the `config list` paths, so a removed key now names the release that removed it instead of "unknown configuration key". `hipfire --help` now describes `config`.
- **Docs: the source-build command builds the daemon that exists.** GETTING_STARTED, NIXOS, `methodology/bench-suite.md` and `multi-gpu.md` said `cargo build --release --features deltanet --example daemon -p hipfire-runtime`, which fails (`no example target named daemon`); they now say `cargo build --release -p hipfire-daemon` (binary `target/release/daemon`).
- **Registry: the `qwen3.8:27b-draft-mq3`/`-mq4`/`-mq5`/`-mq6` entries no longer carry a `vision` block**, so pulling a draft no longer also fetches the 0.93 GB `qwen3.8-27b-vision.hfq` tower. `registry/v1.json` regenerated with `scripts/registry_gen.py`.
- **`hipfire config set` of a retired key names the removal.** `config set kernel.mw16 true` said `unknown configuration key 'kernel.mw16'`; it now says the key was removed in 0.4.1 and has no effect, matching the config-file warning (`hipfire_config::unknown_key`, `ConfigError::RetiredKey`).
- **Docs: `--max-seq` and `--spec` for `run`/`serve` documented as shipped (closes #821).** CLI.md listed `--max-seq` for `run` and `serve`, which did not exist; with the new flags, CLI.md and SERVE.md now give their bounds (512–1048576), that they replace the configured `memory.max_seq` / `speculation.mode` for the load, and `serve --spec off` for an MTP-off A/B.
- **Docs: thinking and `max_tokens`.** SERVE.md's chat example and the README quickstart now send `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false` and warn that `max_tokens` includes the `<think>` span, which otherwise returns `content: ""` with `finish_reason: length` to OpenAI clients; the GETTING_STARTED troubleshooting row says the same. README model counts reconciled to the registry (89 entries, 82 aliases; it said 82 and 80).
- **Docs: new [Pick a model for your GPU](docs/PICK-A-MODEL.md)** (VRAM tier → tag → speculation setting → historical tok/s, each number labelled with its BENCHMARKS.md fixture), linked from README and GETTING_STARTED.
- **Docs: opt-in community Qwen chat template.** `docs/CONFIG.md` (Chat template overrides) now notes that the community [froggeric Qwen chat template](https://huggingface.co/froggeric/Qwen-Fixed-Chat-Templates) (v22.5, Apache-2.0) can be used as an opt-in override via `hipfire config set prompt.chat_template <path>`, `HIPFIRE_CHAT_TEMPLATE_FILE`, or `~/.hipfire/templates/<model-basename>.j2`. It sets default effort to medium, preserves reasoning history by default, adds `<|think_on|>`/`<|think_off|>` toggles and extra tool-prompt guidance. The official template stays the default; overriding changes prompt tokens and can move speculative-decode acceptance and task scores.
- **Crate maps no longer churn on every PR.** `scripts/check-crate-maps.py` now generates only stable structure — each `src/**/*.rs` file with its sorted, uncapped exported symbols, plus dependency and reverse-dependency edges — and drops the per-file line, public-item and test counts and the totals line. `--check` still fails when a file is added or removed, an export changes, or a dependency edge changes; a body-only edit no longer needs a regen. All 50 `crates/*/map.md` regenerated; `docs/crate-maps.md` updated.
- **Qwen4 shape/inventory readiness (developer-only):** Flash-Next remains an exact production profile. Explicit synthetic profile metadata selects a typed dense or MoE FFN, optional PLE with declared row/hash geometry, and optional MTP for checked, header-only inventory planning. Synthetic profiles are refused by default artifact admission and all production config parsers; the real loader's developer admission requires an explicit `qwen4-dev-profiles` Cargo feature build (off by default, forwarded by the loader, generator and daemon). Dense fixture equations are non-upstream and do not establish Qwen4-27B compatibility or GPU execution support. The normalized `qwen_sparse_attention` spelling denotes the existing full-attention branch only with the required QSA fields.
- **PeaceMaker FP8 verify GEMMs for the 27B DFlash/MTP target verify on gfx1201 (`HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_FP8=1`, developer var, default off; a numerics change, not byte-identical to the F16 verify).** A new certified PeaceMaker family `kernels/mq4_fp8_verify_pm_gfx1201.hxaco` has 35 entries: `qkvza`, `qkv`, `gate_up`, `residual` and `set` (lm_head), each at tiles `bt1w1` (direct) and `bt{2,4,8}w{4,8}` (LDS-staged). M7 reports lift byte-exact, `obligations {}` and 0 ambiguous delays on every export. The kernels dequantize the symmetric MQ4G256V2 weights exactly to E4M3 and multiply them by per-row E4M3 activations on `v_wmma_f32_16x16x16_fp8_fp8`. The per-output reduction order is fixed for any row count, so batched rows are byte-identical to singleton rows by construction. Inside the scope every verify forward runs this family: singleton and CB, DFlash and MTP, the verify lm_head and the batched rollback replays. A lean single-pass activation pack produces bytes and scales bit-identical to `pack_f32_to_fp8_mq4v2_gfx12` scale_mode 1. With `=1` the scope fails closed: off-arch or asymmetric weights, an ineligible forward (tree verify, non-dense target, unsupported KV tier) or an F16-input GEMM reached inside the scope is an error, never a silent F16 route.
  - Byte oracle (R9700, real 27B weights, 2 seeds): 3,196 checks, 0 differing bytes. It covers N = 1..128, every tile, chunkings, one-row launches, permutations, HIP-graph replay and the pack. The CPU f64 contract reference agrees to a max relative L2 of 1.6e-7.
  - GEMM per verify step (six shapes + lm_head, gemm-only, median of 3 processes): N=128 is 47.0 ms against 69.1 ms for the exact F16 (K32 PM) verify, and N=16 is 21.1 ms against 25.2 ms.
  - Serve on one R9700 (`conc_steady.py`, 64 prompts × 256 tokens, greedy):
    - C8 DFlash (`HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1`): 239.8 → 289.6 tok/s.
    - C8 MTP: 249.8 → 256.4 tok/s.
    - C1 DFlash: 111.3 → 114.2 tok/s (two processes per arm, interleaved; the first FP8 pair read 110.7 / 107.9).
    - C1 MTP: 66.7 → 73.3 tok/s.
    - Batched FP8 text equals the FP8 singleton on 64/64 prompts for both DFlash and MTP. τ goes 4.019 → 4.029 (DFlash) and 2.091 → 2.095 (MTP).
  - Verify-logit KLD against F16 (512 windows × 16 rows): mean 1.15e-3, top-1 agreement 99.12 %.
  - Task evidence (27B accuracy suite, greedy DFlash, F16 → FP8):
    - HumanEval: 157 → 154 of 164. The 3 tasks only F16 passes are 33, 83 and 116; FP8 passes none that F16 fails.
    - GSM8K 5-shot: 1,282 → 1,280 of 1,319 (4 only F16 passes, 2 only FP8 passes).
    - MMLU-Pro subset: 871 → 871 of 1,400, with identical text on all 1,400 (direct one-letter answers).
    - In total F16 alone passes 7 items and FP8 alone passes 2, and HumanEval drops 1.8 points. The flag stays off by default.
- **`hipfire serve` adds the Anthropic Messages API (`POST /v1/messages`) and the OpenAI Responses API (`POST /v1/responses`), streaming and non-streaming, as adapters onto the chat-completions pipeline.** Both lower to the chat-completions body and then use the chat route's admission (same batching eligibility and model lease), tool gate, worker, Qwen tool-call parser, think routing and stop handling. A test checks that the daemon receives the same `generate` request as the matching chat request, so batching and the session cache behave the same.
  - Messages: `system`, multi-turn content blocks, `tool_use`/`tool_result` round trips, `thinking` (`budget_tokens` → `max_think_tokens`; thinking is off when absent, as in the Messages API), `stop_sequences`, `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k`, cache-aware usage (`cache_read_input_tokens`), Anthropic error shapes. The stream runs `message_start` → `content_block_*` → `message_delta` → `message_stop`.
  - Responses: `instructions`, `input` items (messages, `reasoning`, `function_call`, `function_call_output`), function tools, `reasoning.effort`, `text.format`, `max_output_tokens`, `status: incomplete` on length, and the `response.*` event stream with `sequence_number`.
  - Refused with 400 rather than silently changed: Messages prefill, server/hosted tools, `previous_response_id` (serve stores no responses).
  - Known gap: the daemon terminal doesn't name the stop sequence that matched, so a stop-sequence stop reports `end_turn` with `stop_sequence: null`. CORS now also allows the `x-api-key`, `anthropic-version` and `anthropic-beta` headers. See `docs/SERVE.md`.
- **Qwen4 developer tiny producer (`hipfire-quantize --qwen4-dev-tiny source-exact|packed`, `qwen4-dev-profiles` build only):** converts a local safetensors directory of a synthetic developer profile (dense or small MoE, with or without PLE/MTP) into one complete HFQM artifact planned from the typed profile manifest, reopened and admitted through `admit_hfqm_artifact` with exact predicted/actual extents before it is published. `packed` stores each role in the Flash-Next format its own reduction width tiles (Q8F16 embedding/trunk/dense FFN, MQ6G256V2 head, MQ4G256V2+MQ4G128V2 routed experts together, Q8F16 PLE rows) and BF16 elsewhere, selected by the explicit metadata recipe id `synthetic-packed` (new `Qwen4TierRecipe::SyntheticPacked`); a profile in which no role tiles any format (the width-16 tiny profiles) is refused rather than written. The Flash-Next route, its default manifest and every production parser are unchanged.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) 4-bit dense trunk: exact few-row verify and gfx1151 PeaceMaker prefill (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ4_ROWS_PM=0` / `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ4_X4_PM=0` kill them, developer vars, default on, byte-identical).** A trunk stored as MQ4G256V2 (qt 44; GDN in/out, QSA q/k/v/indexer/o) took the F16-activation WMMA `gemm_mq4g256v2` for MTP verify (2..8 rows), which is not the 1-row decode GEMV, so greedy MTP diverged from greedy AR (gfx1151, candidate artifact `qwen3.8-flash-next.mq4t-gptq.mq4`: the 1200-id `greedy_mtp_identity_hw` p1 batched arm differed from AR) and verify cost 3.96 ms/token. Verify rows now run `gemv_mq4g256v2_rows_x4_pm_gfx{1151,1201}` (embedded PeaceMaker images `kernels/mq4_rows_x4_pm_gfx{1151,1201}.hxaco`, four matrices per launch, each weight group dequantized once for all rows); every output is bitwise `gemv_mq4g256v2` on that row (oracle `mq4_rows_oracle`: rows 1..8 × every trunk shape × 3 seeds × eager and HIP graph, 624/624 on gfx1151 and gfx1201). `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ4_ROWS_PM=0` runs the per-row `gemv_mq4g256v2` launches instead (same bytes). On gfx1151 the MQ4 trunk prefill now shares one F16 rotation per K and runs `qwen4_mq4_x4_pm_gfx1151_*` (`kernels/qwen4_mq4_x4_pm_gfx1151.hxaco`: plain, BF16-out qkv and GDN output, a/b/z regions, out_proj/o_proj HC-write fusion), the MQ4 twins of the MQ6 trunk GEMMs, byte-identical to the hipcc `gemm_mq4g256v2_residual_wmma_gfx11_bt4` route they replace (oracle `qwen4_mq4_x4_pm_oracle`); `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MQ4_X4_PM=0` keeps hipcc bt4 and the unfused HC write. MQ6 trunks (the shipped pin) take exactly their old kernels.
  - Exactness: on the candidate, greedy MTP adaptive / batched / full-head draft head all equal greedy AR on the Halo (1200 ids, `ba08a137…`; earlier session also incremental and the 256-id seasons case); on the R9700 the daemon's greedy text with `--spec mtp` equals `--spec off` on 3/3 prompts (the identity harness does not fit the 124.6 GB model on one 32 GB card). The pin's WT2 KLD sequence and logits sha (`5023b1ea…`, q4 PLE default) are unchanged.
  - Strix Halo, candidate (`hipfire bench`, median of 3 runs, one fresh process per arm): greedy MTP decode 40.0 → 54.2 tok/s, pp8192 1,034 → 1,885 tok/s, AR 37.2 → 37.1 (noise). Pin unchanged: MTP 56.4 → 56.2, AR 33.8 → 33.8, pp8192 1,924 → 1,933 (medians of 4 interleaved processes per arm).
- **DFlash draft body: stream-ordered D2D copies and hoisted lane position uploads (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_ORDERED_DRAFT=0` kills it, developer var, default on, byte-identical).** The draft body's residual saves and context/noise K/V concatenation ran as synchronous null-stream `hipMemcpy` calls, each draining the queue from the host; they are now enqueued on the execution stream ahead of their consumer kernels, and the batched lane draft uploads its key positions before the body instead of between the lane fc GEMMs. Same bytes, same ranges; the ordered singleton and lane bodies also capture and replay as HIP graphs byte-identically. Exactness: eager on/off and lanes == singleton identical; `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (CHUNK128, PM) 83 lanes / 57.7 GB, 0 differing items vs origin/beta for both arms; serve battery and chain single and VMM C1+C8 45/45 identical to the base. Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on one R9700, ABBA fresh processes vs the base: steady C1 (64 prompts × 512) 129.3 → 133.9 tok/s (+3.5%), steady C8 with `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1` 275.0 → 283.9 (+3.2%), with no overlap between arms; HTTP code C1 237.2 → 256.0 (+3.2% against the base's fast mode, 248), and HTTP code C8 (one 14 s round, 8 runs per arm) neutral within its ±5% spread between processes (median 279.1 → 277.4, mean 274.6 → 277.7).
- **gfx1201 MQ4 prefill: the `HIPFIRE_GFX12_MQ4V2_FP8_{QKVZA,GATEUP,QKV,RESID}` opt-outs are now honoured by the FP8-stream producers (numerics fix on the opt-out routes only; the default and FP8 routes are byte-identical).** The fused rmsnorm / silu / sigmoid / GDN FP8-stream producers checked only `HIPFIRE_GFX12_FP8_STREAM`, eager mode and the shape. They wrote just the FP8 planes and left the f32 rotated input unwritten. A consumer whose family flag was 0 fell back to the F16 WMMA GEMM and read that stale input. Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts WT2 KLD (2 chunks) with `HIPFIRE_IU4_PREFILL=0` and all four families off was 11.74. With only GATEUP, QKVZA or QKV off it was 10.73, 7.23 or 1.74. `RESID=0` silently stayed FP8. One predicate now admits a producer only when its consumers take FP8, and the anticipating gate/up h-epilogue, FA gate-in-place and GDN scan-out reader use the same predicate. The all-families-off route measures 0.0307 over 2 chunks and 0.039451 over 24 chunks. The 24-chunk pins on card A4/E are unchanged: default `ff88c499…` (0.069571) and FP8 (`HIPFIRE_IU4_PREFILL=0`) `a4f68b12…` (0.041859).
- **VMM continuous batching: smaller DFlash lanes, so 8 lanes fit at 8K on a 32 GB R9700, and MTP lane state is charged in admission (byte-identical).** On Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 (`serve.vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size = 8`), each DFlash lane held 1,038,090,240 B of DFlash state, plus one 150,994,944 B DeltaNet checkpoint per 2,048 prompt rows, and the vacant singleton kept a 1.04 GB replacement DFlash state. At K=8, long8 (~4K prompts) ran 7 lanes + 1 queued and xl8 (~8K) ran 6 + 2. Changes:
  - Lanes are allocated as `DflashOwnerKind::VmmLane`: no FWHT rotation buffers (`mq_x_rot` / `mq_x_rot_f16`) and no verify `prefill_batch`. The batched verify already runs on the shared trunk scratch. The engine's one shared draft scratch owns the rotation buffers; the batched draft reads them, and every other lane draft call borrows them for the call (single stream; each GEMM call rotates into them before reading, and nothing reads them afterwards). A lane promoted from the singleton frees its own copies (`shed_vmm_lane_scratch`). Lane state: 1,038,090,240 → 805,306,368 B.
  - Lanes take no DeltaNet checkpoints while nothing on the lane side reads them (`DFLASH_LANE_CHECKPOINT_READERS`: spec lanes never keep conversations and have no rewind). Enabling either reader turns the checkpoints back on. The checkpoint bound follows the seed's chunk plan instead of `ceil(prompt / 2048)`.
  - After a promotion the singleton stays vacant and is rebuilt when its next prefill starts, so it no longer holds a replacement state while lanes own the GPU.
  - MTP lanes charge their probed state (`[daemon] VMM MTP lane footprint`, 276,824,064 B per lane on this model) in admission; before, only their KV and DeltaNet were counted. `[vmm-budget] resident lanes=N (high water)` logs each new residency peak.
  - Results on one R9700: long8 and xl8 at K=8 run all 8 lanes resident with no lane waiting, and the text equals K=1 (`60501870…`, `65fbe1ef…`). long8 92.82 → 101.54 tok/s; xl8 70.70 → 73.15 / 69.21 tok/s (two processes; same duration within noise, TTFT p50 12.9 → 17.1 s because all 8 prompts prefill up front). C8 on 64 short prompts, same text as the base: DFlash median 193.26 → 193.84 tok/s (4 base and 5 new processes; 2 new processes ran in a ~188 slow mode, which base processes also showed on another card at 176 vs 190), MTP 247.40 → 247.02 (one process each). `cb_vmm_state_oracle` passes for DFlash probe windows 0–2 (wide verify off, and on with PM) and the MTP singleton `ks 1,2,3,4` at 512/8K/32K. Lanes expect no checkpoints; every other state compare is unchanged.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) prefill: four exact kernel changes on gfx1151 (byte-identical). Strix Halo release-matrix Ciru 8K 1,944.9 → 1,964.8 tok/s (TTFT 4,209 → 4,166 ms, +1.0%, CS95 +0.4 to +1.7%; 3 paired holds, each a discarded warm-up cell then both arms); synthetic pp8192 1,961.5 → 1,993.4 tok/s (+1.6%; 3 fresh processes per arm, CS95 +0.6 to +2.6%).** The product-route logits, top-1 and every state byte after an 8192-row and a 2048-row prefill are identical to the previous build, and so are the WT2 `qwen4_kld` logits (sha256 `5023b1ea…`, 24 × 2048).
  - Shared expert: the gate and up GEMMs, the BF16-recipe SwiGLU with the selector sigmoid, and the hidden's F32 → F16 conversion now run as one launch of a certified PeaceMaker module (`kernels/qwen4_shared_gu_pm_gfx1151.hxaco`) instead of four. Each output keeps the incumbent GEMM's ascending-K16 WMMA chain and hipcc's activation instruction sequence; the shared down reads the F16 hidden directly. 2.42 → 1.80–1.95 ms per layer at 8192 rows (isolated). `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_SHARED_GU_PM=0` restores the four launches.
  - MQ6 PeaceMaker trunk GEMM (`qwen4_mq6_x4_pm_gfx1151`): the 6-bit codes are dequantized in pairs with `v_pk_add_f16` / `v_pk_fma_f16` (one rounding, same operands as the scalar `v_fma_f16`), 30 instead of 46 VALU per K-step. 10.91 → 10.57 ms at 10240 × 2560 × 8192 (isolated).
  - HC-write out-projection (`gemm_mq6g256v2_wmma_gfx11_u3_b8_r8_p2_hcw`, 8192-row chunks): each token's four HC gates are evaluated once per row and shared across its 16 channel items instead of once per item. 9.67 → 9.45 ms per call (isolated).
  - GDN convolution: the batched conv/parameter kernels launch a compact 1-D grid. At 8192 rows 784,896 of their 806,912 workgroups returned at once; the launch is now 22,016 workgroups.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4): PLE row tables as sidecar files, and 4-bit q4 rows by default on gfx1151 (`memory.ple_format` / `HIPFIRE_PLE_FORMAT`, `auto` = q4 on gfx1151 and q8 elsewhere; `q8` is the kill switch).** The PLE n-gram table can live next to the model as `<model>.ple-q8` (the exact Q8F16 rows) or `<model>.ple-q4` (4-bit Lloyd-Max rows, 28.8 GB; each 32-weight block is an f16 scale plus 16 bytes of labels). The loader binds a sidecar to its base by hash and refuses a mismatch.
  - `hipfire ple derive --format q4 <model>` writes the rank-ordered `.ple-q4` from the local Q8 rows atomically in about 1 minute on Strix Halo.
