A `q4` load also derives it when it is missing. `hipfire ple split <model>` turns a monolithic model into a base plus `.ple-q8`. `hipfire pull <tag> --ple auto|q4|q8|all|none` picks the sidecars of a split registry entry. `auto` never derives and never downloads; when the preferred file is absent it falls back to the other format with a note.
  - Exactness: the real `.ple-q4` WT2 KLD logits equal the q4 emulation's (sha256 `23526fdf…`, KLD 0.104865 vs q8 0.103252). Kaden approved the q4 numerics. Greedy MTP and serve-battery text read coherent.
  - Strix Halo Ciru 8K (one run each): the first request after ready with the q4 table cold ran at 1,653.2 tok/s (TTFT 4.95 s), vs ~1,553 for the shipped Q8 default. Steady after the default prewarm ran at 1,828.4 tok/s, vs ~1,599. Natural 8K steady 1,840.2, agentic 8K 1,832.7. That is level with the opt-in Q8 hot-row store (1,785–1,875) at ~6 GB less RAM. The table's misses are read by the row store's 256-reader pool from one ascending cursor: 130,976 rows in 111,696 reads, 858 ms cold.
  - `memory.ple_prewarm_bytes` (`HIPFIRE_PLE_PREWARM_BYTES`): after ready, while no request runs, the q4 table is prewarmed into the page cache, hottest tier first. `auto` = 15 GiB, capped at `MemAvailable` − 8 GiB. Prewarming the whole 26.8 GiB table with ~24 GiB available pushed reclaim into the request, and Ciru 8K steady fell to 1,734.5 tok/s.
  - `memory.ple_load_fill_bytes` (`HIPFIRE_PLE_LOAD_FILL_BYTES`, default `auto` = 14 GiB, `0` = off): the hottest prefix of a rank-ordered q4 table is read into the page cache **while the model loads**, at ≤ 0.2 GB/s. Pages evicted meanwhile are found with `mincore` and read again, hottest first. Ready never waits for it, and the idle prewarm after ready continues where it stopped. 14 GiB is B* of the offline row-hit curve (`plehot/hcurve_q4.py`): the smallest prefix whose modelled held-out prose and agentic 8K prefill is within 1% of the 15 GiB prewarm's. On unified memory the Qwen4 weight upload now drops each range chunk from the page cache once uploaded (the HFQ file's existing `evict_page_cache` policy; PLE row reads keep their pages), so this load's weight bytes no longer evict the fill. Strix Halo, one fresh process each, in the same session as beta `662c7b21d`: load took 39.8–48.8 s vs 49.8–69.0 s. First request after ready: Ciru 8K 1,713.6 tok/s (beta 1,622–1,633), prose 8K 1,649.0 (1,619–1,645), agentic 8K 1,720.9 (1,693.9). Steady Ciru 8K 1,883.3 (1,866.9–1,886.9). Greedy AR token ids equal beta's. The first request still runs below steady: kswapd reclaims 0.9–6.4 GB of file pages during it even with ~30 GB free, and with the weight pages gone the fill's pages are the ones reclaimed (4.3–6.7 GiB resident at ready, 2.0–4.0 GiB after the request).
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4): opt-in PLE hot-row store. The most-used rows of the per-layer-embedding table are read from a rank-ordered copy (the hot-row pack) through the page cache, so a cold prompt reads fewer of them from the SSD, and the memory they use stays reclaimable. Unified memory, `memory.ple_hot_store_bytes` / `HIPFIRE_PLE_HOT_STORE_BYTES`, default `0` = off; byte-identical.** A prefill chunk fetches about 110K distinct 170-byte PLE rows from the model file, about 1.0–1.1 s per cold 8K prompt on Strix Halo's QLC NVMe.
  - `hipfire-quantize ple-hot-rank` ranks rows by how many 8,192-token corpus windows use them and writes a `<model>.plehot` bucket sidecar.
  - `hipfire-quantize ple-hot-pack` copies the rows of the hottest buckets verbatim from the model into `<model>.plehotrows` (default 40 GiB), tier by tier, so the hot rows share OS pages: 24 hot rows per 4 KiB page instead of 24 random-rank rows. The pack header types the row encoding (Q8F16 today), so other row formats can be packed the same way.
  - Rows the pack holds are read from it, others from the model file; both decode through the same row decoder. Before serving, a worker checks the sidecar hash and compares 4,096 pack rows with the model. After ready it prewarms the pack into the page cache while no request runs, hottest tier first, then reads the hottest tier a second time so its pages are promoted to the kernel's active list and stay resident when the cache is reclaimed: `auto` = `MemAvailable` − 4 GiB, an explicit N is N bytes.
  - This replaces the anonymous-RAM store from earlier in this release. Under memory pressure the kernel swapped that store to the same NVMe the PLE reads use, which caused the 737–1,476 tok/s Ciru 8K outliers. In the 1,233 run, ~6 GB of the store was in swap, and the request took ~46K swap-ins and ~26K major faults. Its hits were also decoded serially before the misses were issued: 145 ms of a 364 ms fetch. With a full 29.7 GiB store, steady Ciru 8K was 1,832.9 tok/s serial and 1,880.5 with the hits decoded in parallel with the misses; natural 8K 1,894.8, agentic 8K 1,858.2. The pack design keeps that parallel hit path, and page-cache pages are dropped, never swapped.
  - The row store's readers now take source page groups from one ascending cursor, so the reads in flight stay clustered on the DRAM-less NVMe. 256-thread buffered preads of the Ciru 8K pages run at 112K vs 104K IOPS. The base cold Ciru 8K fetch went 1,075 → 984 ms (1,579.5 → 1,613.7 tok/s, one process each).
- **Cohere2-MoE (North-Mini-Code) over `hipfire serve` no longer fails every request:** `generate_cohere2moe` never emitted `gen_start`, so the CLI's stream latch rejected the first token ("stream must begin with gen_start; got token before contract latch") and each chat completion ended empty or with HTTP 500. The route now opens the contract after prompt validation, like DS4 and LFM2-MoE. North MQ4 greedy battery on gfx1151: 5/5 turns non-empty, transcript byte-identical to the pre-seal `090ec1731` daemon carrying the same one-call patch.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4): appending to a 192K or 256K prompt reuses the prompt again instead of re-prefilling everything past 122,880 tokens. Strix Halo Flash Race prefill grid, +512 tokens: @192K 9.3 → 141.8 tok/s, @256K 4.7 → 197.5 tok/s (Oct 7 RC3 prefix cache: 142.3 / 198.4).** An engine session-cache snapshot copies one 8192-token chunk of QSA rows (513 MiB), so the default 8 GiB budget held the first 15 chunks and skipped every deeper boundary. The append then re-prefilled 69,132 / 133,132 tokens. The cache now also keeps a *resident* snapshot: the fixed state (GDN recurrent and conv, QSA selections, PLE and hyper feedback, the MTP head's fixed parts) at the deepest boundary of the last committed prefill. It is restored over the rows the live state still holds, so it needs no row copy and is not bounded by the budget. Any `begin` that does not restore it drops it, and `Qwen4Bundle::reset` reports outside resets (`SessionCache::live_state_reset`). `plan` probes the pool only above it. Restores are byte-identical to a cold prefill (`session_cache_hw::resident_restore_matches_cold_on_flash_next`, AR and native MTP, pool too small for any snapshot), and the serve A/B/A greedy transcripts at 64K are identical with 8 GiB and 1 GiB budgets. The +512/+4096 @0K and cold @120K drops in the same race were noise: on reruns the unchanged `a582af8fc` build gave 930–949 / 1291–1337 tok/s @0K (Oct 7: 942.7 / 1301.7).
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) MTP prompt fill no longer blocks the host on per-sub-chunk token-id uploads (gfx1151; byte-identical). Strix Halo Ciru 8K 1,518.7 → 1,557.1 tok/s (TTFT 5,390 → 5,257 ms).** The batched head append runs asynchronously, but each 1,024-row sub-chunk uploaded its ids with a blocking `hipMemcpy`, so an 8K prompt held the host for 7 of its 8 sub-chunks before token 0. The chunk's ids now upload once after the target forward's argmax readback into a drafter-owned buffer, the head's VMM QSA coverage is mapped through the chunk once, and the pending-hidden copy is stream-ordered. Same kernels, same order, same id bytes. Greedy AR/MTP ids identical; decode 56.66 → 56.63 tok/s (3 interleaved processes, noise); WT2 `qwen4_kld` logits `6018690d…` unchanged.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4): a large PLE row request decodes its rows into the lease on the reader threads and caches its pages after the ticket completes. Byte-identical; Strix Halo Ciru 8K: removes the 118 ms serial publish from the chunk-0 host wait.** After the up-to-256 readers joined, `RowStore` published every coalesced group serially under the state mutex: the requested rows were decoded into the output one group at a time and each page was copied into the cache, 118 ms for the ~111K groups of an 8K chunk, with the waiter blocked. Each reader now decodes its groups' requested rows straight into the lease (the rows' output ranges are disjoint, so the threads share the buffer without a lock), and the ticket completes when the reads join. The worker then inserts the pages into the cache, in the same group order and under the same 256 MiB bound, before it takes the next ticket, so a following request still sees them and a quiesce still waits for them. Cancellation still caches the pages that were read; a failed read still fails the ticket; hit, miss and read counters keep their meaning. Rows, cache contents and eviction order are unchanged.
- **VMM continuous batching admits long-context lanes by device memory instead of failing the batch:** on an R9700 (Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2, `serve.vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size = 8`), 8 concurrent ~4K or ~8K prompts failed every request with `VMM admission refused: mapping 4066 positions needs 67108864 B, budget 0 B`. The staged KV budget was not what ran out: 1.4 GB of KV was mapped against a 14.3 GB budget. The device-free term hit zero (88 MB free, 384 MB pooled, 512 MiB reserve). Besides its KV, each DFlash lane holds 1.04 GB of DFlash state, 151 MB per DeltaNet checkpoint and 120 MB of DeltaNet state, and admission charged none of it. Lanes were admitted until device memory was gone, and the next 64 MiB KV chunk a lane needed was refused, which failed the whole batch. Changes:
  - The map room now counts the pool's parked buffers (a VMM map returns them to HIP first).
  - Each lane records the KV end it may grow to (`prompt + max_tokens + 64`).
  - The DFlash engine probes one lane's state and one checkpoint at staging (`[daemon] VMM DFlash lane footprint`).
  - The inbox head stays queued (`[vmm-admit] … waits for device memory`), not refused, while admitting it would leave the admitted lanes unable to finish (a banker's check over every lane's remaining KV growth). A lane whose next KV chunk would break that check waits a step, as a lane over the row budget does. No arithmetic changes, so output is byte-identical.
  - Results, same card: long8 (3780–4354-token prompts, 640 tokens) at K=8 ran 7 lanes + 1 queued, and xl8 (7651–8316-token prompts, 512 tokens) ran 6 + 2 queued. Both completed 8/8 with text equal to K=1. DFlash and MTP C8 on 64 short prompts (3 fresh processes per arm, interleaved with the base): median 189.77 → 194.59 and 250.79 → 250.40 tok/s, the same text, and no lane waited.
- **Serve gateway admits multi-turn histories on every exact batch route by typed capability (fix):** with `serve.vmm_batch` on a Flash-Next model the load ack reports `continuous_batch_route: "fn-lanes"`, but the CLI gateway only recognized the route name `vmm`, so appended-history (`[user, assistant, user]`) requests were serialized before reaching the daemon's lane predicate and only first turns could batch. Both exact routes (qwen35 `vmm`, qwen4 `fn-lanes`) now publish `continuous_batch_history_capable: true` in the load ack, and the gateway admits histories from that flag (together with `continuous_batch_capable`) instead of comparing route names. Routes without the flag keep the single-user-turn rule; unit test `ack_history_capability_is_typed_not_route_named`. Flash-Next lane done events now also carry `execution_mode: "continuous_batch_independent"` and the `continuous_batch` lane block, as the other batch drivers' do. Strix Halo (gfx1151, q4 PLE, AR, 4 conversations × 3 turns at 2,048-token depth, K=4, one run per arm): all 12 responses have the same text md5 on the lane arm as on the singleton arm; 6 of the 8 turn-2/3 requests ran batched (the other two arrived alone and took the singleton route), and the workload took 38.3 → 28.1 s.
- **Sampled requests on the exact VMM batch route (`serve.batch_sampled` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_BATCH_SAMPLED`, default on with `serve.vmm_batch`, `0` is the kill switch, singleton-exact; oracle PASS: 2000 trials/arm under penalties; MTP C8 == C1 48/48, DFlash 48/48; BetterBench C1 → C8 MTP 58.8 → 186.9, DFlash 95.9 → 163.2 tok/s):** temperature>0 requests used to serialize on the singleton route (`is_vmm_batch_request_eligible` ended in `temp <= 0`), so a sampled C8 workload ran at C1 speed. With the flag on, a sampled request batches in the lane mode its singleton route selects, byte-identical to that singleton for the same wire seed: AR lanes (singleton seed/window rules; promotion only through the AR boundary, which moves the advanced RNG and history), native MTP lanes (singleton sampled first token on the lane's `MtpRng`; sampled drafts batched per depth over one column-identical lm_head, one row-independent softmax and one readback per depth; singleton sampled accept), and FAST_SAMPLE selector-chain DFlash lanes (singleton first token, selector draft, target rows, rejection loop and decision→commit factored into shared seams; each sampled lane's head runs Product at the singleton's own block). One capability decision drives eligibility, promotion and lane admission; anything a lane cannot serve in its own mode stays singleton, never an AR substitute, and sampled speculative singletons are not promoted. The VMM AR executor's head sampling now enqueues every lane's singleton sampler kernels (`batch_sampler::sample_batch`; one H2D, one D2H) and the penalty prepass finds (last index, count) by an LDS sort (bit-identical; 0.23 ms instead of 5.5–7.4 ms for 8 rows at window 2048). Receipts: load ack `continuous_batch_sampling` / `continuous_batch_sampled_modes`, per request `continuous_batch.{spec_mode,sampling,equivalence}`. Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts on one R9700, BetterBench corpus at T0.7 / top-p 0.95 / top-k 20 / seed 42, 48 requests, one process per cell: all 48 C8 texts are identical to C1 in every mode; aggregate tok/s C1 → C8 is AR 40.6 → 143.6, MTP 58.7 → 199.3, DFlash (chunk128 on) 77.0 → 150.5 (singleton-serialized C8 was ≈ C1).
  - A sampled request whose singleton mode has no batch lane never reaches the batch with its start announced. The daemon now runs the admission's lane decision on the rendered prompt before enqueueing (`vmm_request_fits_batch`): a sampled MTP/DFlash request whose prompt + `max_tokens` exceeds the staged lane engine's cap (MTP head 32,768) while the singleton speculator still fits it takes the singleton route. Previously such a request (the serve harness's default `max_tokens` 81,920) was admitted, could not be handed back once announced, and returned HTTP 400 "sampled request cannot be served exactly by the VMM batch route" (5 of 40 C8 requests). A think budget or stop strings likewise keep a sampled spec request singleton at eligibility. `scripts/serve_sampled_batch_gate.sh` runs that C1+C8 battery and requires HTTP 200 for every request.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) batch-invariant prefill profile (`HIPFIRE_PREFILL_PM_INVARIANT=1`, developer var, default off, gfx1151 only):** the prefill forward routed many kernels by the row count of the forward (the 8 / 512 / 2048 / `x_src_rows >= 5120` thresholds), so a row's bytes depended on how many other rows shared its forward. With the profile on, every row-count routing decision of a qwen4 prefill forward is taken as if the forward had 8192 rows (the gfx1151 default chunk, above every threshold) while launches keep the real row count; at 8192-row chunks it equals the product route byte for byte, short chunks and appends move to the long-route arithmetic, and the dense IU4 trunk route is off under it. Decode, MTP verify and the MTP head never take it. A lab arm `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_GDN_PREFILL=wy|persistent|canon` selects the prefill GDN recurrence; `canon` adds the token-canonical Q8 kernel `gated_delta_step_halves_state128_persistent256_canon_q8`. With both unset every route is unchanged.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) lane step: gfx1151 PeaceMaker multi-column row kernels (`HIPFIRE_FN_LANE_PM_ROWS`, default on for gfx1151, `0` is the kill switch, byte-identical to the 1-row incumbents, lane step only; Halo C8 +21.6 % tok/s):** the four MQ6 (`gemm_mq6g256v2_f32_rows`, `_rows_x4`) and Q8_0 (`gemv_q8_0_k8_rows`, K = 320 `gemv_q8_0_staged_rows`) rows launchers re-read the 8 activation columns once per weight row. On exact gfx1151, inside the lane step (`Gpu::lane_rows_scope`) and outside recording/capture, the flag routes them at 2..=8 rows to the embedded builder module `qwen4_lane_rows_pm` (`kernels/qwen4_lane_rows_pm_gfx1151.hxaco`), which shares each activation load across 4 (MQ6, k8) or 8 per wave (k320) weight rows while every column keeps the arithmetic DAG of the 1-row kernel. Oracles on Strix Halo: `lane_rows_pm_oracle` (15 shapes × B = 1..8, 225 launches, 0 differing bytes against the 1-row incumbent and the incumbent rows kernel; at B = 8 the PM kernels are 2.5× (MQ6 x4), 1.9× (lm_head), 6.7× (k8) and 1.3× (k320) faster), and `fn_lane_oracle` with the flag on (AR, MTP cycle with its controls, MTP floor at 512–8192 context: every lane byte-identical to its singleton). Qwen3.8-Flash-Next gptq3 (q4 PLE), `fn_lane_oracle --bench` at 8 lanes: AR lanes 82.6 tok/s and floor-routed MTP lanes 82.5 tok/s (2.47× the 33.4 tok/s AR singleton). The first `conc_steady` C8 A/B (before the row-spill AR floor below) read 64.6 → 72.8 tok/s with C8 TTFT p50 825 → 1,355 ms, which kept the flag off. On one binary with that floor, `conc_steady` C8 (`serve.vmm_batch`, 64 prompts × 256 tokens, one fresh process each, PM off and on interleaved): PM off 65.91 / 63.40 / 66.35 tok/s, TTFT p50 847 / 905 / 1,006 ms (p90 2,413 / 2,626 / 2,377); PM on 78.98 / 79.42 / 79.42 / 79.30 tok/s, TTFT p50 978 / 996 / 953 / 976 ms (p90 2,402 / 2,327 / 2,282 / 2,308); identical text md5 in every run. `predict ab`: tok/s win +21.6 % (CS95 [+6.5 %, +38.8 %]); TTFT p50 unresolved at this noise (PM on +6 % point estimate, CS95 ±40 %), every PM-on p50 inside the PM-off range and the 662–1,365 ms process-to-process spread of one binary. Peacemaker codec fix: a gfx11 MUBUF OFFSET decodes as an unsigned 12-bit field.
- **Sampled speculative decoding samples AR's exact law (distribution fix):** the FAST_SAMPLE truncation kernel `softmax_temp_topp_batched_f32` used a different law from AR's sampler (`llama::sample_top_k_p`). It took the nucleus over the FULL vocab mass and intersected it with a 512-bucket top_k count, so when top_k bound, it kept more tokens than AR (a Zipf row at T0.7 / top_p 0.95 / top_k 20: AR keeps 11, the kernel kept 25), and tokens ranked past k could be kept. Its consumers were: the DFlash acceptance rows, the GPU `chain_accept_spec` bonus, the DFlash C8 draft sampler, the qwen35 MTP verify and draft rows, and the n-gram `spec_impl` picks. Absent top_k also meant "no cap" in these speculators, where AR uses a 20-token pool. The kernel now radix-selects the exact top-`cap` probabilities, then applies min_p and the nucleus over the pool's own mass, as AR does. The DFlash, MTP, DFlash-CB and n-gram speculators now pass the AR candidate cap (`llama::ar_candidate_cap` / `SpecRequestConfig::ar_candidate_cap`: absent → 20, 0 → 64, k → min(k, 64)), and every sampled DFlash row always truncates. The host (FAST_SAMPLE-off) rows apply the same law (`apply_host_ar_trunc`). Greedy decoding is unaffected. Oracle: `crates/hipfire-arch-qwen35/tests/sampled_dflash_bonus_distribution_hw.rs` (HIP GPU, `--ignored`). On fixed rows, including a binding-top_k Zipf case, a ranked-past-k near tie, a min_p case and the absent-top_k pool, the kernel's kept set equals AR's support exactly. Every consumer's draws pass chi-square goodness-of-fit and homogeneity against AR. The pre-fix kernel law and the pre-fix bonus both fail.
  - Cost (gfx1201, card A, vocab 248,320, T0.7 / top_p 0.95 / top_k 20): the truncation kernel goes 287 → 349 µs per call at 1 row and 336 → 485 µs at 16 rows, averaged over 300 calls and reproduced in a second pass. End to end on Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts, `hipfire serve` at the same sampling, 3 fresh processes per arm interleaved with the base `e9044be`, 5 prompts × 256 tokens: sampled DFlash median 76.63 → 76.27 tok/s (×0.995) and sampled native MTP 59.18 → 58.49 tok/s (×0.988). Both are within the 4–5 % spread between processes. Greedy DFlash serve output is byte-identical to the base (2 prompts × 256 tokens, same τ).
- **DFlash draft sampler rows draw independent uniforms (distribution fix):** `batched_categorical_sample_f32` seeded each row as `(seed ^ row) | 1`. That gave draft rows 2k and 2k+1 the same uniform, and adjacent rows lattice-spaced draws, so the draft tokens of one block were coupled (oracle: row-pair rank correlation +1.0). The kernel now takes a per-row seed buffer. The host derives each seed with `rdna_compute::sampling::spec_draft_row_seed(stream, position, row)`, a hash of the request's sampler stream at the window start, the absolute window position, and the row. It's deterministic per request and independent of co-scheduled lanes. The oracle checks the draws' chi-square against AR, and that the row-pair and cross-window correlations stay within 5/√n.
- **Sampled DFlash bonus honours top_p / top_k (distribution fix):** with `HIPFIRE_DFLASH_FAST_SAMPLE` on (the default), the host accept loop truncated each acceptance row with the GPU nucleus cutoff, but the full-accept / budget-bound bonus was drawn from the raw temperature softmax. So whenever the host loop ran (PLD drafts, the candidate selector, or a budget that binds mid-window), bonus tokens could fall outside the requested top_p / top_k. Every sampled target read in the loop (accept test, rejection residual, bonus) now goes through one row transform (`speculative::sampled_target_row_into`). The GPU `chain_accept_spec` path already truncated its bonus. Greedy decoding is unaffected. Oracle: `sampled_bonus_draws_from_the_acceptance_row_law` (no GPU; the pre-fix raw-row bonus fails it).
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) exact AR lane batching (experimental, default off, byte-identical per request):** with `serve.vmm_batch` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_VMM_BATCH=1` and `serve.continuous_batch_size >= 2`, concurrent greedy text-chat requests on the Qwen3.8 Flash-Next model share one trunk step over per-request lane states (QSA K/V, GDN, PLE history) on the one resident weight set. Stateful stages run per lane on the lane's own state; row-local stages run once over all lanes' rows only where the G0 oracle proved byte identity. Committed ids and state bytes per request equal the singleton route. The route refuses tools, images, logprobs and penalties, and a lone request keeps the singleton route. The loaded ack reports `continuous_batch_route: "fn-lanes"`, `continuous_batch_exact: true`, `continuous_batch_sampling: "greedy_only"` and the `fn_lanes_*` lane-store receipt. `ContinuousBatchRoute::Qwen4` and the qwen4 `supports_continuous_batch` cap are on, staged only through the VMM branch; `HIPFIRE_SERVE_VMM_BATCH=0` keeps today's serial behavior. No measured speed claim.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) MTP lanes (experimental, byte-identical per lane):** the exact lane route now runs per-lane native MTP windows by default when the MTP head is loaded: a step-synchronous batched draft for every drafting lane, then one `(d+1)`-row verify per lane inside the shared lane step, each window byte-identical to the one singleton window it takes (a batched window at the lane's draft depth, or the singleton's AR step), with a per-lane AR floor that retires a lane whose drafts do not pay. `serve.batch_spec = false` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_BATCH_SPEC=0` keeps the AR lanes. The loaded ack reports `continuous_batch_spec`, `continuous_batch_spec_mode` and `continuous_batch_spec_k`, and each lane's `done` carries `mtp_windows`, `ar_windows`, `mtp_retired`, `mtp_drafts`, `mtp_accepted`.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) promotion of a running singleton into the lanes (experimental, byte-identical per request):** with the lane route staged, a request that starts alone still runs the unchanged native-MTP singleton. When a batched peer queues behind it, the singleton now moves into a lane at its next committed window boundary instead of finishing first, as the qwen35 VMM route already did (developer reference: `HIPFIRE_VMM_PROMOTE_AT=<n>`). The resident trunk state, MTP head state and draft-request state swap into a free lane, with one device copy of the drafter's pending hidden. The lane's MTP floor starts from the singleton's agreement and retirement. The resident owners are reset for the next singleton request, and the lane skips its first forwarded token, which was already streamed. Promotion runs in three steps: `Qwen4Bundle::prepare_promotion` (fallible, touches only the lane), then `abort_promotion` or `commit_promotion`. It is refused when the resident ring is armed or a snapshot ticket is open. Oracle: `fn_lane_oracle --spec mtp --promote 4 --controls` on Strix Halo, 2 and 4 lanes at 512/2048 context. Request 0 runs 4 singleton windows and is then promoted next to lanes that are still prefilling. Every window's committed ids and the trunk, head, pending-hidden and draft-request bytes equal the uninterrupted singleton. The zeroed-pending-hidden negative control is detected, the skip-rollback control is detected and the positive cycle control matches.
- **Flash-Next (qwen4) MTP lanes: the lane AR floor prices row-group spill (perf, byte-identical; Halo C8 72.5 → 79.2 tok/s):** each lane's floor priced its windows from a per-lane-count fit of the step wall, `W = α_C + m_C·e` (e = verified drafts). At C8 every lane drafts, so e barely varies, and most of a window's cost is the extra 8-row group it spills into (the lane step runs row-local stages in groups of at most `LANE_MAX_ROWS = 8`; on a C8 serve trace 8 AR lanes took 91.7 ms and the same step with one 2.7-draft window 137.4 ms). α and m traded off against each other: `window_units(8, 3)` swung between 1.16 and 5.42 over the trace, and 37 of 64 requests kept drafting for their whole life although a verify row costs about what an AR lane does. The store now fits one pooled, decayed ridge model `W = F + a·C + r·e + G·s` (C decoding lanes, s = row groups beyond the first, counted with the lane step's own packing), with the prior F 16.9, a 9.7, r 8.2, G 18.5 ms from the gfx1151 C2 and C8 step traces, and prices a window of d drafts at `1 + (r·d + G·spill share) / (α(C)/C)` AR-row units. At C8 lanes now retire to AR after their probe windows; at C2 (no spill) they keep MTP. Routing changes only which exact window a lane takes, never its bytes. Halo, Qwen3.8 Flash-Next q4 PLE, `conc_steady` 64 prompts × 256 tokens, PM rows on, fresh processes: C8 72.5 → 79.2 tok/s (interleaved A/B, TTFT p50 ≈ 1.0 s), C4 72.5 (70.1 before, s7b), C2 72.5 (unchanged), C1 57.4 (the singleton, untouched), so C8 = 1.38× C1. Text md5 identical in every run. Developer diagnostic: `HIPFIRE_CB_PHASES=1` prints one `[fn-lane-step]` line per lane step (host gap, draft, forward, commit and emit ms; rows by kind; committed tokens).
- **DFlash chain verify: the HIP-graph verify now matches eager byte for byte (exactness fix, gfx1200/gfx1201):** the captured DFlash verify forward sized its batched attention by the KV physical capacity, so every replayed window ran the flash tile + reduce attend while the eager verify (and the exact VMM batched route) at context ≤ 4096 ran the single-pass LDS kernel `attention_{fp8_e4m3,q8_0}_kv_batched`. The fp32 reduction orders differ: the first difference was layer 3's attention output at ~1e-6 relative, and greedy text diverged on 10 of 64 code prompts. DFlash chain-verify blocks (`DflashFusionCtx::ChainVerify`, `DispatchCtx::verify_tile_attend`) now always take the tile + reduce (on gfx1201, the VerifyAttn twin), whose output does not depend on `max_ctx_len`. Eager and graph are therefore identical, and the batched route's fp8 scalar segment twin no longer applies to those blocks. MTP verify, tree verify and every other workload keep the crossover and are unchanged. At B=16 the LDS kernel was 3.8–23× slower (ctx 64–4096). Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on an R9700 (fp8 KV, 64 code prompts, 256 tokens): eager DFlash C1 105.1 → 110.6 tok/s (now equal to the graph's 111.3 and byte-identical to it), DFlash C8 189.8 → 194.1 tok/s. Default greedy DFlash text changes on these arches (it now equals the former HIP-graph text); batched = singleton holds.
- **`hipfire bench --concurrency` measures the serve route with a vLLM-style closed loop:** the bench used to load the batch backend without `serve_vmm_batch`, so it measured the legacy fixed-slot route `hipfire serve` never runs. It also timed one EOS-terminated burst of K raw-prompt requests (`repeat_penalty` 1.1, prompt 0 alone at K=1, no nonce on the batch driver), warmed up only at the smallest K, and its `multiturn` arm on the batch backend reran the stateless burst. Now the `batch` backend loads `continuous_batch_size` plus the `serve_vmm_batch` object serve builds from `serve.batch_spec`/`batch_nonexact`/`max_batch_tokens`/`prefill_min_tokens` (shared `VmmBatchFlags::load_params`), and refuses a daemon that did not stage `continuous_batch_route: "vmm"`. At every K, K lanes drain the same N requests, after a discarded warmup at that K. Requests are serve-shaped chat turns with neutral penalties and a per-run nonce: every K within one `--runs` round sends the same prompt tokens (so text and τ are comparable across K), later rounds get a fresh nonce, and request ids stay unique per sample. The bench reports output tok/s, steady-window tok/s, TTFT/TPOT p50/p99, achieved concurrency, τ, finish reasons, routes and cached prompt tokens. `noslots` queues the lanes client-side behind one daemon request, and `multiturn` sends a real follow-up turn on the same lane. `--bench-shape steady` (default: the 0.4.2 concurrency audit's 64 code prompts, 256 tokens, greedy) or `betterbench` (BetterBench 0.6.0's 48-request concurrency workload over `--bb-corpus DIR`), plus `--num-prompts` and `--temperature`. `--max-tokens` now defaults to 128 on the standard bench and 256 on `steady`. Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts, native MTP, `vmm_batch`, `continuous_batch_size` 8, one R9700, same daemon binary: the bench's K=1/2/4/8 read 65.8 / 118.2 / 184.3 / 246.8 tok/s (τ 2.082, all 64 requests finished on length, identical text md5 at every K), against the audit harness `conc_steady.py`'s 66.6 / 110.2 (a slow-mode outlier) / 186.0 / 247.5 (τ 2.091). With the nonce off, the bench's text md5 equals the harness's. See [docs/CLI.md](docs/CLI.md#bench-concurrency---concurrency).
- **`HIPFIRE_DEVICES` honored by every `Gpu::init` entry point:** examples, tests and bins that open the GPU through `rdna_compute::Gpu::init`/`init_with_device` used HIP device 0 regardless of `HIPFIRE_DEVICES`. `Gpu::init_with_device` now resolves a set `HIPFIRE_DEVICES` (index, `gfxNNNN`, `GPU-<uuid>`, PCI BDF) through the daemon's `hardware.devices` resolver before the HIP runtime loads (`hipfire_config::devices::apply_entry_point_device_visibility`, no card lock taken) and fails when it is unresolvable; unset keeps device 0, and a process that already applied visibility (the daemon) is not re-applied. `eval_hipfire`'s per-binary resolver is removed in favour of this common path.
- **QSA incremental pool/RoPE grid sized by rows, not context (byte-identical):** every incremental QSA pool/RoPE launch (each Qwen4/Flash-Next decode token and prefill chunk) used `grid.x = ceil(max_seq / compress)`. At the automatic `max_seq` 262144 on unified-memory APUs that is 65,536 workgroups, of which only 1–2 did any work. The grid is now `ceil(rows / compress)` and the kernel offsets each block by `first_block`, which is already a replay-declared kernarg, so retained PM4 programs and HIP graphs still see a constant grid per launch shape. The full (non-incremental) pool is unchanged. Flash-Next on Strix Halo (gfx1151), `HIPFIRE_GRAPH=1`, automatic 262144 context: C1 decode recovers to the 32768-context speed (median 63.1 tok/s over 5 fresh processes, against 60.0 for the 32768 override in the same session), with byte-identical text. Greedy token IDs are identical to beta for AR and native MTP at 262144 context on gfx1151 (automatic) and gfx1201 (`memory.max_seq = 262144`; discrete cards default to 32768).
- **Exact 64–128-row DFlash verify chunks for VMM continuous batching (`HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_CHUNK128=1`, developer var, default off, byte-identical):** the aggregated DFlash verify trunk was capped at 63 rows per chunk, so 8 lanes of 16-row blocks needed two chunk forwards. With the flag on, exact gfx1201 dense MQ4G256V2 targets run up to 128 rows per chunk through the explicit `gemm_*_verify_exact` kernels; `HIPFIRE_CB_VERIFY_PM` (default on inside the feature) selects the embedded PeaceMaker twins and `0` runs the byte-identical hipcc `_k32` twins. Qwen3.8-27B mq4-xts + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 on gfx1201, HTTP, one card, on/off per process: DFlash C8 code 238.8 → 282.6 tok/s (+18.4%, per-process ratios 1.1812/1.1915/1.1831), short-prompt C8 +24.4%, C4 +11.6%, C1/C2 unchanged (−0.00%/−0.12%); decoded text and τ byte-identical between arms. Covers DFlash only, not MTP (MTP C8 unchanged, −0.23%, within noise). Evidence: `cb_verify_gemm_oracle` (kernel bytes) and the extended `cb_vmm_state_oracle` (all aggregates, wide off/HIP/PM), serve battery/chain at C8 with 0 mismatches.
- **Unified-memory (APU) memory accounting for GTT-backed devices:** on Strix Halo with a 1 GiB carve-out and a 120 GiB GTT, `hipMemGetInfo` reports TTM's cap minus live GTT (118 GiB free with `MemAvailable` at 13.5 GiB), and freed GTT pages parked in TTM's page pool are missing from `MemAvailable`. Every free-memory sizing site (Qwen4 placement and context admission, the dense Qwen card bound, VMM KV provisioning, prefill rungs, the Qwen4 session cache, the daemon's free-memory hint) now reads `Gpu::device_mem_info`, which on a GTT-backed APU clamps the free figure to `min(hipMemGetInfo free, MemAvailable + TTM pool estimate)` (`rdna_compute::uma_memory`); the `kv_slots` preflight counts the same pool on unified memory. Discrete GPUs and carve-out APUs are unchanged. On unified memory, Flash-Next's omitted `max_seq` is now automatic (the model context, capped by what the corrected free figure admits after load), its session-cache snapshots yield to context growth, and a fully resident load that cannot fit refuses before allocating instead of risking a global OOM. Discrete Flash-Next keeps the 32768 default.
- **Developer-only EP MoE bisection:** `HIPFIRE_EP_ORACLE_DUMP` now records the EP2 oracle's single-arm post-step state/logits; `HIPFIRE_EP_ORACLE_DUMP_PRE_STEP=1` additionally writes separately labeled pre-step replay snapshots. `HIPFIRE_MOE_DUMP_DIR` adds opt-in per-layer route, expanded-row, fold, shared-add and final-residual snapshots; fused-down paths explicitly mark expanded rows unavailable. `tools/ep_bisect.py` reports raw-byte differences in position/layer order, without mistaking EP partial-fold buffers for single-arm residuals. Instrumentation is off by default and does not change kernel selection or the unset launch sequence.
- **MQ4V2 expanded MoE down exactness:** pin affine dequantization and each eight-weight dot's initial independently rounded product plus FMA chain to the shipped fused ninepath-D4 arithmetic. The former compiler-chosen first-pair contraction differed in expanded K=512 group-1 rows 0–2, making EP differ from the unchanged single-device default. A fixture-free contraction-sensitive GPU bit oracle covers both K=512 and K=768; expert ownership, slot fold, shared-add order and single-device D4 remain unchanged.

- **Batched exact DFlash draft across VMM continuous-batching lanes (`HIPFIRE_CB_DFLASH_DRAFT_BATCH=0` kills it, developer var, default on, byte-identical):** the draft of every planned full-block DFlash lane ran serially (8 lanes ≈ 61 ms/step on the 27B mq4-xts target + qwen3.8-27b-dflash.mq4v2 draft, gfx1201). Full-block lanes now share one draft-model forward (`hipfire_runtime::dflash::draft_forward_lanes`) and one target lm-head GEMM per chunk of ≤ 48 rows (three 16-row lanes), keeping each lane's own context K/V rings, positions, hidden context, attention and (DFlash2) causal convolutions, and the per-lane candidate selector; budget-tail lanes, lone lanes and non-exact machines/drafts (non-gfx12, non-MQ4v2 weights, MoE) keep the singleton `dflash_lane_draft` call. Eight lanes now draft in ≈ 36 ms/step. The CB state oracle (`cb_vmm_state_oracle --spec dflash --phase probe`) gains batched-draft cases for 1..4 lanes (tokens + full draft/ring state compared with the isolated singleton draft) plus the 8-lane serial-vs-batched timing.
- **gfx1201 MQ4V2 verify-tile GEMMs for 16 < N < 64 rows (`HIPFIRE_WMMA_BATCH_TILES`, default on, byte-identical):** the multi-request speculative-verify trunk (several requests' 4-/16-row blocks packed into one forward) ran the one-tile WMMA GEMMs, whose every block re-streams the whole activation panel for 16 weight rows, so qkvza/qkv/gate-up/residual/lm_head cost grew ~linearly with N. New `*_mq4g256v2_wmma_gfx12_vt{2,3,4}w{4,8}` kernels (`gemm_qkvza`, `gemm_qkv`, `gemm_gate_up`, `gemm_mq4g256v2_residual`, which is also the batched lm_head) run 4 or 8 waves per block over the whole batch panel, stage each 128-K slab in LDS once per block and feed up to four independent accumulators per wave. Each (weight row, batch row) output keeps the one-tile kernel's single WMMA chain, K order, headers, dequant and epilogue, so every row is byte-identical at any N (probe memcmp, N = 17..63). On an R9700, 27B kernel time at N = 63: gate/up 650 -> 304 us, lm_head 5.39 -> 2.06 ms, residual-down 275 -> 186 us; an 8-request x 4-row MTP verify trunk step went 62.3 -> 48.2 ms. N <= 16 and capture/replay keep the one-tile kernels; `HIPFIRE_WMMA_BATCH_TILES=0` restores them. Compiler-less installs need the four new `gemm_*_mq4g256v2_wmma_gfx12_vt` modules in the gfx1201 pack.
- **VMM continuous batching (experimental, default off, non-exact):** `serve.vmm_batch` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_VMM_BATCH=1` together with `serve.batch_nonexact` / `HIPFIRE_SERVE_BATCH_NONEXACT=1` batches concurrent eligible TP1 Qwen3.5-family requests over the one resident weight set with per-request VMM KV/DeltaNet owners, planned by the runtime `BatchPlanner` (decode first, rotating prefill quantum within `serve.max_batch_tokens`) and executed through the arch VMM executor. The route is not singleton-exact (different prefill/decode kernels), hence the mandatory nonexact opt-in; the loaded ack reports `continuous_batch_route: "vmm"`, `continuous_batch_nonexact: true`, `continuous_batch_exact: false` and the actual owner receipt. Stage 1 serves greedy requests only and AR rows only; a request alone when it arrives keeps the unchanged singleton route (native MTP/DFlash included), and arrivals during a singleton generation wait for it. `serve.batch_spec` (`HIPFIRE_SERVE_BATCH_SPEC`) is a registered, not yet active, switch. Flags off, load/route/ack are unchanged. No throughput claim.
- **Multi-slot: quant-recipe admission is per SITE, not per layer.** A fused projection group (DeltaNet QKVZA, FullAttn QKV, FFN gate+up — dense AND MoE attention bodies) keeps its fused launch when every member shares one container, and any admitted mix inside the group falls back to per-weight plain GEMMs with each member reading the activation variant its own dtype wants (rotated consumers read the FWHT-rotated buffer, unrotated read the plain normed buffer; AWQ sidecars refuse mixed groups). Residual roles (wo, w_down) and the lm_head dispatch on their own dtype. This makes every `--tier` recipe load on slots — including `--tier pro` (MQ4G256V2 body + Q8_0 wo/embed/lm_head/conv1d), previously refused with "neither uniformly Q8_0 nor a uniformly batchable MQ-family dtype" — plus `--fixed-tier` mixes and `--kmap-dense`. Admitted projection containers are listed in QUANTIZE.md § "Multi-slot recipe admissibility"; Lloyd-V2/PARO/E8/G128 containers still refuse with named errors.
  - Review fixes folded in before GPU testing: the wo/w_down rotated residual path now resolves its kernel through an explicit per-container table (`slots_residual_gemm_key`) instead of the dispatch-level wildcard that would have silently decoded 200 B/group MQ6G256 and 104/112 B/group MQ3 headers through the 136 B/group HFQ4 v1 kernel; uniform-Q8 QKVZA/QKV groups degrade to the reference's plain-GEMM fork off WMMA archs and under `HIPFIRE_Q8_PREFILL_WMMA=0` (previously they silently took the WMMA-only fused kernel); `fused_gate_up_key_for` gained explicit Q8_0/HFQ6/MQ3/Lloyd arms (its HFQ4 catchall was a latent silent-misdispatch). New CPU unit tests lock all three invariants.
  - Verification (gfx1101): `saddle-lab/examples/test_forward_slots_golden.rs` reworked into a per-KV-mode matrix (legacy slab + paged pool) — q8/fwht2/fwht3/fwht4 at 0.000x tolerance (10/10 slot-steps each, n_slots 1..=4), paged fwht{2,3,4} at 0.000x, per-mode negative controls failing correctly at ~6000x; a fresh `--tier pro` MiMo-9B artifact passes 20/20 at 0.000x on q8 and fwht3. The negative control itself was vacuous before (with DECODE_STEPS=0 its "expected failure" was an index-out-of-bounds panic) — it now runs an explicit decode-step corruption and asserts the failure is a tolerance mismatch. f16 KV stays a slots-only tier (no sequential constructor exists to reference against); paged q8 skips the matrix (the WMMA attend family is slab-only, so paged q8 is numerically — not bit — comparable), both documented in the harness.
  - fp8 (E4M3) slot KV is implemented behind the same gfx1201-only gates as the sequential path (desc-aware wrappers around the shared q8 TUs, paged symbols, tier-plan geometry, fail-closed named refusal elsewhere). Kernel-level descriptor oracle on gfx1201: fp8 attend is BIT-EXACT for zero-base (4,096 elements), nonzero-base with poisoned slot 0, and mixed two-slot cases; fp8 write is BIT-EXACT too. The same bf16 checks also pass. This validates kernel-level parity only, not full-model logits or serve behavior; fp8 remains refused on every other architecture.
- **Multi-slot: bf16 KV attend now calls the same kernel as the sequential path** (`attention_bf16_kv_batched`, via a new desc-aware slots wrapper; the slots arm previously used the windowed flash tile with window=0). Kernel-level slots-vs-sequential parity for bf16 write and attend is BIT-EXACT on gfx1101 (`rdna-compute/examples/test_bf16_slots_parity.rs`: zero-base, non-zero-base, mixed two-slot, and write parity). Full-model logits still diverge ~6x a 1e-3-relative golden tolerance on one vocab element, unchanged by the arm switch — the sequential bf16 attend selection is itself a flash-mode/capture heuristic (`bf16_attend_key`), and the end-to-end root-cause is a tracked follow-up (docs/plans/slots-quant-recipe-admission.md §9); bf16 is therefore excluded from the golden matrix default until then.

- **ROCm 10.1 bring-up (HIP 7.16 / clang 24):** shared `ROCM_PATH` resolver, strict ROCm oracles (missing tools fail unless `HIPFIRE_TEST_REQUIRE_ROCM=0` explicitly marks a no-ROCm host), HIP code object v6 and a new JIT cache ABI. Remove `restrict` from both gfx1201 FP8 producer-pack helpers' cross-thread shared scratch, preserving the post-barrier row-scale broadcast without disabling load-PRE globally. Fresh-hipcc SiLU oracles select committed clang-23 or clang-24 goldens by compiler major and fail clearly for unknown toolchains; native builders retain their original DAGs. PeaceMaker's native writer identity now names the oracle-verified lld 24 layout while keeping its 0.4.1 ELF stamp and committed bundle bytes stable. Quality pins in `AGENTS.md` are re-measured on ROCm 10.1; HIP 7.15 values remain historical. Halo pins explicitly require a warm kernel cache: cold-JIT runs can differ in chunk 0, and this bring-up does not claim to fix that path.
- **Release kernel packs now build on the pinned ROCm 10.1 image (HIP 7.16.26385 / clang 24).** The `release-kernel-packs` workflow defaults to `docker.io/rocm/dev-ubuntu-26.04:10.1.0-full@sha256:4f5ed1bf6532a4b9920400401b0ad0f706af356dae61e40afefbfd6c8073f0ce` (packages `10.1.0-3`), so the default manifest range is ROCm [10.1, 11.0); explicit range overrides keep their meaning. A host whose local hipcc is another build, such as ROCm 10.0, compiles locally instead of installing the pack; a host with no compiler accepts an admitted pack. `HIPFIRE_ROCM_IMAGE` and the range variables still override the default. The v0.4.0 prebuilt-pack entry below keeps its original ROCm 10.0 image as history.
- **Host-scaled kernel-pack builds:** compile independent modules with one compiler per worker, reserving one core per eight and bounding workers by available RAM (~1.5 GiB/job). `HIPFIRE_PACK_JOBS` overrides the budget; `scripts/build-kernel-pack.sh --jobs N` shares it across concurrent architectures without multiplying CPU usage. Kernel sources, compiler recipes and package formats are unchanged.
- **Kernels ready before the first dispatch on closed routes:** for Qwen3.8-27B MQ4G256V2 XTS (`qwen3.8-27b.mq4-xts`) on exact gfx1201 (single GPU, native fp8 K/V, Q8 DeltaNet state, default flags, AR or native MTP) the common loader compiles the route's missing kernels during the weight load with a bounded worker pool (`HIPFIRE_JIT_JOBS`; `1` is the concurrency kill switch) and loads every planned HIP and embedded PeaceMaker module before the first dispatch. `loaded` now waits for weights plus kernels, and no compile or module load happens after it on that route; an unplanned kernel fails instead. Every other route keeps lazy JIT. The daemon `profile` command now precompiles the arch's registry inventory instead of a separate hand-assembled Qwen3.5 recipe list. Cold-start/readiness only: no steady-state tok/s change and no numerical-fix claim.
- **PeaceMaker decode twins on gfx1201 by default (`kernel.pm_decode`, auto on exact gfx1201 only):** the five W1 decode projection modules (QKVZA, QKV, gate/up, multirow-r2 and residual MQ4v2) load embedded builder-emitted images instead of hipcc objects, at every load funnel and in the planned-route preload, so the route no longer compiles them. Each image is pinned by its bundle and gfx1201 ELF SHA-256 and by the incumbent HIP source digest the full-buffer oracle compared against; a mismatch is refused, never replaced with hipcc output. `HIPFIRE_PM_DECODE=0` restores the hipcc modules, which stay in the kernel packs. Other architectures ignore the key. Qwen3.8-27B MQ4 XTS (`qwen3.8-27b.mq4-xts`) on an R9700 (all A/B on one card): retained PM4 and HIP-graph greedy traces at ctx 512/8192/32768 (256 steps) have byte-identical logits, hidden, KV and DeltaNet dumps; native MTP gives identical greedy IDs and trunk KV/recurrent hashes at ~0.5K/9K/38K prompts; WT2/code24 `.kldseq` pins are unchanged (`ff88c499…`, `9b061631…`, two cards). Decode speed is unchanged: AR bench_decode median 37.17 vs 37.08 tok/s, MTP generate 71.4/55.1 vs 71.5/55.2 tok/s (3 fresh processes per arm). No speedup is claimed.
- **gfx11 IU4 opt-out packs:** package `gemm_mq4g256v2_residual_mmq` on gfx1100/gfx1151 with its exact runtime source and all Q8_1/X128 exports, so `HIPFIRE_IU4_PREFILL=0` does not need a first-run JIT for this module. The default IU4 objects, compiler flags and kernel math are unchanged.
- **Qwen4 and dense Qwen tool argument replay is on by default:**
  restores producer ordering for reordered echoes on Qwen4 and dense Qwen3.8,
  preserving typed values through one shared renderer.
  `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_TOOL_ARG_REPLAY=0` opts out; only exact extensions of the active committed
  token record are accepted. Byte-identical echoes and retired-MTP guards remain
  unchanged; uncertain candidates retain the safe miss. No measured speed claim.
- **GDN replay route no longer depends on memory pressure (exactness fix):** the gfx1201 multi-layer GDN tape replay (and the D8 snapshot-source tables) armed lazily at a tape's first accept, and an allocation failure there silently moved that tape to the per-layer replay for life, which commits different DeltaNet bytes on the production model. `GdnTape::new_for_config` now arms the tables when the tape is built (`GdnTape::new_for_dflash` adds the D8 tables), so an allocation failure refuses the lane or request at provision with a logged error; an admitted replay never falls back. The replay's q/k/v/out scratch is now a `Gpu`-owned map keyed by scratch shape (layers, rows, value width): every armed tape of one shape shares one refcounted buffer, released when no tape or replay graph references it and drained on model unload, so eager arming costs a lane only its pointer tables instead of ~75 MB on the 27B. The serve engine's batched verify tape, which is repaired per layer and never replayed, is built with `GdnTape::new_capture_only`. `HIPFIRE_GDN_REPLAY_ML_OFF=1` stays an explicit diagnostic opt-in and is documented as not byte-identical. `GpuPool::alloc` now returns its free lists to HIP and retries once when `hipMalloc` runs out of memory (freed scratch parked in the pool is invisible to HIP; 9 GiB in the reproduction). `DeltaNetSnapshot::new_for` frees partial buffers on a mid-way failure, and `take_dn_checkpoint` logs a skipped checkpoint and frees the snapshot when its save fails. Open: the per-layer `replay_gdn_inner` is still not byte-identical to the multi-layer replay.
- **VMM maps reclaim pooled memory:** `hipMemCreate` cannot see buffers parked in `GpuPool` either (12.6–13.7 GiB pooled with 0.1–1.4 GiB reported free in the 4-lane MTP state oracle, which failed its 32K lanes with `hipMemCreate: out of memory`). A whole-segment VMM map (`alloc_vmm_tensor`, `grow_vmm_tensor`) that runs out of memory now returns the pool to HIP and retries once; a granule map (`grow_vmm_tensor_granules`, prefix forks), whose failure aborts its arena, returns the pool first when HIP's free figure cannot cover it plus 64 MiB. Both log a `GpuPool: VMM map ...` line.
- **Removed config keys warn instead of failing the load:** 0.4.1 removed `kernel.mw16` (shipped in 0.3.1 and 0.4.0) from the schema outright, so a `config.toml` that still set it made every command fail with `unknown configuration key 'kernel.mw16'`. Removed keys that shipped now live in `hipfire_config::RETIRED_CONFIG_KEYS`; through their deprecation window (`kernel.mw16`: until 0.5.0) the load drops them and run/serve/chat and `hipfire config show` print `warning: ignored kernel.mw16 in <file>: removed in 0.4.1 (…)`. Keys that never shipped (such as `kernel.gfx11_iu4_swizzle`, which only existed on an experiment branch) and typos still fail the load. See [`docs/CONFIG.md`](docs/CONFIG.md#lifecycle-status).
- **Generic (llama-family) DFlash no longer panics the daemon on a 1–3 row verify block.** When a request's remaining budget left the block below the batched-verify minimum, the per-token fallback captured no drafter hidden rows, and `GenericDflashSpeculator::finish_chain` sliced the empty buffer (`dflash_generic.rs:473`, `range end index 20480 out of range for slice of length 0`). The fallback now captures through `forward_scratch_compute_capture`, as per-token prefill does. The panic itself was not reproduced before release (no generic DFlash target/draft pair was available locally); what was verified is that the non-capture path is byte-unchanged and a registry DFlash pair serves identical text.
- **Serve `/metrics` completes the gateway telemetry.** The decode and prefill tok/s histograms used the 10…60000 ms latency buckets; they now use `1 … 5000` tok/s buckets (bucket boundaries changed). New: `hipfire_prompt_tokens_total`, `hipfire_completion_tokens_total`, `hipfire_cached_prompt_tokens_total` (from the daemon `done` event, counted the same way as the response `usage`, and only when `done` carries the field), a `hipfire_time_per_output_token_milliseconds` histogram (gateway-measured `(latency − ttft) / (tokens − 1)`), a `hipfire_spec_tau` summary on speculative routes, `hipfire_retries_total` / `hipfire_retries_succeeded_total`, and the gauges `hipfire_engine_up`, `hipfire_model_loading`, `hipfire_context_capacity_tokens`. `hipfire_ttft_milliseconds` now stops at the first generated token, reasoning included; before, a thinking model recorded the time to the first answer token, or no sample when the answer never started. **Breaking for scrapers:** `hipfire_model_loaded` is replaced by `hipfire_model_info{model="…"} 1`, which has no sample while no model is resident. Successful `/v1/images/*` requests now count in `hipfire_requests_total` and request latency. Metric list in `docs/SERVE.md`.
- **TUI Dashboard "Live metrics" card:** `hipfire-tui` scrapes serve `/metrics` every 1.5 s for its whole lifetime (rocm-smi and the other probes stay focused-tab only) into an in-memory ring of the last 120 scrapes (~3 min), so history already exists when the Dashboard opens. The card shows generated tok/s and req/s sparklines, windowed TTFT/TPOT p50/p95 (bucket deltas, PromQL `histogram_quantile` interpolation), errors and admission rejects per minute, cache hit % and mean spec tau when present. History is never persisted and is dropped when serve goes offline or restarts (uptime or a `_total` counter going backwards). Older serves without `/metrics` show "metrics unavailable (older serve)".
- **Configuration: `serve.continuous_batch_size` accepts up to 256 concurrent batch lanes, matching the CLI (the schema stopped at 128).** The ceiling is one shared constant (`hipfire_config::MAX_CONTINUOUS_BATCH_SIZE`) for schema, CLI and serve precheck; runtime memory admission may still allow fewer live lanes.
- **PeaceMaker crate renames: the XDNA2 / AIE2P NPU builder crate `pm-npu` is now `peacemaker-npu` (binaries `peacemaker-npu-sim` and `peacemaker-npu-objdump`), the builder crate `hipfire-isa` is now `peacemaker-isa` (library `peacemaker_isa`, binary `peacemaker-isa`; the `peacemaker` binary is unchanged) and the standalone RIP front end `hipfire-rip` is now `peacemaker-rip` (library `peacemaker_rip`, binary `peacemaker-rip`, formerly `rip`).** Pure rename of the crate directories, packages, library and binary names, dependents and docs; no behavior change. The `dflash_accept_emu` and `qwen4_ep_owner_assign_pm` CPU integration tests move to `rdna-compute/tests`, so `peacemaker-isa` no longer has a dev-dependency on `rdna-compute`. PeaceMaker gets a standalone history-preserving mirror with guarded local publication through `scripts/mirror-push.sh`; no Actions publishing workflow or deploy-key secret is needed.
- **Developer-only online DFlash draft tuning (`HIPFIRE_DFLASH_ONLINE_TUNE`, off by default).** Covers Qwen3.5 chain DFlash and the generic (llama-family) chain, greedy and temp>0 naive-sampling verify. The draft's top-16 per row is re-ranked by a per-request learner trained from verified tokens; it uses chain n-grams, suffix matches with injection, stutter and neighbouring rows. On gfx1151, tokens per verify window against shipping DFlash (20 cold requests): Qwen3.5-9B ×1.044, decode ×1.07; Qwen3-8B with its weak generic draft ×1.146, decode ×1.14. Tooling: `sweep` mode plus `examples/dflash_online_replay.rs --simulate` evaluate policies with their own block starts. Evidence: `docs/perf-checkpoints/2026-10-05-gfx1151-dflash-online-draft-tuning.md` and its two 2026-10-06 amendments.
- **Native MTP is opt-in on a resolved host-mapped routed-expert spill, on either execution seam.** `speculation.mtp = "auto"` declines to AR after placement resolves a spill under `memory.offload_exec=pcie` (the unchanged default) or `cpu`, logging the host-mapped layer count, seam and `opt in with --spec mtp`. `speculation.mtp = "on"` / `--spec mtp` opts in, subject to the uniform-MQ6 gfx12 FP16 safety refusal below. Other resident attachment behavior is unchanged. Unset/null `memory.gpu_layer_budget` and `memory.moe_expert_budget` keep all weights device-resident on every model; explicit `auto`/`-1` uses fit capacity, spilling routed experts before whole layers, and manual `N` remains a hard resident-layer budget. `speculation.dflash=on` still requires a DFlash draft and never aliases to MTP.
- **Host-placed mixed MoE layers load again: a non-uniform layer routes to the graded packer instead of being refused.** The uniform expert packer admits host-only dtypes (qt 15/19/31/34), so a graded layer whose expert 0 is admitted but differs from expert 1 — `qwen3.6-35b-a3b.mq4p` layer 8 leads with MQ6 (qt 15) before an MQ4 (qt 13) expert — entered the uniform path and returned "no packer applies" on the second expert instead of falling through to the graded host packer, failing the whole load closed (`layer 8 routed experts are host-placed … but expert packing does not apply`) under any host MoE spill, `auto` and manual alike. Layer layout is now classified once, up front: one shared admitted dtype and stride per projection means the uniform packer, anything else means the graded packer (host placement) or the literal per-expert tensors (device). No packing arithmetic changes; the same `.mq4p` fixture loads with all 40 layers and the graded route's per-tag blob count.
- **CPU-seam MTP measurement (superseded above, same draft).** The qwen3.5 auto-degrade was scoped to the PCIe seam for one patch, on the 2026-10-11 16-cell record where `memory.offload_exec=cpu` recomputes the routed FFN on the host and MTP beat AR 8/8 at +28–44% on `qwen3.6-35b-a3b.mq4p`/gfx1201 (79.7/66.7% acceptance). The keep was withdrawn: it armed the head on every offloaded MoE with no opt-in and exposed an unvalidated drafter on `mq6`. Those cells are reachable with `--spec mtp` / `speculation.mtp = "on"`. No arithmetic change.
- **RDNA4 hardware queues with MTP over a `pcie` expert spill:** hipfire leaves `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES` unset. If decode throughput drops at random with native MTP opted in over a host-mapped expert spill on `gfx1200`/`gfx1201`, set `GPU_MAX_HW_QUEUES=1` before starting hipfire (it applies to the whole process).
- **Graded Qwen3.6 MoE experts on the CPU splice; narrow spec verify:** `memory.offload_exec=cpu` now hosts no-AWQ graded (mixed-dtype) packed routed experts — per expert tier, each expert's gate and down decoded in its own dtype with a checked bucket-owner offset. On this route the sealed GPU program keeps only the router, the shared expert and the residual base; the routed GPU FFN is omitted, so no GPU expert pointer (sink, dummy or twin) is bound and every routed FFN comes from the real host-mapped packed bytes. An AR single-token decode and any `<=4`-row batched forward reuse this CPU splice — the splice gate is width-only, so the speculative verify and the `Standard` tape-disabled accepted-prefix rollback replay both take it, as does an ordinary very short `<=4`-row prompt prefill — while wider prompt prefill and wider verify batches keep the real expert pointer tables and the GPU grouped PCIe read. The CPU splice preserves each format's accumulation order, reuses one f32 partial sum across k-tiles for qt 13/15 (flat MQ), and makes no per-call warm allocation and no activation quantization. AWQ experts, unpackable/unsupported host formats, and the graph/retained-replay (Redline) refusal are unchanged. Native Qwen3.5-family MTP remains explicit opt-in on a resolved routed-expert spill under either CPU or PCIe execution, subject to the uniform-MQ6 gfx12 FP16 safety refusal below. The opt-in `HIPFIRE_MOE_CPU_ORACLE` and `HIPFIRE_MOE_EXPERT_STATS` diagnostics fail closed with CPU-deferred experts (`explicit GPU routed expert oracle/statistics unavailable with CPU-deferred experts; use PCIe/resident`) instead of reading the omitted routed FFN's stale `down_expanded`. The independent-implementation CPU-vs-GPU numerical contract is unchanged.
- **CPU splice trace split (opt-in diagnostic, no behavior change):** `HIPFIRE_CPU_EXEC_TRACE=1` splice lines add coarse `setup` (expert resolve), `middle` (whole SiLU+FWHT loop), `combine` (whole weighted-combine loop), and `total` (splice wall excluding the trace print) means alongside the existing d2h_first/d2h_rest/gu/dn/h2d split. No new env var, no per-row/dot timers, no HIP events, no new device sync. Per-phase sums reconcile within a splice, but per-quant attribution does not: `quant` is the first selected expert's gate_up format only, `graded=true` collapses gate_up-mixed, down-mixed, and gate_up≠down mixes into one flag, the down quant is never printed, and no worker count is printed — see `docs/env-vars.md`.
- **Adaptive CPU MoE row scheduling:** shared-weight GEMVs retain expert-level Rayon parallelism and group consecutive weight rows using the current pool size, nonempty expert-job count, output rows and activation-row count. Grain follows a square-root per-worker row budget (one whole-job block on a single worker), with no CPU tuning table, calibration, configuration knob or pool resize. Dot kernels, accumulation order and row-major outputs are unchanged; exact scalar/SIMD multi-row tests cover 1/2/4/8 workers, partial tails and wide batches.
- **Exact AVX2 six-bit CPU unpacking:** packed MQ6/HFQ6 and six-bit V2 GEMV decode eight codes with one bounded qword load instead of two dword loads. The byte shuffle preserves decoded indices, FMA/reduction order and cached activation sums; mixed-format/multi-row execution, Rayon scheduling and CPU/GPU dispatch are unchanged. Prefix/end-boundary coverage includes the two readable preceding bytes without reading past the final payload.
- **Explicit CPU-offloaded Qwen3.5-family MTP execution restored:** invalidate pointer-keyed FP16 activation staging at the CPU-deferred expert handoff: the omitted GPU routed tail no longer changes that cache key, while the next layer reuses activation scratch with new contents. Native MTP `auto` still declines a resolved routed-expert spill; explicit attachment remains subject to the uniform-MQ6 gfx12 FP16 safety refusal below. Shared GPU launchers, kernel arithmetic, CPU accumulation, mixed-quant handling, Rayon scheduling and CPU/GPU dispatch remain unchanged. Validation uses the existing CPU MTP verify/rollback hardware oracle.
- **gfx1201 host-mapped grouped MoE verification: coalesced packed-weight reads for 2–4-row speculative batches.** The mixed grouped WMMA kernel stages each packed weight group cooperatively in LDS instead of issuing scattered small PCIe reads. Quant decoding, WMMA order, 16-slot grouping, output layout and weighted residual combine are unchanged.
  - Packed Qwen3.5 expert residency comes from the owning allocations, independently for gate/up and down. Borrowed expert subviews retain their ownership and free semantics; they no longer hide the packed host allocation from dispatch.
  - Ordinary prefill, unknown/wider batches, device-resident projections, other architectures and specialized QT44/QT53 routes retain the existing kernels. Resident narrow `SpeculativeVerify` keeps beta's legacy grouped routing; narrow Path1 applies only to host-mapped routed projections. The coalesced gfx1201 host-weight narrow kernel remains available. The broken all-indexed graded verify route is not enabled.
  - GPU channel parity covers packed host owners, permuted expert IDs, sparse/padded slots, row tails, sentinel tiles, both projections and weighted combine at batches 2–4. Candidate outputs match the original grouped route bit-for-bit; reused activation storage is refreshed before the baseline can prime the conversion scratch.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) on gfx1151: the F16 WMMA routed-expert GEMMs share X through LDS, on by default for the F16 WMMA MoE route (`HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_X_LDS=auto`; `0` is the kill switch, `1` forces it where admissible). Bytewise identical; on that route pp512 +6.6 %, pp1024 +7.4 %, pp8192 +9.4 % on Strix Halo. The default symmetric IU4 route (GPTQ3) is unchanged.** The grouped gate/up SiLU GEMM runs four 16-row output tiles per workgroup (`gemm_mq4g256v2_moe_grouped_wmma_k2_silu_bf16out_x4`, M % 64 == 0, K % 256 == 0) and the grouped down GEMM eight (`gemm_mq4g128v2_moe_grouped_wmma_gfx1151_x8_bf16out`, M % 128 == 0). An expert run of several 16-slot tiles reads each X chunk once per workgroup instead of once per wave, and a three-tile run does not compute a masked fourth tile. Each tile's WMMA sequence, operands and epilogue are the per-wave kernel's. `auto` arms exactly when a layer's MoE runs on the F16 WMMA route, i.e. its symmetric IU4 arm is not selected: `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0`, or experts that fail the symmetry check (the asymmetric `qwen3.8-flash-next.mq4`). The GPTQ3 default takes the symmetric IU4 route on gfx1151 and never reaches these GEMMs; every other arch keeps the per-wave entries. Split out of #774 (fivetide).
  - Identity (Strix Halo, `qwen4_moe_sym --mode check`, synthetic experts): the incumbent gate/up and down outputs, every live slot row in slot order, are byte-equal to beta `639907cc3` in 30 of 30 cases (routing one / all / skew × 512, 777, 1131, 2048 and 4096 tokens × seeds 7 and 11).
  - serve_harness, greedy, same F16 route: a three-prompt battery of 730–1,880-token prompts (prompts-file md5 `638c23fd…`) is byte-identical to beta (`transcript_byte_identical=true`). On the default route (symmetric IU4, which this change does not touch), the stock battery passes 5/5 and the chain finishes every turn `stop`; chain turn 4 ends inside its reasoning, which is the known v0.4.1 issue.
  - Both kernel modules are append-only, and every incumbent symbol disassembles identically to beta on gfx1100 and gfx1151 (production JIT flags, hipcc 7.16, kernel descriptors included). The new lab oracle `qwen4_moe_f16_lds_oracle --mode check` (production scatter/rotate/unscatter, asymmetric synthetic experts, whole poisoned buffers, negative controls) passes 30/30 on Strix Halo with the LDS entries armed: they and the production launchers equal the per-wave entries byte for byte. A CPU unit test pins the `auto` / `1` / `0` predicate (IU4 selected → off, F16 route on gfx1151 → on, other arch → off, `0` → off).
  - Identity (Strix Halo, canonical `qwen3.8-flash-next-gptq3.mq4` with `HIPFIRE_QWEN4_MOE_SYM_IU4=0`, serve_harness greedy battery on three long prompts of 936–5,103-token contexts, thinking off, 256 tokens): armed, `=0` and beta `d608ac9ed` give byte-identical transcripts (md5 `48bbbcc0…`). On the default IU4 route the module-load audit shows no X-LDS entry loaded and the battery is identical to beta; with the entries disarmed the F16-route battery and chain are identical to beta `09540d8c4` (chain 5/5 `stop`).
  - Speed (Strix Halo, same artifact and route, `hipfire bench --matrix --pp 512,1024,8192 --ctx 128 --tg 1 --runs 2 --warmups 1 --spec off --backend noslots --workload stateless`, synthetic stream `token[i] = 10 + (i % 1000)`, graph on, 3 fresh processes per arm in a Latin-square order, medians of per-process medians; armed vs beta `d608ac9ed`): pp512 1,289.9 → 1,374.5 tok/s, pp1024 1,445.7 → 1,552.4, pp8192 1,676.3 → 1,833.8 (ranges disjoint); decode at ctx 128 33.6 → 33.7 tok/s. `=0` on the same binary measures as beta (1,289.2 / 1,440.4 / 1,669.7).
  - Caveats: the entries are HIP, without PeaceMaker twins; the Redline prefill phase capture of Qwen4 refuses on beta and on this branch alike (the QSA gather workspace may not grow while a capture is armed), so no Redline prefill fingerprint exists for it.
- **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4) sampled MTP decode: author measurements against v0.4.1 on Strix Halo show HumanEval/0–9 with the Qwen card sampler (T0.7 / top_p 0.8 / top_k 20 / presence 1.5, thinking off) going from 40.1 to 51.9 tok/s (+29 %).** This is not a fresh current-beta comparison: beta now has a 16-window singleton AR-floor grace. The changes:
  - **gfx1151 singleton only:** the per-depth draft-agreement decay goes 0.875 → 0.97 (a window memory of ~30 instead of ~8); the same scoped decay applies to the AR floor's measured window costs and the n-gram yield history. Other architectures and all MTP lanes, including gfx1151 lanes and promoted singletons, keep 0.875. The floor prices each native depth by its measured cost over `expected_emitted(agreement)` and retires a request to AR, stickily, once no native route beats AR. In the author's v0.4.1-based measurements, with the short memory an early run of rejections, or one slow window, was enough to retire a request native MTP was winning: HumanEval/1 and /6 retired in every run (τ 0.03, ~32 tok/s AR for the rest of the request). At 0.97 they stay on MTP; in 14 runs at 0.97 one request (HumanEval/1) retired once. Mean τ goes 1.58 → 1.91. The emitted stream is still AR's in distribution; only the route and depth choice change.
  - `llama::gather_pool`, the pool gather of the CPU AR sampler and of `SparseDist::build_from_logits`, skips 256-logit chunks whose lane-wise maximum cannot enter the pool. Slot order, tie choice and the maximum are those of the sequential gather, and a unit test pins them bit for bit. A 248K-vocab verify row's distribution went from ~1.4 ms to ~40 µs of host time.
  - The live QSA pool/RoPE grid change of the original branch is dropped: beta's `b013f9437` already sizes the incremental pool/RoPE grid by rows. The "+ QSA grid" step below was measured with the dropped variant.
  - A 2–8-row verify merges two launch pairs per layer: the router with the BF16 shared selector (one `gemv_bf16_xf32_x4_rows`), and, on gfx1151 only, the Q8 shared gate with up (the new `gemv_q8_0_k2560_staged_rows_x2`). A GPU unit test pins the merged Q8 launch bit for bit against two single launches.
  - Measured on Strix Halo (gfx1151), canonical GPTQ3 artifact, fresh `hipfire serve` per run, `max_seq` 262144, seed 123, one warm pass and one measured pass, decode (n−1)/time. Cumulative means: v0.4.1 40.1 (2 runs), + gather 43.5 (2), + QSA grid 45.0 (2), + launch merges 44.7 (2), + decay 51.6 (2). Final stack 51.7 / 51.3 / 52.3 / 52.2 / 51.5 / 52.4 (six runs, mean 51.9). Ablations against the final stack: without the launch merges 51.5 / 50.4 / 51.7. With the agreement decay at 0.97 but the floor's cost decay left at 0.875, HumanEval/6 still retired in every run (48.3 / 49.4 / 49.5). Adding a re-measured static window-cost table changed nothing, so it is not included: the floor's measured costs supersede it. All runs: 10/10 answers finish on `stop` and pass their HumanEval tests.

- Gemma 4 layers run as engine `Step`s: 12B dense, E2B/E4B, the 26B-A4B MoE
  and the EAGLE draft head (`gemma-4-*-assistant`) all execute
  `[SandwichAttention, SandwichMlp | ParallelMoeMlp, PerLayerInput?, Scale?]`
  from `hipfire_dispatch`; dense and E-series batched prefill and EAGLE verify
  run the same program with `rows > 1`. The Gemma 4 super-op path and its
  `HIPFIRE_FORWARD_LOWERED` hand arms are gone.
  - The 26B-A4B now takes the 12B's fused qk-norm+RoPE and post-norm+residual
    kernels. Greedy text stays coherent and can diverge late.
  - `calib_sweep`, `eval_hipfire` and `prefill_parity_gemma4` prefill Gemma 4
    through the same program (`forward::forward_prefill_batch`, with
    architecture-specific chunk sizes, Q8 KV). The dispatch sandwich
    projections feed the calibration collector, so any architecture on these
    steps calibrates without per-architecture taps. Gemma calibration and
    KLD numbers move: the tools now use the serve kernels. The 26B-A4B MoE
    block runs batched too.
  - `HIPFIRE_BATCHED_PREFILL` and `HIPFIRE_WMMA_PREFILL` are removed.
  - The `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_FUSED_{FFN,QK,QK_ROPE,POSTNORM,ATTN_NORM,PROJ}` developer
    switches are removed; the fused routes are always on. The debug switches
    `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_{BASELINE_ATTN,ATTN_VERIFY,GEMM_VERIFY}` and
    `HIPFIRE_MOE_{BYPASS,BUCKETED}` are removed with their hand-written paths.
  - The 26B-A4B loads MQ4G256V2 weights and runs MQ4G256V2 and MQ6G256 routed
    experts on the indexed kernels, which is what the current quantizer emits
    for it. A MoE checkpoint whose expert formats have no indexed kernel pair
    now refuses to load instead of running a host-side expert loop, and so
    does one whose HFQ4-G128 expert `down_proj` (K = 704) was packed across
    rows by a quantizer before `b4846285e`; both produced garbage. Requantize
    such files.
- Gemma 4 runs on one weight stack: the 26B-A4B MoE loads through the same
  config, weights, state and forward as the 12B and E-series, and the separate
  `lowered.rs` stack is gone. Its sliding cache is position-indexed like the
  12B's (no 1024-row ring), so batched prefill (default on gfx1100/gfx1151/gfx1201)
  now reaches the 26B on Q8/Q8 loads.
  Its hipGraph decode is off when expert `down_proj` is Q8_0, whose atomic
  sum is not replay-exact. Text on the q8-experts and requantized MQ4 26B
  fixtures is unchanged from the previous stack.
- Gemma 4 joins the engine session cache (`memory.session_cache_bytes`, `0`
  = off): prefill snapshots the Q8 KV rows every 1024 prompt tokens and at
  the prompt length rounded down to the prefill width. The next turn of a
  conversation resumes there and recomputes the rest of the previous prompt
  and the decoded reply on the cold prefill grid, so a cached turn is
  byte-identical to a cold prefill (decode and batched prefill write
  different KV bits; decoded rows are never reused). A repeated long prompt
  restores the snapshot instead of re-prefilling it.
  Each request now starts from its own prompt; previously a Gemma 4 request
  continued the previous request's KV in the same process until the context
  filled, so its output depended on what ran before it. The route-generic
  live/plan/begin/commit logic moved from Qwen4 into
  `hipfire_runtime::session_cache::SessionDriver`, which Qwen4 and Gemma 4
  both use.
- Gemma 4 EAGLE (`params.drafter`, opt-in `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_EAGLE=1`) runs on
  the generic speculative seams: `Gemma4Drafter` is an `MtpDrafter` behind
  `MtpSpeculator`, the bundle is a `SpecTarget`, `GemmaSpecEmit` routes the
  thought channel, and `generate_spec` drives the loop. The inline EAGLE loop
  and `speculative.rs` are deleted, along with the `infer_gemma4_spec` example.
  The draft head is one step list ending in the engine `DraftHead`. The first
  token now comes from prefill, and each window pairs its seed with the hidden
  that predicted it, so per-prompt tau moves slightly. Batched verify is not
  bitwise-equal to single-row decode, so greedy EAGLE can leave the AR stream
  where two logits are nearly tied. Measured on gfx1151: one of five fixture
  prompts at a 0.021-logit margin.
- Gemma 4 prefills in 256-row batches by default on gfx1151
  (`HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_PREFILL_BATCH` overrides; gfx1100/gfx1201 keep 64, other
  architectures stay per-token). On gfx1151 the batched rows:
  - route the 26B-A4B's experts sorted by expert: one scatter, a grouped WMMA
    gate/up (MQ4G256V2, MQ6G256) and a grouped HFQ4-G128 down per batch, so
    each expert's weights are read once per batch. The router stays an
    F32-activation Q8 GEMV: the F16 WMMA GEMM moves router logits by ~1e-3
    and flips near-tied experts;
  - project MQ4G256V2 weights through WMMA (previously one GEMV per row) and
    Q8 weights through the 4-warp 64x64 WMMA tile;
  - attend with a GQA-shared WMMA flash kernel, sliding window included
    (`HIPFIRE_Q8_PREFILL_GQA_WMMA=0` restores the tile kernels);
  - compute the LM head on the last chunk only.
  Batched prefill changes floating-point reduction order; it is not a
  byte-identity claim against per-token prefill.
- Gemma 4 long-context decode runs its own Q8 flash-decode pair: in
  `attention_flash_q8_0_tile_swa` tiles that lie wholly below the sliding
  window exit without reading V, and `attention_flash_q8_0_reduce_skip` skips
  them and loads tile partials eight at a time. Only Gemma 4 launches them;
  the shared `attention_flash_q8_0_tile` / `_reduce` modules every other
  model runs are unchanged. On gfx1151 the Gemma decode shapes
  (head_dim 256 / GQA 2, head_dim 512 / GQA 8) run a GQA-shared 8-wave tile
  (`HIPFIRE_GFX1151_Q8_DECODE_ATTN_GQA_GEMMA=0` restores the swa tile), also
  only from Gemma 4. The HFQ4-G128 routed down kernel computes eight rows per
  workgroup with all eight experts' loads in flight.

### Known issues (0.4.2)

- **Uniform MQ6G256 V1 (qt15) Qwen35 MoE native MTP on gfx1200/gfx1201 with FP16 WMMA enabled is refused before serving/generation when a head would attach.** Admission uses tensor metadata, not filenames, and covers explicit MTP and resident `auto` attachment. `--spec off` / `speculation.mtp=off` and a host-mapped routed-expert spill whose `auto` has declined remain AR, not blanket-refused. `HIPFIRE_FP16=0` permits explicit MTP. Mixed/graded MQ4+MQ6, MQ6V2, gfx1151 and dense models remain allowed. This guards the reported garbled narrow verify windows; it does not fix or establish the root cause of the underlying WMMA defect. Historical performance checkpoints remain author-reported measurements, not validation of this admission change.
- **Explicit CPU MoE expert execution (`memory.offload_exec=cpu`) is opt-in and not bit-identical to resident or PCIe execution; follow-up for 0.4.3.** On `qwen3.6-35b-a3b.mq4p`/gfx1201 with 16 expert layers host-mapped, serve batteries and chains with and without MTP decode coherent text, but greedy output diverges from resident beta after a shared prefix (turn 3 or 4). The CPU splice is an independent implementation with its own accumulation order. The `mtp_cpu_offload_rollback_oracle` k3-full trunk-KV delta against the serial CPU reference measured 2.384e-2, above its 2e-2 bound, while committed tokens and same-route byte identity held. Defaults are unchanged: unset budgets stay fully resident, and explicit PCIe offload is byte-identical to resident beta. No speed claim is made for either offload seam.
- **Gemma 4 Redline (retained PM4 replay) and its Redline lowering are unvalidated and off by default.** No Gemma 4 load takes the retained Redline default on any arch, `.mq4r` names included (`retained_redline_default` carve-out); Redline runs only when selected explicitly. Redline lowering is validated only for the Qwen and DeepSeek V4 families. On gfx1201 the `redline_daemon_harness.py --kv-mode q8 --pm4 --skip-prefill` shadow is exact for the 12B MQ4 and the 26B-A4B but **fails for the E4B Q8** (`exact=False`).
- **Gemma 4 defaults on gfx1151 and for the 26B-A4B change numerics; no KLD was measured.** On gfx1151, 256-row batched prefill and the GQA decode tile are the default: the 12B MQ4 greedy battery's top-16 logits differ from beta on all 5 prompts, and the prose prompt's text splits at step 76 (both continuations coherent). With `HIPFIRE_GEMMA4_PREFILL_BATCH=1 HIPFIRE_GFX1151_Q8_DECODE_ATTN_GQA_GEMMA=0` the battery equals beta, logits included. On every arch the 26B-A4B takes the 12B's fused qk-norm+RoPE and post-norm kernels and the reworked HFQ4-G128 routed down kernel; those fusions have **no kill switch**. On gfx1201, 26B-A4B (HFQ4-G128 fixture) greedy text matches beta on 5/5 chain turns and 4/5 battery prompts; the prose prompt diverges at character 257, coherent. 12B, E2B and E4B on gfx1100 and gfx1201 are byte-identical to beta, logits included.
